It now throws a `TableConfigurationException` at configuration time, and the reserved list has grown from Enter/Space to everything the grid claims: `Enter`, `Space`, `ArrowUp`, `ArrowDown`, `Home`, `End`, `PageUp`, `PageDown`, `ContextMenu`, `F10` and `?`. If an app shipped `->onKey()` on one of these, it was already dead code — rebind it to a free key. `Backspace` is deliberately *not* reserved: it acts as a platform alias of `Delete` in the client matcher, and an explicit `->onKey('Backspace')` stays valid. A `keyboardShortcut()` stamped on the action itself (serving a toolbar or palette) is still only skipped, never fatal, when the action is reused as a record action.

### Changed
- **TipTap upgraded from v2 to v3 across the wire-forms rich editor.** Three v3 breaking changes reached the pre-bundled editor. First, **StarterKit v3 now bundles Link and Underline**, so registering them again from their standalone packages tripped v3's duplicate-extension warning; the standalone `Link`/`Underline` imports are gone and Link is configured through `StarterKit.configure({ link: { openOnClick, HTMLAttributes } })` (Underline is on by default). Second, **`setContent`'s second argument is now an options object, not a boolean** — the old `setContent(content, false)` no longer means "don't emit an update": a bare `false` is not an object, so it falls through to the v3 default `emitUpdate: true` and a server-driven fill echoes straight back into Livewire, re-entering ProseMirror mid-transaction — exactly the feedback loop the `$wire.$watch` guard exists to prevent. It is now `setContent(content, { emitUpdate: false })`. Third, **`@tiptap/extension-table` dropped its default export** (Table is a named export in v3 alongside `TableKit`), so the addon chunk's `import Table from …` resolved to `undefined` and the tables editor booted without ever registering the extension; switched to the named `import { Table }`. The ESM code-split dist is rebuilt (core entry + opt-in addon entry sharing one ProseMirror core chunk, unchanged in shape). Covered server-side by `TiptapAssetTest` (bundle delivery + shared-chunk reference, version-agnostic) and end-to-end in a real browser by `workbench/scripts/verify-tiptap-split.mjs` (11/11): the core editor boots via its module script with the addon chunk absent, `toggleBold`/`toggleUnderline`/`setHeading` prove StarterKit v3 registered the marks, the tables page loads the addon chunk and `insertTable()` renders a `<table>`, and a console-warning guard asserts no duplicate-extension registration.
- **`wire-core` no longer depends on `wire-forms` — the action/modal form coupling is now inverted through a core-owned seam.** The package graph is `wire-sortable → wire-table → wire-forms → wire-core`, and `InteractsWithActions` documents that "wire-core must not depend on wire-forms," yet six sites in core imported and even *constructed* wire-forms' concrete `Form` / `FormConfig` (`HasModal`, `ActionHalt`, the `HasForm` contract, both `FormSaving`/`FormSavedPayload` plugin hooks) and the core `actions.modal-host` view called a bridge method (`getActionModalFormInstance()`) that only wire-forms defines. In a split-published, standalone `nyoncode/wire-core` this is not academic: the payloads and contracts reference undefined classes, and opening any action modal on a plain `WithActions` host **fatally** calls the missing bridge method. Core now owns the seam: a narrow `ModalForm` interface (`extends Htmlable`; `statePath`/`livewire`/`fill`/`getInitialState`/`validate`), a `ModalFormFactory` resolved from the container via `ModalForms`, and a `FormConfigContract` for the hook payloads. wire-forms' `Form` implements `ModalForm` (no method-body changes), a `FormModalFormFactory` is bound in `WireFormsServiceProvider`, and `FormConfig` implements the contract. When wire-forms is absent the factory is unbound and `ModalForms::make()` degrades to `null` (the modal renders form-less) instead of fatally naming a class it does not ship; two new null form-instance seams on core `InteractsWithActions` (overridden by the bridge via `insteadof`) keep the core modal-host view honest. The public `HasModal::form()` / `ActionHalt::form()` API is unchanged in practice — `Form implements ModalForm`, so `->form(Form::make()->schema([...]))`, `->form([...])`, and closures all still type-check; only the internal type surface moved off the concrete `Form`. Field objects passed to `->form([...])` remain wire-forms types, so this is compile/load-level decoupling (a standalone core boots and references only its own dependencies), not a claim that core is value-level form-free. Covered by `ModalFormsTest` (factory resolution + graceful degradation when unbound), a form-free-host seam test in `InteractsWithActionsTest`, and the existing form-behaviour suites, which now build through the factory bound in the core `TestCase`.

## [1.13.2]

### Fixed
- **An `ActionGroup::lazyMenu()` item did nothing when clicked — a Delete never opened its confirmation modal, an edit never ran.** A lazy menu ships no server-rendered item markup; each item is built client-side from a serialized spec and invoked in JS as `$wire[method](...args)`. But the dropdown captured `$wire` into Alpine reactive state (`this._wire = this.$wire`), and assigning the Livewire proxy into reactive state wraps it in a `@vue/reactivity` proxy that corrupts its methods: `$wire[method]` reads back as `undefined` and `$wire.call` collapses to the native `Function.prototype.call`. So the click guard `typeof this._wire[item.method] === 'function'` was always false and the action silently no-op'd — while the eager path (a real `wire:click="method(...)"` evaluated server-side) worked, hiding the regression. It bit every resolution, not just mobile, and was invisible to server-side tests because the failure lived entirely in the teleported panel's JS. The dropdown now stores only the host component's `wire:id` (a plain string, safe in reactive state) and resolves the live component at click time via `Livewire.find(id).call(item.method, ...args)` — the same server-directed invocation the eager `wire:click` performs. Verified end-to-end in a real browser (`workbench/scripts/verify-lazy-menu.mjs`, 8/8) across mobile and desktop: a lazy Delete opens its modal, a lazy View/Edit fires its table action, and closing the modal leaves no lingering overlay. Requires rebuilding the core JS bundle (`npm run build:core-assets`).
- **A standalone single-date `DateFilter` never filtered anything.** `->filters([DateFilter::make('issued_at')])` (no `->range()`, no `->month()`) submits a scalar value, so it fell between the two routes in `TableQueryService`: the apply() route only picks up filters with a `->query()` callback, month mode, or an array (the range shape), while the planner route asks `toPlannerDefinitions()` — which `DateFilter` returns empty for *every* mode, because a date-truncated `whereDate` comparison cannot be expressed as a plain column/operator/value definition. The filter was therefore neither applied nor planned; the panel rendered, the indicator chip appeared, and every row survived. It hid because `bypassesPlanner()` was scoped to month mode only, and `DateFilterTest` asserted that scoping instead of exercising the single-date value through the real query pipeline. `DateFilter::bypassesPlanner()` now returns `true` in every mode — matching its own contract that all modes route through `apply()`, the same pattern `TernaryFilter` already follows — so single-date filters apply their `whereDate` constraint. Range and month were already applied (as an array and via month-mode bypass respectively) and are unchanged. Covered by a new single-date row in `FilterPipelineMatrixTest`, which drives each filter type through `TableQueryService` and asserts the surviving rows.
- **An action's `slideOverOnMobile()` / `fullScreenOnMobile()` was silently ignored on a confirmation action.** The mobile presentation flags were meant to be independent of the modal type — "a normal dialog on desktop, a bottom-sheet (or full-screen) on a phone" — and they worked for a form or infolist modal. But a confirmation action carries no form/infolist, and `doesRequireConfirmation()` returns true whenever the action has a modal but none of those (`slideOverOnMobile()` itself sets `hasModal`), so the blade routed it to the `Confirmation` render object — which had no `slideOverOnMobile` / `fullScreenOnMobile` / `breakpoint` at all. The flag was dropped: a `->requiresConfirmation()->slideOverOnMobile()` (or a bare `->slideOverOnMobile()`) action rendered a centered dialog on a phone, never a sheet. The asymmetry was that `slideOver()` *routes* (the blade tests it first, so it always reaches the slide-over shell) while `slideOverOnMobile()` was only ever a parameter of the `Modal` branch that a confirmation never enters. `Confirmation` (and its `ConfirmationStyle`) now accept the same three flags and **delegate the mobile-sheet variant to the canonical `ModalStyle` owner** instead of re-encoding it, so a confirmation honours `slideOverOnMobile()` / `fullScreenOnMobile()` / `mobileBreakpoint()` exactly like a form modal — the default centered dialog is byte-for-byte unchanged. Both action-modal render paths (the table partial and the core `WithActions` modal-host) pass the flags through. Covered by `ConfirmationStyleTest` (variant class delegation), `ConfirmationObjectTest` (the shell renders the sheet), and a confirmation-action row in the table's `ActionModalMobileVariantTest`.
- **The core `WithActions` modal-host dropped `mobileBreakpoint()` on its slide-over and plain-modal surfaces.** The same class of bug as the confirmation one: the table's action-modal partial passed `mobileBreakpoint` into every rendered surface, but the consumer-facing `wire-core::actions.modal-host` blade (the host an app includes in its own `WithActions` component) only passed it on the confirmation branch — the slide-over and `Modal` branches omitted it. A `->mobileBreakpoint('md')` on a slide-over or form action in a non-table host was therefore ignored and the sheet still switched at the default `sm`. Both branches now pass `breakpoint` through, matching the table path. Covered by a render row in `InteractsWithActionsTest` that drives the core modal-host and asserts the `md`-gated switch.
- **A `SlideOver` config object's `position('left')` was dropped when attached to an action.** `->modal(SlideOver::make()->position('left'))` translated the config into the action's modal state but never carried the panel edge, and neither action-modal blade passed a `position` to the rendered `SlideOver` — so an action slide-over always opened on the right regardless of the config. `HasModal` now stores the position (default `'right'`), `->modal()` maps it from `SlideOver::getPosition()`, `getModalConfig()` exposes it, and both blades pass it into the rendered panel. Covered by `HasModalTest` (config mapping) plus left-pinned render rows in `InteractsWithActionsTest` (core host) and `ActionModalMobileVariantTest` (table).

## [1.13.0]

### Added
- **Excel-style fill handle for editable tables.** `Table::fillHandle()` opts a table in; hovering or focusing a fillable cell parks a small handle just inside its bottom-right corner, and dragging it down writes that value over the rows below. Nothing is written and **no request is sent while dragging** — the drag only paints a preview. On release the whole range goes out as **one** request (`fillTableCells`). `Column::fillable(false)` excludes a column that is otherwise editable (a unique code, an invoice number); `Table::fillMaxRecords(int)` caps a single request (default 500). Mouse, touch and pen share one Pointer Events path, dragging past the viewport edge auto-scrolls, and Escape abandons the drag without writing. See `docs/table/columns/fill-handle.md`.
- **A fill is one request but not one `UPDATE`, and deliberately not all-or-nothing.** Every record still goes through the same path as a single inline edit — its own `canEdit()`, its own rules, its own optimistic-lock version — so one row losing its race (or being disabled for this user) is reported as a per-record failure while the rest land, and the client rolls back only the cells that were refused. A single `UPDATE … WHERE key IN (…)` was rejected as the implementation: it skips Eloquent events, casts and mutators, never touches `updated_at` (which is what the optimistic lock compares), and cannot express a column persisted through `editableUsing()`, a relation or a pivot. Records are resolved through the table's own query, so a key outside it is never written, and the endpoint refuses outright unless `fillHandle()` is on. Per-cell `CellUpdating`/`CellUpdated` fire exactly as for a single edit — there is no separate bulk event.
- **Known limit — the optimistic lock has one-second resolution.** A version is `updated_at` as a Unix timestamp, so two writes inside the same second are indistinguishable and a stale version is not caught there. It matters for a client that writes the same rows repeatedly: the fill controller therefore serialises its requests and reads each row's version at send time, rather than firing a second fill with the versions the page held before the first one answered. Left as-is rather than moving to sub-second stamps, which would change `RecordVersion` for every surface (including editable panels) and assumes a timestamp precision many schemas do not have. Covered by `FillHandleTest`.

- **"Select all matching" — a selection that can be larger than the page, or than memory.** Selection now has two shapes: an explicit key set (as before), or a mode meaning *everything the current filter matches*, in which `selection.records` holds the **exclusions** instead. Unticking one row out of 128 000 stores one entry, not 127 999, and no key list of the whole result set ever reaches the browser. The bulk bar always shows which shape is active and offers the way across ("3 selected. **Select all 1 284**" ⇄ "**Only this page**"). New on the component: `selectAllMatchingRecords()`, `selectsAllMatching()`, `selectOnlyPageRecords()`, `deselectPageRecords()`, `selectedRecordsQuery()`, `eachSelectedRecord()`, `getMatchingRecordsCount()`.
- **A filter or search change drops "all matching" back to an explicit selection.** "Everything" is defined by the filter that was on screen; narrowing it while that selection stands would silently redefine what the next bulk action touches — which is how a bulk delete reaches records nobody saw. Sorting and paging leave it alone, since neither changes the set.
- **Bulk actions are capped instead of dying.** An action callback takes a `Collection`, so `Table::bulkMaxRecords()` (default 1 000) bounds what one action may materialise; past it the action refuses with a notification naming the count and the cap. `eachSelectedRecord(callback, chunk: 500)` walks the selection through the query for actions that must handle any size, and `selectedRecordsQuery()` hands back the same selection as a builder. `bulkMaxRecords(null)` lifts the cap.
- **Active-filter chips scroll sideways on a phone instead of stacking into rows.** The chip row wrapped, pushing the table down by a line for every filter; below `sm` it is now a horizontal scroller that fades at the trailing edge, with the reset link pinned outside it so it never scrolls out of reach. It wraps again once there is width to wrap into.
- **Totals reach the card view.** The summary footer is a `<tfoot>` inside the table the stacked layout hides, so a phone showed no totals, no sub-row grand totals and no scope toggle — in an accounting table, the number the user opened it for. They now render below the cards as label/value rows sharing the right edge with each card's metric, with the same *All / This page / Selection* toggle. Nothing to configure: a column with `summarize*()` gets its total here as well.
- **The card view has a select-all, and sorting.** Both used to live only in the table header row — which the stacked layout hides — so on a phone selecting a page meant tapping every card, and sorting was impossible. A tri-state select-all strip now sits above the cards (the same `allSelected`/`someSelected` state the desktop header checkbox uses), and a sort control renders in the toolbar below the stacking breakpoint, naming the active sort on its trigger and opening as a bottom sheet. Neither is behind a gesture: what is not visible is not found.
- **The stacked mobile card reads as a record instead of a column dump.** It used to be the column order in disguise — first column as the title, every other column stamped into a two-column label/value grid, so reordering columns changed what the phone emphasised. Five named slots now carry the hierarchy (`title`, `subtitle`, `metric`, `meta`, and the detail grid for the rest), with the metric set right on the title line in tabular figures so a column of amounts can be compared down the edge. **No configuration is required**: `MobileCard` derives the slots (title = first column, metric = last right-aligned column — what `money()`/`numeric()` produce, meta = badge columns), so existing tables improve without a change. Override per column with `mobileTitle()` / `mobileSubtitle()` / `mobileMetric()` / `mobileMeta()` / `mobileDetail()`, or for the whole table with `Table::mobileCard(fn (MobileCardConfig $card) => …)`, which wins over both.
- **Sub-rows finally render on a phone with everything the desktop panel has.** The card used to flatten every child of every parent into one `<dl>` — three items across two columns arrived as six label/value pairs with nothing marking where one item ended. Children are now a list: name on the left, its figure on the same right edge as the card's own metric, supporting detail underneath. Per-parent subtotals, the "Show N more" affordance and per-child actions all work there for the first time. Child actions always collapse into a single `⋮` trigger regardless of `collapseActionsOnMobile()` — a child line is narrower than the card holding it, and two labelled buttons crush the item name to an ellipsis at any phone width. The collapsed toggle names the child count (`3 items`) when the number is already in memory and falls back to `Details` when it is not, since a collapsed row has no eager-loaded children and counting would cost one query per card; `->withCount('items')` on the base query lights it up for free.
- **Sub-row expansion is one control in the expander column header, not three buttons in the toolbar.** The header cell above the row chevrons now holds a master chevron that opens or closes every row; ⌥/Alt-clicking any row chevron does the same (the spreadsheet shortcut), and **Expand on every row** joins the column list in the view menu — which is the only bulk control a phone gets, since the stacked card layout has no header row to hang a chevron on. The `Expand all | Collapse all | Show all` toolbar (`partials/sub-rows-toolbar.blade.php`) is gone. The view menu now also opens for a table that has sub-rows but no toggleable columns, and its section heading is finally translated instead of a hardcoded Czech string.
- **Expansion state was two flags that fought each other; it is now a baseline plus exceptions.** `rows.flattenMode` and `subRowsDefaultExpanded()` both meant "everything is open" by different routes, so with flatten mode on, **Collapse all did nothing** — the template asked `isRowExpanded($key) || $flattenMode` and the second half won. State is now `rows.expandAll` (the baseline: `null` follows `subRowsDefaultExpanded()`, `true`/`false` is the user's own choice) with `rows.expanded` listing only the rows that differ from it. New: `toggleAllRowExpansion()`, `setSubRowsExpandAll(bool)`, `expandsSubRowsByDefault()`. `toggleFlattenMode()` and `flattenSubRows()`/`isFlattenSubRows()` remain as deprecated aliases, and the legacy `$flattenMode` property now points at `rows.expandAll`.
- **The expansion baseline is remembered per user.** Tables that opted into `rememberColumns()` now store it alongside the hidden-column set — one payload for the two "how I look at this table" settings. `loadColumnPreferences()`/`persistColumnPreferences()` are renamed `loadViewPreferences()`/`persistViewPreferences()`; `rememberColumns()` itself is unchanged.

### Fixed
- **A misspelled sub-row relation threw a cryptic `BadMethodCallException`.** `subRows('itemz')` surfaced as `Call to undefined method Invoice::itemz()`, which never mentioned `subRows()`. It now throws a `TableConfigurationException` naming the relation, the model and the likely cause; the check uses `Model::isRelation()`, so a relation registered via `resolveRelationUsing()` is still accepted.
- **A crafted request could sort the sub-rows of a table whose headers were not clickable.** `subRowsSortable(false, default: 'x')` configures a default sort without interactive headers, but `sortSubRows()` shared the whitelist guard `isSubRowColumnSortable()` — which must stay lenient toward the default column so the query can apply that default sort. A hand-posted `sortSubRows('x')` therefore rode that leniency and flipped the direction. `sortSubRows()` now additionally requires the table to be interactively sortable, while the query's own default sort is unaffected.
- **The interactive sub-row filter bar (`subRowsFilterable()`) filtered the parent table, or nothing at all.** Each control rendered through `Column::renderFilter()`, whose inline partials bind hardcoded to `tableState.columnFilters.<name>` — so a value typed under an expanded row was written to the *parent* table's column filters. When a sub-row column shared a name with a parent column it silently filtered the parents; otherwise it went to a key nothing read and did nothing, and the "✕ Reset" affordance never appeared. It survived because every server-side test set `rows.subRowFilters` by hand — exactly the state the broken control never produced. `renderFilter()` now takes a state path, the bar binds to `tableState.rows.subRowFilters.<name>`, filterable sub-row columns are seeded at mount (a select's `entangle` no-ops on an undefined path, the same gotcha the column filters already guard), and `resetSubRowFilters()` clears each slot instead of dropping the keys. Verified end-to-end in a browser (`workbench/scripts/verify-subrow-filter.mjs`): typing narrows the children 3→1, the parents stay put, and reset restores them. **Known gap left as-is:** the stacked mobile card has no sub-row filter bar (it never did); the fix is to the table-view bar, which is where the binding was wrong.
- **One `Event::listen('*', …)` anywhere in the app silently disabled the entire audit log.** `AuditEventSubscriber::subscribe()` guarded its own registration with `$events->hasListeners(AuditableEvent::class)` to stay idempotent for apps that kept a manual `Event::subscribe()`. But Laravel's `hasListeners()` also answers true for **wildcard** listeners matching the name (`hasWildcardListeners()` runs `Str::is($pattern, $eventName)` over every registered pattern) — so a single catch-all listener from Telescope's event watcher, Pulse, debugbar, or an app's own event log made the guard swallow the real registration. Nothing threw and nothing was logged: `HasAuditable` models fired `RecordCreated`/`RecordUpdated`/`RecordDeleted` into a void, `app(AuditLogger::class)->log()` still worked when called directly, and `Event::getListeners(AuditableEvent::class)` even *showed* listeners — the wildcard ones, which merge into that result — making the pipeline look wired while no row was ever written. Whether it bit depended on provider boot order, so the same code audited fine in one app and not in the next. The guard now inspects `getRawListeners()` for *this* subscriber's own `handleAuditableEvent` (as a class-string tuple, an object tuple, a `Class@method` string, or a subclass), which is what "already subscribed" actually means; wildcard and unrelated listeners no longer suppress it. Covered by `AuditPipelineTest` (a wildcard registered before the subscriber still audits, and still audits exactly once) and `AuditEventSubscriberTest`.
- **`AuditEntry::byUser()` was fatal for a UUID/ULID actor.** The scope type-hinted `int $userId` while the `user_id` column is a string and `AuditLogger::resolveUserId()` deliberately returns `int|string|null` for exactly that case — so the documented `AuditEntry::byUser($user->id)` threw a `TypeError` under `strict_types` on any app with non-integer user keys. Widened to `int|string`.
- **Switching the summary scope to "This page" was fatal on any paginated table.** `computeTableSummaries('page')` passed `getTableRecords()` straight to `Column::computeSummaries()`, which takes a `Collection` — but a paginated table hands back a paginator, so the toggle threw a `TypeError` on desktop and mobile alike. It survived unnoticed because summary coverage runs on unpaginated tables. The page scope is now unwrapped before it is summed. Found while giving the card view its own totals.
- **Selecting a page discarded the selection made on every other page.** `toggleAll()` and `selectAllRecords()` both *replaced* the set with the current page's keys, so selecting page one, paging, and selecting page two silently threw the first page away. Both now union, and `deselectPageRecords()` removes only the current page.
- **Labelled row actions crushed the card's identity to nothing.** On a stacked card the action buttons shared the title line; two labelled buttons take the width the title needs and `min-w-0` then collapses the record name and subtitle to zero — the card rendered as two buttons and no name. Labelled actions now render on their own row below the card; only a `collapseActionsOnMobile()` group — one icon wide — stays beside the title.
- **`setPage()` left the record memo pointing at the previous page.** Paging normally arrives as a fresh request where the memo is empty, but a programmatic `setPage()` within one request (select this page → page → select again) then read the old rows.
- **"Expand all" cost one query per open row and could show unfiltered children.** `expandAllRows()` resolved the page *before* writing the expansion state, so `eagerLoadSubRows()` had already run against the old (empty) expanded set and the record cache was consumed — every open parent then fell back to its own query. Worse, when the caller's own query eager-loaded the relation (`Invoice::with('items')`), `getSubRows()` took the in-memory fast path over a set that no `Filter::subRows()` scoping had ever touched, so a filtered table listed children the filter excluded. Expand/collapse-all now move the baseline and read no records at all, which also makes them O(1) and effective on pages the user has not visited — the old version only ever expanded the current page.
- **A `TernaryFilter` with a `->query()` callback returned the same rows for "Yes" and "No".** The callback was handed the raw select state, and the select submits the option *keys* `'true'` / `'false'` — both non-empty strings, so the natural `$value ? $q->whereNotNull(…) : $q->whereNull(…)` took the true branch either way. It failed silently: the table rendered, the indicator chip appeared, only the rows were wrong (and inverted for one of the two options). The value is now normalized to a real `bool` by `TernaryFilter::normalizeValue()` before the callback runs — as is `'1'`/`'0'`, `1`/`0` and a real bool arriving from a URL or a `->default()`. Anything else means "All": the filter is inactive and the callback is not called at all. `normalizeValue()` is a new hook on the base `Filter` (identity by default) for any filter whose UI state is a transport stand-in for another type.
- **The filter-panel path never reached `Filter::apply()`.** `TableQueryService` collected `getQueryCallback()` and invoked the closure itself, so any normalization or guard living in a filter's `apply()` was bypassed for panel filters — which is why the ternary bug hit the panel while the column header path (`Column::applyFilter()` → `apply()`) shared the same code. Custom callbacks now route through the filter's own `apply()`, which is the single owner of value normalization and callback invocation.
- **`TernaryFilter::getFormFields()` declared different option keys than the filter renders.** It offered `'1'`/`'0'` while `getOptions()` (and the rendered ternary view) uses `'true'`/`'false'`, so the two surfaces disagreed about what "false" looks like — and it is where the docs' incorrect `$value === '1'` recipe came from. Both now use `getOptions()`.
- **A table with `cacheQuery()` froze: changing the page size did nothing until you happened to change page.** `executeWithCache()` used the caller-supplied `cacheQuery($ttl, $key)` verbatim and appended only the page number, so a custom key carried none of the state that shapes the slice it caches — the per-page select, the sort, the search term and the filters all wrote to the state container and then read back the entry cached under the same key, for the whole TTL. Changing page finally shifted the key and the new page size appeared, which is exactly what made it look like a UI lag rather than a stale read. Cache keys are now built by one canonical owner, `Services\TableQueryCacheKey`: a **namespace** (the query's SQL and bindings, or the custom key, or an overridden `generateQueryCacheKey()`) plus a **state fingerprint** covering search, filters, column filters, sort, per-page and page — the fingerprint is appended to every namespace, so a custom key scopes entries without being able to collapse two views into one. The fingerprint normalises key order and numeric strings, so the `"25"` the select posts and the `25` the mount set share one entry instead of doubling the cache.
- **An out-of-range page rendered as an empty table instead of re-anchoring.** The clamp existed but ran only from `afterActionExecuted()`, so it caught a delete through a row action and nothing else: a shared or bookmarked `?page=5`, a filter that shrank the result set, or rows removed by another user between two polls all left the user parked on a page past the end with "no records" and no indication why. The rule now lives in `getTableRecords()` — every fetch that produces a `LengthAwarePaginator` past its last page re-anchors to the last populated page and re-queries once — so it holds for every route into that state. `clampPageToBounds()` stays as the explicit post-mutation hook. Simple and cursor pagination have no total to clamp against and are deliberately untouched.
- **A `wire:poll` tick could swallow the render a per-page/sort/filter change needed.** Livewire pools commits fired in the same tick, so `refreshTable()` could share a request with the user's change; `pollChangeDetection()` then compared checksums, found the *data* unchanged, and `skipRender()`ed away the render that change was made for — leaving it invisible until the next roundtrip. The poll now never skips a render in a request that also wrote result-shaping state.
- **The per-page select could display a page size the table was not using.** `perPageOptions()` returned the configured options untouched, so `->perPage(3)` against the default `[10, 25, 50, 100]` rendered a control claiming "10" above three rows — and picking "10" fired no change event, because the control already claimed to be on it. `getPerPageOptions()` now always offers the configured `perPage()`, and the option matching the live state is marked `selected` server-side rather than relying on the first paint landing on the right one.

### Changed
- **Behavioral fix: a `TernaryFilter` `->query()` callback now receives a `bool` instead of the raw select state.** Code that compares against a string (`$value === '1'`, `$value === 'true'`) stops matching and must branch on the bool — `$value === '1'` was never correct anyway, since the select submits `'true'`/`'false'`, so such a callback is already returning the wrong rows today. A callback that coerced defensively (`filter_var($value, FILTER_VALIDATE_BOOLEAN)`) keeps working unchanged; the coercion is simply redundant now. Every `->query()` callback also receives the raw submitted state as an optional third argument, for the rare case that needs the transport form.
- **`cacheQuery()` entries written before this release are orphaned by the new key format.** Nothing to do — the first load of each cached table after deploying is a cold read, then the cache warms as before. A `Cache::forget($yourCustomKey)` never busted a table's entries anyway (the old key already appended a `:page:` suffix) and still does not; clear the store or wait out the TTL.

### Security
- **The per-page value from the client was written to state unvalidated.** `tableState.pagination.perPage` is bound with `wire:model.live`, and nothing checked the incoming value — a crafted Livewire payload could ask for `perPage: 500000` and get the whole table read into one page. It is now coerced to an int and checked against the offered options (the URL-seeding path already did this), falling back to the configured `perPage()` otherwise. The same normalisation removes the int/string drift that previously reached the cache key and the query-string `except` comparison.

## [1.12.1]

### Security
- **Stored XSS in `ButtonColumn`'s `wire:click` attribute.** `getWireClick()` hand-builds the whole `wire:click` attribute and the cell view emits it raw (`{!! !!}`); the record-derived confirmation text was protected only with `addslashes()`, which is JS-string escaping, not HTML-attribute escaping. A record name (or a string primary key) containing `">` broke out of the attribute into element context and executed in the viewer's session. Every interpolated value is now escaped for the HTML attribute (`e()`) on top of the JS-string `addslashes()`, and the record key / column / action identifiers are escaped too. Covered by a `ColumnPartialRenderTest` regression that feeds a breakout payload through the cell.
- **`WithTable::importTable()` ran without checking the import action's authorization.** It is a public Livewire endpoint, so a client could invoke it directly — bypassing an `->authorize()` / `->hidden()` guard on the `ImportAction` and feeding an arbitrary server-readable path to the CSV importer. It now resolves the `ImportAction` and refuses (an empty `ImportResult`) when its `canExecute()` denies.

### Fixed
- **Record-aware `visible()` / `hidden()` / `disabled()` closures crashed an action rendered as a direct row button.** `button.blade.php` checked `isHidden()` without the row record, and `visible()` wrapped the closure in a variadic that masked its arity, so a `->visible(fn ($record) => …)` action fataled with `ArgumentCountError` whenever it was resolved without a record. `isHidden()`/`isDisabled()` now route through the same reflection guard as the dynamic label/color resolvers (degrading to the static default instead of erroring), `visible()` tracks inversion separately so the closure's arity survives, and the button view passes the row record. `ActionGroup` was unaffected (its dropdown always passed the record).
- **`Filter::visible(Closure)` silently discarded the condition.** `visible()` delegated to `hidden(! $visible)`; for a `Closure`, `! $closure` coerces the object to `false`, so the visibility condition was dropped and the filter always showed. `visible(Closure)` now stores the closure and inverts on read, a literal `hidden(bool)` clears any stale closure (last call wins), and `isHidden()` arity-guards a record-required closure.
- **`array`/`json`-cast columns were double-JSON-encoded on save.** The dehydrator pre-encoded the array to a JSON string before `Model::setAttribute()`, whose cast then encoded it again — persisting a double-encoded column that read back as a string (`KeyValue`, `CheckboxList`, `Tags`, multiple `FileUpload`, any array-cast field). The `array`/`json` arms now pass the value through and let the Eloquent cast encode it once, mirroring the `datetime` arm.
- **Editable panels bypassed the optimistic lock on a custom `updated_at` column.** `EditableEntry::getRecordVersion()` read the literal `updated_at` attribute, so a model naming its timestamp column differently (`const UPDATED_AT`) returned the `'0'` sentinel and was left unguarded on the load→first-edit window; it now delegates to the canonical `RecordVersion` owner, which resolves the column via `getUpdatedAtColumn()`.
- **`after()` callbacks ran when the action halted.** `AfterCallbacksStage` fired the `after()` hooks unconditionally, so an action that returned `$halt()` (e.g. to raise a confirmation modal) ran its side effects before the user confirmed and again when the action re-ran on confirm. The stage now short-circuits on a halted result, matching the before-hook behaviour; an `after()` hook that itself initiates a halt is unaffected.
- **`Radio` per-option colors/icons were misaligned for int-backed enums.** `getColors()`/`getIcons()` merged the enum-derived map with `array_merge`, which renumbers integer keys from 0, so an int-backed `HasColor`/`HasIcon` enum's map keys no longer matched the option values — every option rendered the wrong color/icon. It now uses `array_replace`, preserving the int keys while explicit `->colors()`/`->icons()` still win.
- **Summaries threw an ambiguous-column error over a relation-joined query.** Query-scope summaries wrapped the column unqualified; sorting by a relation column adds a `LEFT JOIN`, and a bare column present on both tables (`id`, `amount`) made the `SUM`/`COUNT` ambiguous — taking down the whole table render, not just the footer. Plain columns are now qualified to the base table in both `SummaryBatch` and `SummaryCalculator`; rollup columns aggregate a subquery alias over a derived table and stay unqualified.
- **CSV and Excel exports threw an ambiguous-`id` error over a relation-joined query.** The exporters iterate with `chunkById()`, whose default cursor is the unqualified `id`; a relation-sorted export query carries a `LEFT JOIN` where `id` is ambiguous. Both exporters now pass the qualified key to `chunkById()` (PDF was unaffected — it uses `->get()`).
- **`groupBy` on a `date`/object-cast column split every row into its own group.** Boundary detection and subtotal partitioning compared the raw group value with strict `===`; a date-cast column returns a fresh `Carbon` per record, so equal dates were distinct objects. A new `HasGrouping::getGroupComparisonKey()` normalises the value to a stable scalar (enum scalar, formatted date, scalar as-is) for both the boundary check and the partition matching, while the raw value still drives the header label.
- **Form save pipeline — four defects around relationship-shaped fields.** `MorphToSelect` fataled every save (its own name is a morph relation, not a column) and its `{name}_type`/`{name}_id` sub-fields carried no validation rule and were dropped — the phantom key is now replaced with the two real columns read from raw state. An unruled repeater **child** field was silently dropped by `validate()` (only ruled children got a wildcard rule, unlike top-level fields which fall back to `['nullable']`) — children now get the same fallback. A repeater child `DehydratesState` field (`FileUpload`, `DateTimePicker`) never dehydrated (`dehydrateFields()` matched only top-level keys) so a nested file was never stored and a nested date kept its raw value — they are now dehydrated per item. A relationship-bound `Tags` field fataled the same way `MorphToSelect` did and is stripped from the parent write.
- **A relationship repeater recreated every child on update.** The child's primary key is not a schema field, so `validate()` stripped it before the data reached `RelationshipSaveHandler`, which then matched no existing row and deleted-and-recreated every child — losing its identity and any column not in the form. The key is now restored from raw state before the relationship cascade.
- **Default searchable columns were dropped when any column used a custom search callback.** `TableQueryService` nulled the search term for the planner whenever a `->searchUsing(...)` column existed, so every plain `->searchable()` column was excluded and only the custom callbacks ran. The custom callbacks are now threaded into `ApplySearch` and OR-combined with the default-column clauses in the same search group.
- **A standalone `TextFilter` did an exact match instead of its operator.** `buildPlannerFilters()` built a generic `=`/`in` definition for every planner filter, ignoring the filter's own `toPlannerDefinitions()` — so `->filters([TextFilter::make('name')])` planned `name = 'term'` instead of `name LIKE %term%`. The standalone path now delegates to `toPlannerDefinitions()` like the column-header path already did.
- **A recordless wizard rendered an empty first step.** `ModalStep::getSchema()`/`getValidation()` evaluated their closure only when `$context` was truthy; a header-action wizard seeds its first step from an empty data bag, and `&& $context` treated `[]` as falsy, so the closure was skipped and the first step rendered no fields. They now evaluate for any non-null context (including `[]`); a literal `null` (the wizard chrome enumerating steps) still falls back to the base schema.
- **A wizard's last step never ran its `afterValidation()` hook.** The final step is never stepped off of, and the submit path re-validated every step with `runAfterValidation: false` to avoid double-firing — so a final-step async/uniqueness gate was bypassed. It now runs `afterValidation` for the last step on submit while still skipping the earlier steps.

### Added
- **Seam and whole-system test coverage for the recurring bug classes.** The fixes above were seams (view↔model, `validate()`↔persist, planner↔custom-callback) or one variant of a polymorphic input, which single-component unit tests kept missing. New pipeline / equivalence-class matrices exercise a whole surface at once — the cast round-trip (every cast type through the full save → DB read-back), the filter pipeline (every filter type, ternary three-state, empty-value no-op, filter over a JOIN), the summary aggregate types over a JOIN, and the wizard step-context matrix. On top of them, whole-system end-to-end tests drive the full stack through the real Livewire lifecycle: a rendered table (columns, relation, grouping, summary, row action) with search/filter/sort, the form hydrate→edit→persist→remount→re-hydrate loop, a reorderable table, inline cell edits with optimistic-lock conflict, and `exportTable()` reflecting the component's active filter/search. The Integration `TestCase` now boots the table and sortable providers too, so these run against the whole stack — and on the MySQL/PostgreSQL matrix, not just SQLite.

## [1.12.0]

### Added
- **Relation columns resolve by mechanism instead of forcing one JOIN for everything.** Dot-notation relation columns (`TextColumn::make('company.name')`) now always eager-load the displayed relation with `with()` — every relation type, including nested chains like `company.country.name` — so a shown relation value never triggers an N+1 and never depends on a join, while **sorting** a singular relation (`belongsTo`/`hasOne`/`hasOneThrough`) still uses a real `LEFT JOIN` and aggregate filters compile to a `whereHas` count rather than a fragile `HAVING`. Benchmarked against the previous join-everything path (`RelationSortBenchmarkTest`) and documented in the `wire-table` guideline.
- **Render engine: cells and view components render Htmlable-first with per-request memoisation.** A canonical `Foundation\Concerns\HasViewRenderCache` caches a component's rendered HTML per request, record-invariant primitive markup (spinner, success check) is owned once by `Foundation\View\Primitives` (singleton, string-memoised), the floating-dropdown asset URL is owned by `Foundation\View\FloatingAssets`, and icons render through a plain `icon()` helper (`{!! icon(...) !!}`) instead of a `<x-wire::icon>` component per cell — cutting the per-row view-render count on wide tables. Enforced by a render-count fuse (`TableRenderCountTest`, `TablePrimitiveRenderTest`) and codified in `AI_CODING_STANDARD.md#Rendering`.
- **Modals render as Htmlable objects (Rule 5).** Confirmation, modal, and slide-over surfaces are constructed as first-class Htmlable value objects with a shared style value object, so a host embeds `{{ $modal }}` rather than a `<x-wire-modals::*>` tag — one canonical render path across the framework, covered by object/style/render tests.
- **`Table::collapseActionsOnMobile()` — the mobile stacked card folds its row actions into one dropdown once a row has enough of them.** `stackedOnMobile()` renders each row as a card and laid every row action out inline in the card header, which crowds the header as soon as a row carries three or four buttons. Opting in collapses them into a single "⋮" trigger — but only from a **threshold** of actions (default **3**), so a one- or two-action row keeps its buttons inline where the extra tap of a dropdown would just be friction: `->stackedOnMobile()->collapseActionsOnMobile()`, or `->collapseActionsOnMobile(threshold: 2)` to fold sooner (the threshold is clamped to at least 1, i.e. "always collapse"). It reuses the canonical `ActionGroup` rather than inventing a second dropdown: a new `Table::getMobileActionGroup()` wraps the row actions in one group, flattening any nested `ActionGroup`s and dropping dividers so everything lands under a single trigger, and inheriting the table's `sheetOnMobile()` / `mobileBreakpoint()` so the menu presents as a bottom sheet on small screens by default. The threshold counts the same flattened, divider-free set the dropdown would actually contain (`shouldCollapseActionsOnMobile()`), and because `ActionGroup` already collapses to a lone inline button when only one action is visible, a row whose per-record guards hide all but one action still shows that action directly rather than a one-item menu. **Desktop is untouched** — the full table keeps its inline action buttons; only the stacked cards collapse. Covered by unit tests (threshold on/off, custom + clamped threshold, flattened non-divider counting, disabled-outright) and a `CollapseMobileActionsTest` feature test rendering the card end to end, and **browser-verified** at a 390px mobile viewport via the new `workbench/scripts/verify-collapse-mobile-actions.mjs` CDP driver (7/7): four cards each show one group trigger with no inline action buttons, and opening it reveals a bottom-sheet menu with all three actions. Documented in [`docs/table/advanced.md`](docs/table/advanced.md) / [`docs/table/overview.md`](docs/table/overview.md) (EN + CS).

### Changed
- **`describe-component-api` (wire-boost) returns a purpose for every fluent method, not a bare signature.** The MCP tool reflects a component type's public builder API and reads each method's one-line docblock as its `summary`, but most fluent setters carried no docblock — so an agent asking about a column or field got `color(string $color)` with no hint of *what* it does or which vocabulary it takes, the part it actually has to guess. Every public **fluent (chainable) setter** across the built-in `TypeCatalog` — 767 methods spanning core (schema layouts, widgets, infolist/panel entries, actions, `HasModal`), forms (all 24 field/display components), and table (columns + filters) — now carries a one-line `/** … */` summary, documented once at the declaring trait/base so every subtype inherits it (documenting `HasLabel::label()` covers every column, field, and entry at once). The summaries are surfaced live through reflection, so nothing in the shipped guidelines/skills or the docs corpus changed. This is codified as a contributor convention in [`AI_CODING_STANDARD.md#Fluent API`](AI_CODING_STANDARD.md) and enforced by a new `FluentApiDocumentationTest` guard that walks the catalog through the same `ComponentReflector` the tool uses and fails — naming the exact `Class::method` — if any chainable setter is added later without a summary (negative-control checked: removing one summary fails the guard).
- **Row actions render through one canonical `wire-core` owner — `core` no longer reaches up into `wire-table`.** `Action` and `ActionGroup` live in `wire-core`, yet their render path pointed straight at `wire-table` views (`Action::render()` → `wire-table::tables.actions.action`, `renderForDropdown()` → `…dropdown-item`, `ActionGroup::render()` → `…action-group`) and, worse, `Action::getRenderData()` hardcoded the table's own Livewire method names (`executeTableAction` / `openActionModal`) into the shared class — an upside-down dependency against the `wire-table → wire-forms → wire-core` graph that left any other host (a standalone `WithActions` form) rendering table-shaped markup it could not wire. The click contract is now injected, not assumed: a new **`Actions\Contracts\ResolvesActionClick`** maps an action + record to the bare `wire:click` expression, with **`MountActionClickResolver`** (core default → `mountAction($name)`) and **`TableActionClickResolver`** (the single place that still knows `executeTableAction` / `openActionModal`) as the two implementations, threaded through `render()` / `renderForDropdown()` / `ActionGroup::render()` / `getDropdownItemsHtml()` as an optional argument (absent → the mount default, so standalone hosts are unchanged). `core` now imports nothing from `table`.

- **The two parallel action-button renderers collapsed into one.** A row action was rendered by `wire-table`'s `action.blade.php` + its own `button-content` partial, while a standalone action went through `wire-core`'s `button.blade.php` + a near-identical `button-content` (the two differed only by defensive `?? ''`), and the core view branched on `method_exists()` — the anti-pattern the coding standard calls out. A new **`Actions\Contracts\RendersAsButton`** (`toButtonRenderArray(?Model, ?ResolvesActionClick)`) lets the view consume a resolved array instead of probing the object, and the four duplicated `wire-table` views (`action`, `dropdown-item`, `action-group`, `partials/button-content`) are deleted in favour of the single core owner. The hand-rolled `<svg>` spinner from the old table view now routes through the canonical `Foundation\View\Primitives::spinner()` (resolved once per request, wrapped in a per-record `wire:loading` span so the string stays cacheable), and the button base-class string — previously written out in three places — is now one `BaseAction::BUTTON_BASE_CLASSES` constant consumed by both the per-record assembly and the record-less fallback.

- **Record-invariant button fragments are memoised across rows.** `toButtonRenderArray()` resolves the button classes and icon SVG once per action and reuses them for every row, recomputing per record only when `color` / `size` / `icon` is a per-record closure — so a table of static-styled actions stops rebuilding identical class strings and re-rendering the same icon on every row. The render-count fuse (`TableRenderCountTest`, `TablePrimitiveRenderTest`, including its row-action spinner path) stays green, confirming the unification adds no per-row view render.

- **Row actions wrap instead of overflowing on a narrow viewport.** The actions cell laid its buttons out in a `flex` with no wrapping, so a row with several actions overflowed on a constrained screen; the desktop and mobile-card action containers now `flex-wrap`, a CSS-only change that defers and duplicates no markup (the opt-in `ActionGroup::lazyMenu()` / `Table::collapseActionsOnMobile()` remain the separate levers for actually restructuring the menu).

  A behaviour note for anyone asserting on raw markup: because the unified view echoes the whole `wire:click` expression through `{{ }}`, its quotes now render as `&#039;` entities (the old table view left them literal). This is functionally identical — the browser decodes the attribute before Livewire reads it — and safer than a raw echo for string record keys. Verified end to end in a real browser via CDP: the escaped `wire:click` actually fires Livewire for both a row button and a context-menu dropdown item, the group dropdown opens, and a narrow viewport does not overflow. Full core / table / forms / sortable / Integration suites green; Pint and PHPStan clean; new `ActionClickResolverTest` (core: resolver default, host-supplied click, `RendersAsButton` contract, memoised-vs-closure fragments, record-less render) and `TableActionClickResolverTest` (table: `executeTableAction` / `openActionModal` routing, rendered `wire:click`, group threading).

### Security
- **Inline cell edits and editable-panel writes now fail closed.** `WithTable::updateTableCell()` and `WithEditablePanel` delegated to a hand-rolled permission check that let the write through when no user was resolvable; both now delegate to the canonical fail-closed `HasAuthorization::isAuthorized()` (Gate/policy-backed), so an unauthorized or unauthenticated caller is denied.
- **`belongsTo` relation columns with a non-`id` owner key join on the correct column.** `RelationMetadata` never read `getOwnerKeyName()`, so a custom owner key fell back to `.id` and the planner joined on the wrong column (wrong rows / none); the owner key is now resolved correctly.
- **`BadgeColumn` escapes its value by default.** The badge view emitted the cell state with `{!! !!}`, a stored-XSS sink for any user-controlled column; it now escapes like `TextColumn` and only renders raw markup through the explicit `->html()` opt-in.
- **Sortable reorder writes are scoped to the table's base query.** `WithSortable::reorderRows()` wrote through a model-global query keyed only on the client-supplied primary key, an IDOR that let a caller reorder rows outside the visible/tenant scope; writes now go through a clone of the table's base query, plus a `canReorder()` gate.
- **Repeater relation rows save through the model, not a query-builder `update()`.** The relationship save handler bypassed casts, mutators, and model events on edit; it now fetches and `fill()`/`save()`s so casts and events apply.
- **`wire-config` MCP tool no longer exposes arbitrary application config.** A free-form key read *any* config path (`app.key`, DB passwords, service secrets) into the model context; it is now restricted to the wireStack namespaces. A `wire-core` boost guideline also wrapped a live component tag in `@verbatim` so the compiled guideline no longer renders it.
- **MarkdownEditor preview no longer executes injected markup (DOM-XSS).** The preview escaped only `<`, `>` and `&`, so a markdown link could break out of the `href` attribute (an unescaped `"`) or use a `javascript:` scheme — stored-XSS-capable when one user's markdown is previewed by another. Quotes are now escaped and link URLs are scheme-validated (only `http`/`https`/`mailto`/`#`/relative), with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`.
- **CSV and Excel exports neutralise spreadsheet formula injection.** Cell values were written raw, so a value such as `=HYPERLINK(...)` or `=cmd|'/c ...'!A1` was evaluated as a formula when the file opened (and OpenSpout turned a leading `=` into a live formula cell in `.xlsx`). Values a spreadsheet would evaluate — leading `=`, `@`, tab/CR, or a non-numeric leading `+`/`-` — are now prefixed with a single quote; genuine numbers (including negatives) are left intact. The guard is shared via `ResolvesExportValue`.
- **Audit entries keep a non-integer (UUID/ULID) actor.** `resolveUserId()` was typed `?int`, so a string user key was dropped (a caught `TypeError` under `strict_types`) or coerced to a wrong integer; it now returns `int|string|null`, and the `audit_logs` `user_id`/`auditable_id` columns are strings. `user_id` is no longer a `nullOnDelete` foreign key, so an audit entry retains who acted even after that user is deleted.

### Fixed
- **A per-record closure icon on a column no longer throws.** `TextColumn::make(...)->icon(fn ($record) => ...)` passed the raw `Closure` to `renderIcon(string)` → `TypeError`; the icon is now resolved with the record (and a closure icon opts out of the shared cell skeleton, since it is not record-invariant).
- **`FormatsState::since()` works on its own.** The `diffForHumans()` branch was gated behind `date()`/`dateTime()` also being set, so `->since()` alone was a silent no-op and returned the raw value; it now formats standalone.
- **Action button labels are no longer double-encoded.** The label was `e()`-escaped into the render array and then echoed again through `{{ }}`, turning `&` into `&amp;` and showing `<`/`>` as literal entities; the view is the single escaper now.
- **The confirmation dialog honours the action's configured icon.** The modal-host hardcoded `exclamation-triangle` and ignored `->modalIcon(...)`; it now reads the configured icon (falling back to the triangle).
- **`AuditEntry` no longer shadows Eloquent's `Model::getChanges()`.** The old/new diff was named `getChanges()`, overriding the framework method with an incompatible shape; it is renamed to `getChangeDiff()`.
- **The state-driven view-render memo is flushed between Octane requests.** `HasViewRenderCache` keeps its memo in a class static that would otherwise accumulate across requests in a long-lived worker (unbounded growth, potential cross-tenant bleed); `WireCoreServiceProvider` now flushes it on Octane's `RequestTerminated`, guarded by `class_exists` so there is no hard Octane dependency and it is a no-op on FPM/CLI.
- **Chart widgets no longer leak a Chart.js instance on re-render.** The widget's Alpine component had no `destroy()`, so a Livewire morph that changed the datasets baked into `x-data` re-initialised Alpine and ran `new Chart()` again — leaking the previous instance (and its RAF/listeners) and throwing "Canvas is already in use". It now tears the old chart down in `destroy()` and `wire:ignore`s the canvas Chart.js owns. Browser-verified via CDP.
- **A repeater no longer resets every row's collapse state when a row is added or removed.** The collapsed state was a per-item array (length = item count) baked into `x-data`, so changing the item count changed the attribute text → Alpine re-initialised the whole repeater. Collapse state is now keyed by index in a stable object, so `x-data` stays byte-identical across the add/remove morph. Browser-verified via CDP.

### Performance
- **The TipTap editor bundle is code-split — the default editor ships ~12.5 kB gzip less.** The opt-in extensions (`withTables()` / `withImages()` / `withHighlight()` / `withTextAlign()`) all default off yet were statically bundled into every editor page (~40 kB / ~12.5 kB gzip of `prosemirror-tables` alone). They now ship in a separate ESM addon bundle, and esbuild `--splitting` keeps the shared `@tiptap/core` + ProseMirror in one chunk both entries import — so a table-less page downloads less and enabling tables never ships a second copy of the ~270 kB core. The addon `<script type="module">` is injected via `@assets` only when a field on the page needs it. Browser-verified via CDP (core boots without the addon; enabling tables loads it and `insertTable()` works).
- **A searchable select no longer embeds its options map twice.** Every combobox instance baked the full options JSON into `x-data` under both `initialOptions` and `options`; `options` is now seeded from `initialOptions` at init, halving the options payload per select. Browser-verified via CDP.
- **Dead render-path views removed.** The base column `cell.blade.php` (superseded by the `tables.columns.text` render path) and the `filter-chevron` overlay (select-like filters delegate to the searchable-select combobox, which draws its own chevron) were unreferenced; both are deleted, along with `FilterControl`'s dead `withChevron` branch and a shared `entry-label` partial replacing the label markup copy-pasted across 10 infolist/panel entry views.

## [1.11.0]

### Added
- **`FileUpload::cropInteractively()` — the user places the crop frame, still without a dependency.** The centre crop shipped earlier implements the stated API (`imageCropAspectRatio('16:9')` names a ratio, not a UI), but choosing *which* 16:9 region needed a frame. Built on the canvas pipeline that was already there rather than on `cropperjs`: the only missing piece was an offset, so the whole feature is a draggable box — **4 KB of bundle against ~50 KB of library**, loaded only for fields that crop, and no v1/v2 API churn to track. Locked to the ratio (so the output shape cannot vary), guarded to a single raster image (there is no sensible frame to drag over five files at once, or over an SVG), and requires a ratio — an unbounded frame has nothing to constrain it. Verified in a real browser (`workbench/scripts/verify-image-crop.mjs`, 5/5) by pushing a half-red/half-blue image through the picker: framing the top yields red, the bottom yields blue. That pair matters — asserting only the top would pass just as well if the offset were ignored.

  The browser found two bugs a green suite could not. The frame rendered **0×0 and invisible**: `fitFrame()` ran on the image's `load`, which fires while the modal is still `display:none`, and the crop maths reads the image's *natural* size — so the pixels came out right while the user had nothing to drag. Underneath that, the modal had been placed outside the field's `x-data`, so the component could not see a single `x-ref`.

- **A multiple `FileUpload` now enforces per-file size, not just the file count.** The two limits cannot share a key — `max:` means kilobytes of a file but a *number of items* of an array — so a multiple upload could bound how many files it took and nothing about their size. Rules that describe one item of a list now mount at the wildcard path (`data.photos.*`) via a new `Contracts\ProvidesItemValidationRules`, generalising what the resolver previously did only for `Repeater`. `FileUpload` contributes `maxSize`/`minSize` there when multiple, and keeps `maxFiles`/`minFiles` on the field's own key. Driven through the real form runtime with actual oversized uploads (a rule sitting in an array proves nothing), and the tests were checked to fail when the per-item rules are withheld.

- **`FileUpload` manages files on a private disk end-to-end — previews that actually resolve, and real deletion — behind a new shared URL resolver.** The field's preview URL was built by a naive local resolver: a full URL passed through, everything else went to `Storage::disk()->url()`. On a **private** disk that throws (`This driver does not support retrieving URLs.`) and took the whole field down, and `visibility('private')` — which until now only chose `storePublicly*` vs `store*` at save time — was ignored on the way back out. The very same monorepo already resolved this correctly one package over, in `table`'s `ImageColumn` (`data:` passthrough, a signed `temporaryUrl()` for non-public files, a `url()` fallback for drivers that cannot sign). That ladder is now a single canonical owner — **`Foundation\Support\StoredFileUrlResolver`** in `core`, the lowest layer both consumers can reach — and `ImageColumn` and `FileUpload` both delegate to it instead of each carrying their own copy (the resolver names its downstream consumers in prose, not `@see`, so `core` never imports upward). On top of it `FileUpload` gains: **`visibility('private')`** previews served through a signed, expiring URL, its lifetime set by **`signedUrlExpiration(minutes)`** (default 5); a graceful **degrade to no-thumbnail** (the filename still shows) instead of a fatal when a driver — a `local` disk with no temporary-url route — cannot produce any URL at all; and **`previewUrlUsing(fn (string $path) => …)`** to supply the URL (or a `data:` URI) yourself for exactly that case. Deletion is now opt-in rather than never: removing a stored file still only drops the reference by default, but **`deletesFromDisk()`** also removes the physical file, and **`deleteUsing(fn (string $path) => …)`** runs custom teardown (a derived thumbnail, a detached record) and implies deletion — a full URL / `data:` URI (an external reference the field never stored) is never deleted either way. Runtime-checked in passing that a *single* pending upload holds a bare `TemporaryUploadedFile`, not a one-element array as an earlier note assumed — so no consumer normalisation is owed. New `StoredFileUrlResolver` at 100%, plus `FileUploadDiskLifecycleTest`; PHPStan and Pint clean; documented in [`docs/forms/fields/file-upload.md`](docs/forms/fields/file-upload.md) (EN + CS).

- **`FileUpload::fileNameUsing()` / `storeFileUsing()` — name the stored file, or own the whole path.** Storage could pick any disk (`disk()`, or the `wire-forms.file_upload.disk` config) and a folder (`directory()`), but the filename was all-or-nothing: a random hash, or the original client name via `preserveFilenames()` — nothing in between and no way to place a file at a computed path. `fileNameUsing(fn (UploadedFile $file) => $order->id.'.'.$file->extension())` names the stored file (within `directory()` on `disk()`) and wins over `preserveFilenames()`; returning an empty value falls back to the default naming. `storeFileUsing(fn (UploadedFile $file, string $disk) => $file->storeAs("reports/{$year}", 'summary.pdf', $disk))` hands you the whole store step — pick the folder, write to the disk, return the stored path — and takes precedence over `directory()` / `preserveFilenames()` / `fileNameUsing()`. Both route through `storeUploadedFile()` with a thin `resolveStoredFileName()` seam, so with neither callback set the stored path is byte-for-byte what it was before. Covered by `FileUploadStorageNamingTest` (default hash, `preserveFilenames`, custom name + fallback + precedence, full-path override + precedence + non-string result, and the private-visibility store branches); documented alongside (EN + CS).

- **Every fluent setter in the library now reaches a consumer — the dead-API debt is zero, and `composer api:dead` keeps it there with no baseline.** A gate added this version found **34 setters whose value nothing ever read** (all confirmed independently). They are now resolved one by one rather than counted: `TextColumn::fontFamily()` reaches the canonical text classes; `ToggleColumn::onIcon()`/`offIcon()` render inside the knob; `Section::aside()` puts the heading beside the fields; `DateTimePicker::closeOnDateSelection()` closes a datetime picker on the date click (browser-verified); `TextInput::revealable()` swaps the input type client-side, so the secret never leaves the field; `CheckboxList::groups()` renders a heading per group (its option markup extracted to a partial, so the flat and grouped layouts cannot drift); `ColorPicker::format()`/`hex()`/`hsl()`/`rgb()`/`rgba()` convert both ways around the hex-only native swatch (browser-verified 6/6); `PollColumn::refreshMethod()` names the host method the poll calls and `keepContentWhileLoading(false)` hides the stale value while refreshing.

- **`StackedColumn`/`SplitColumn::searchable(['name', 'email'])` searches every listed column.** The list was set and never read: the planner emitted one clause from the column's own name, so searching a stacked name-over-email cell only ever matched the name. A new core contract `Core\Query\Contracts\HasSearchColumns` lets a composite column widen its own search planning; an empty list means "no opinion" and falls back to the column name, so implementing it can never silently disable search. Care taken where it bites: the strategies OR their clauses inside one `where()`, so extra clauses widen the match rather than narrowing it, and a column's `sqlExpression` is applied only to the column it describes. Asserted against real rows, since the whole change lives in the SQL.

- **Editable panels — a new `wire-core` surface for editing one record in place (`NyonCode\WireCore\Panels`).** A *panel* looks like an [infolist](docs/core/panels.md) (the same declarative schema of sections, grids, and `Entry` components) but alongside read-only entries it hosts **editable** entries — `ToggleEntry`, `CheckboxEntry`, `SelectEntry`, `TextInputEntry` — that write **straight back to the bound Eloquent record**, each committing on its own with optimistic UI and optimistic locking. Infolists stay read-only by contract; the panel is the read-*and*-write surface (settings screens, quick inline edits) that sits between an infolist and a full form. It reuses the canonical machinery rather than duplicating it: editable entries extend the infolist `Entry` base (so read-only and editable entries mix in one schema) and commit through the **shared `wireEditableCell` engine** — generalized here with a configurable `commitMethod` (default `updateTableCell`, so existing editable columns are byte-for-byte unchanged) — into a new host trait `WithEditablePanel::updatePanelEntry()`, which mirrors the table's `updateTableCell` contract: `skipRender`, a **write whitelist** (only entries declared `EditableEntry` in the schema are writable — a read-only entry name or any out-of-schema attribute is refused), `permission()` + record-aware `disabled()`/`canEdit()` guards, and a locked `updated_at` optimistic-lock transaction. The record is always resolved **server-authoritatively** from the component's own bound record inside the transaction (the client-supplied key must match), so a component that shows one record can only ever write that record. Extend `PanelComponent` (holds your record, returns the schema from `panel()`) or compose the trait onto an existing Livewire host. Fully covered by `EditablePanelTest` (happy-path writes, whitelist rejection of read-only/out-of-schema names, optimistic-lock conflict, `disabled`/permission/key-mismatch guards, `saveUsing`/`afterStateUpdated`, layout-nested entries — 100% coverage on the new files), PHPStan level-6 clean, Pint clean, and **browser-verified end to end** via the new `workbench/scripts/verify-panel-editable.mjs` CDP driver against a Model-backed `/previews/panels-editable` screen (toggle, select, and text each flip optimistically *and* persist to the row, no 419, no console errors). Documented in [`docs/core/panels.md`](docs/core/panels.md) (EN + CS).

- **`wireEditableCell` now syncs its optimistic-lock version to sibling cells of the same record after a commit — editing several fields of one record in a row no longer falsely conflicts.** Every editable cell captures the record's `updated_at` version at render time, and the host `skipRender`s on commit (to preserve Alpine state), so once one field writes, the record's version advances while its siblings keep the stale version — the *next* field edited on the same record would hit a spurious optimistic-lock conflict. This was invisible in tables (one record per row) but is the common case for a panel (many fields of one record). On a successful commit the engine now broadcasts a `wire-editable-committed` event scoped to the host's Livewire component id + record key; sibling cells adopt the new version (unless mid-save or actively being edited). A genuine external change by another client still never dispatches this event, so real cross-client conflicts are still caught. Also fixes sequential edits of two cells in the same table row. Guarded against dist drift by `DropdownAssetTest`.

- **Nested modals can return data to any ancestor — new callback bindings `$set` / `$setParent` / `$setFrame` / `$parentData` / `$arguments`.** Because every stack level is state of one Livewire component, an action or footer callback can now write straight back into the form that opened it: `$set(path, value)` (own frame), `$setParent(path, value)` (the immediate parent — a safe no-op at the root), `$setFrame(depth, path, value)` (any level, for power users), `$parentData` (read the parent's live data), and `$arguments` (the arbitrary array passed to `mountAction($name, [...])` / `openActionModal($key, $name, [...])`). Any write re-renders the whole stack, so this is the canonical **create-and-select** pattern — a sub-form that fills a field on its parent, e.g. `->action(fn (array $data, $setParent) => $setParent('customer_id', Customer::create($data)->id))`. Kept deliberately callback-driven (no rigid fluent "return" API) so any composition is possible. Covered by new `ModalStackingTest` cases in both hosts (`$setParent` round-trip, `$setFrame` into an arbitrary depth, `$arguments` exposure).

- **Modal-navigation primitives `replaceMountedAction()` and `cancelParentActions()` (Filament parity).** Two lifecycle controls for composing deep flows, callable directly (`wire:click`) or from any action/footer callback as `$replace(name, arguments)` / `$cancelParents(?upTo)` (and via `$component`). `replaceMountedAction($name, $arguments = [])` swaps the **active** modal for another **in place** — pops the top frame and mounts the named action at the same depth, leaving parent frames untouched — so a footer button can navigate within a modal (e.g. "back to step one", or trade an edit modal for a confirm modal) instead of stacking a new layer; when the replaced modal was a single-record row action, its record is inherited unless the caller passes an explicit `record`/`recordKey`. `cancelParentActions(?string $upTo = null)` closes the active modal **and its parents**: with no argument it tears down the whole stack (one "Cancel" that dismisses every level), or given an action name it unwinds up to and including the nearest ancestor with that name. Both live in the shared engine (`InteractsWithActions`); each host implements a small `mountActionByName()` seam that routes a replacement through its own row/header/bulk resolution. Covered by new `ModalStackingTest` cases in both hosts (in-place swap with record inheritance, callback-driven `$replace` that survives the auto-close guard, full-stack and named-ancestor cancel) and demonstrated on the `/previews/actions-modal-stacking` workbench screen.

- **`BelongsToSelect::editOptionForm()` now auto-fills and auto-updates the related record through the relationship — matching `createOptionForm()`'s auto-create.** Previously only `createOption()` had a relationship-aware auto path (create the related model when no `createOptionUsing()` was given); the edit modal fell through to the base `Select`, which does nothing without explicit `fillEditOptionUsing()` / `updateOptionUsing()` callbacks. `BelongsToSelect` now overrides both: `getEditOptionFormData()` loads the selected related record by key and fills the edit form from **only the columns the edit schema declares** (a form of `[TextInput::make('name'), TextInput::make('email')]` pulls `name` and `email` alone — never the id, timestamps, or unrelated columns — so a subset edit form shows and touches exactly its own fields), and `updateOption()` writes the submitted data straight back to that record — each still yielding to an explicit `fillEditOptionUsing()` / `updateOptionUsing()` callback when you provide one. A **dotted field name walks a nested relation** on fill: `TextInput::make('company.name')` fills from `$related->company->name` into a nested `['company' => ['name' => …]]` state path (via `data_get`/`data_set`, relation-type agnostic, so an empty relation yields `null`). On write-back the relation **kind** decides ownership: an owned **`hasOne`** (or its polymorphic **`morphOne`**) — a 1:1 record the related model owns, e.g. `profile.bio` — is written back through the relation, updated in place or **created** if missing (a `morphOne` create sets the polymorphic `*_type`/`*_id` itself) — and the same owned record can equally be persisted explicitly from `updateOptionUsing($record)` via the relation's `save()` (`$record->avatar()->save($avatar)`, which likewise stamps the morph type on a new record), while a shared **`belongsTo`** (e.g. `company.name`, or its polymorphic **`morphTo`** — a `MorphTo` *is* a `BelongsTo`) and any other dotted relation are left to `updateOptionUsing()` rather than being mutated or crashing on a non-existent column — and to make that write-back ergonomic, both `fillEditOptionUsing()` and `updateOptionUsing()` are now **handed the resolved related `$record`** (Filament-parity), so the callback can read/write through the relation itself (`$record->company?->update(...)` / `$record->origin?->update(...)`) or **re-point** it (`$record->company()->associate(...)` / `dissociate()`) instead of re-loading the model — and for a **`morphTo`**, `associate()` re-points a *different type* (it sets both the `*_type` and `*_id`), while `dissociate()` nulls both. Re-pointing a `belongsTo` also works through the auto path when the foreign key is edited as its own column (it is, after all, a real column on the related model). An owned **`hasMany`** (or **`morphMany`**) is edited through a relationship-backed **Repeater** in the edit form (`Repeater::make('books')->relationship('books')`): the fill loads the existing rows into the repeater (scoped to its subfields plus the key, so rows can be matched), and the write-back **syncs** them through the same canonical `RelationshipSaveHandler` the main form save uses — creating new rows (a morph create sets the polymorphic `*_type`/`*_id` itself), updating matched ones in place, and deleting removed ones. (When you'd rather keep control, `updateOptionUsing($record)` can persist the collection explicitly with the relation's `saveMany()` — building the model instances yourself and letting the relation stamp each foreign key; `saveMany` creates and updates but never deletes, which is the caller's trade-off to make.) (The cascade now keys off `HasOneOrMany`, so both `hasMany` and `morphMany` repeaters save — from the edit-option modal **and** the main form, where a `morphMany` repeater previously no-op'd.) An owned **`belongsToMany`** (or either polymorphic direction, **`morphToMany`** / **`morphedByMany`**) works the same way through a relationship Repeater, but its rows carry the **related model's key plus pivot columns** (e.g. a `role`): the fill reads each row's key and pivot data off the loaded pivot, and the write-back **`sync()`s the pivot** in one statement — attaching new associations, detaching removed ones, and updating pivot columns on the rest (or, from `updateOptionUsing($record)`, a non-destructive `$record->tags()->syncWithoutDetaching([...])` that leaves unlisted associations attached, a `$record->tags()->toggle([...])` that flips membership of exactly the listed ids, or a `$record->tags()->updateExistingPivot($id, [...])` that rewrites the pivot columns of an already-attached row without attaching or detaching) (a morph sync writes the polymorphic `*_type` into the pivot on its own, since both `MorphToMany` and the `morphedByMany` inverse are a `BelongsToMany`). A **custom pivot model** (`->using(...)` — a plain `Pivot` for `belongsToMany`, a `MorphPivot` on either polymorphic direction) is honoured on both ends — the fill reads pivot columns through it (so its casts apply) and the write-back `sync()`s through it — because the flow works at the relation level rather than touching the pivot table directly; equally, an **ad-hoc pivot with no `->using()` model** (the framework's plain `Pivot`/`MorphPivot`) round-trips several declared pivot columns just the same. (Extending `RelationshipSaveHandler` with the `BelongsToMany` branch also means a relationship-backed `belongsToMany` Repeater now saves from the **main form** too, where it previously no-op'd.)
