A **read-through relation** (`hasManyThrough` / `hasOneThrough`) is deliberately **not** auto-written: it is a `HasOneOrManyThrough` — neither an owned `HasOneOrMany` nor a `BelongsToMany` — and it has no `create`/`sync` that could establish the through-link (which intermediate parent would own a new distant row is unknowable), so the fill still loads it for display (a `hasOneThrough` via a dotted field, a `hasManyThrough` via a repeater) but the write-back safely skips it — no orphaned or deleted distant rows — leaving any intentional write to an explicit `updateOptionUsing($record)`. Because the form runtime propagates the parent record into relationship fields everywhere (including per Repeater item), this works at the top level **and inside a Repeater item** with no callbacks at all. Covered by new `BelongsToRelationshipRecordTest` cases (auto-fill + auto-update at top level and inside a repeater item, a subset-columns form, nested-`belongsTo` (and polymorphic `morphTo`) dotted fill / empty-relation `null` / own-column-only write-back / shared-parent left untouched unless written via `updateOptionUsing` / re-associate via FK own-column and via `associate()`/`dissociate()` / `morphTo` re-associate to a different type and dissociate, nested-`hasOne` (and polymorphic `morphOne`) fill / write-back / create-when-missing / explicit `save()` update and create via `updateOptionUsing`, and nested-`hasMany` repeater fill-with-keys / sync create-update-delete / explicit `saveMany()` create and mixed-update via `updateOptionUsing`, nested-`belongsToMany` pivot fill / sync attach-detach-update-pivot, nested-`morphMany` fill-with-keys / sync with polymorphic type on create, nested-`morphToMany` pivot fill / sync with polymorphic type in the pivot, nested-`morphedByMany` inverse-morph pivot fill / sync, read-through `hasManyThrough` / `hasOneThrough` fill / safe-no-op write-back / explicit `updateOptionUsing` escape hatch, custom-pivot-model `belongsToMany` (plain `Pivot`) / `morphToMany` / `morphedByMany` (`MorphPivot`) whose cast round-trips through fill and sync, ad-hoc-pivot `belongsToMany` / `morphToMany` with no pivot model whose several columns round-trip through the plain `Pivot` / `MorphPivot`, a non-destructive `syncWithoutDetaching` pivot write via `updateOptionUsing` that never detaches an unlisted association, a `toggle` pivot write that flips membership of only the listed ids, and an `updateExistingPivot` write that rewrites an attached row's pivot without attaching a not-yet-related id).

- **Inline nested actions — `Action::registerActions([...])`.** A modal opened from within another can resolve its nested action without polluting the top-level `actions()` / `->actions()` list: declare it inline next to the action that opens it, and both host resolvers (the standalone `resolveAction()` and the table `findAction()`) find it by name via a shared, depth-first `matchRegisteredAction()`. Because the definitions live in the same `actions()` / `table()` closure that re-runs each request, they survive Livewire roundtrips (a raw closure-carrying inline instance would not). The table's `openActionModal()` / `openBulkActionModal()` / `openHeaderActionModal()` also gained an optional `array $arguments` so a callback can seed a nested action's `$arguments`. Covered by new `ModalStackingTest` cases (forms + table).

- **Sorting and filtering by a related column now actually work — through a real `LEFT JOIN`, for `belongsTo` / `hasOne` / `hasOneThrough`, and honouring the relation's own scopes and constraints.** The docs promised "simple belongsTo relations → LEFT JOIN (enables sort/filter)", but production silently didn't: `TableQueryService::registerRelationChain` registered the related *models* with the metadata registry and never the *relations*, so `MetadataRegistry::getRelation()` always returned null, the planner set `canJoin = false`, and a sort on `company.name` compiled to `select * from users` — no join, no `order by`, the sort lost. `RelationMetadata::fromRelation()` (the write side) was dead as a result. Now the chain registers the singular relations the join builder can key, and the whole path is covered end to end (the test that would have caught the original regression, plus a mutation check that both the join keys and the base-select qualification are load-bearing):

  - **`belongsTo` and `hasOne` join and are sortable/filterable**, including nested chains (`company.country.name` → two chained joins). The base `select *` is qualified to `{table}.*` when a join is present, so a base column and a joined column that share a name (`users.name` vs `companies.name`) no longer collide into the wrong cell. Filtering rides the same join: the table filter and the column-header filter both plan through it, so `SelectFilter::make('company.name')` (or `make('company')->column('company.name')`) constrains the joined table in SQL rather than silently dropping. Sorting and filtering the same relation share **one** join.
  - **`hasOneThrough` expands to two chained joins** through its intermediate table (base → intermediate → far), the intermediate getting its own synthetic alias so sort + filter still dedupe to those two joins. The scoping is deliberately narrow: only the singular, safely-keyable relations register — `hasMany` / `belongsToMany` / `hasManyThrough` / `morph*` stay eager-loaded for display (a join would multiply parent rows), each guarded by a test.
  - **The joined side matches what Eloquent's relation query returns.** A relation carrying any constraint — the model's **global scopes** (`SoftDeletes`, tenancy, an `active` flag, anything from `addGlobalScope()`) **or** constraints declared on the relation method itself (`belongsTo(...)->where('active', true)`) — is joined as a scoped **subquery** (`left join (select * from companies where deleted_at is null and active = ?) as …`) rather than the bare table, so trashed/scoped-away rows never contribute to sort or filter. The `LEFT JOIN` stays a `LEFT JOIN`: a parent whose related row is scoped away keeps its row with the related value read as `NULL`, it is not dropped. A relation with no scopes or constraints keeps a plain direct-table join (no subquery overhead). Two subtleties that a green SQLite run hid and a browser/real-DB reasoning surfaced: the subquery must be handed to `joinSub()` as the **Eloquent** builder, since global scopes are compiled in `toSql()` and are absent from the raw query builder; and "does this relation need a subquery?" can't be answered from `->wheres` alone (global scopes aren't stored there) — it is `getGlobalScopes() !== []` **or** a method-declared `where`.

  New `Core\Query\JoinScope` value object (pure, cacheable) carries how each side is constrained; `JoinClause` / `JoinRegistry` / `QueryPlanner` / `ApplyRelations` thread it through. Covered by `RelationJoinScopesTest` (global scope + soft-delete combined, a method constraint on a scope-less model, LEFT-JOIN preservation both ways, a through relation scoping both hops, the plain-relation direct-join fast path, combined scope + filter + sort binding order, and a nested chain whose first hop is a scoped subquery) and by new `TableQueryServiceTest` / `RelationMetadataTest` cases; PHPStan and Pint clean. Documented in [`docs/table/columns/relations.md`](docs/table/columns/relations.md) (EN + CS), where the remaining limits are stated: relation-method constraints are not applied to a `hasOneThrough` (only its models' global scopes are), a constraint that correlates to the parent row (`whereColumn('companies.x', 'users.y')`) can't be a subquery, and `morphOne` is not join-scoped.

### Changed
- **The action-modal endpoints split out of `WithTable` into `Concerns\InteractsWithTableModals`** — submit, close, run a bulk action, resolve which action a frame belongs to, and the pre-frame-stack confirmation API. A host concern for the same reason the action seams are: it works on the component's state container. `WithTable` is now **2,165 lines**, from 3,479 at the start of this version.

  A note left in the code for whoever reads that block next: it is filed under "Legacy Confirmation Modal (Backwards Compatibility)", but **only half of it is legacy**. `confirmTableAction()`, `executeConfirmedAction()` and `closeConfirmationModal()` are the pre-frame-stack API and carry `@deprecated … v2.0`; the three `*WithData()` methods sitting under the same heading are live — the halt modal executes through them. The heading is the only thing they have in common.

  Verified in a browser: `verify-nested-modal` 8/8 (a nested modal stacks over an inert parent, one scrim, Escape closes only the top frame, the parent's form value survives) and `verify-wizard-live` 14/14 (a live toggle adds a step, per-step validation blocks Next, a valid step advances).

- **The action-engine seams split out of `WithTable` into `Concerns\InteractsWithTableActions` — and doing so revealed that four core defaults were being overridden silently.** These are the seams the shared `InteractsWithActions` engine calls to ask where a table keeps its action frames (a stack under `modal.actions`, halt modal under `modal.halt.*`). They stay a host concern: they read and write the Livewire component's state container, so they cannot be a service.

  **The find:** while `haltModalFormStatePath()`, `afterActionExecuted()`, `resolveActionRecordIds()` and `sendActionNotification()` lived in `WithTable`'s own body, they *silently replaced* the concrete defaults in `InteractsWithActionForms` and `InteractsWithActions` — PHP lets a method defined in the using trait beat an imported trait's without any `insteadof` and without a trace. Moving them into a trait of their own turned all four into real collisions, which is the improvement: the table's answers now say out loud that they replace the engine's, next to the two `insteadof` lines that were already explicit. The behaviour is unchanged (`haltModalFormStatePath()` still returns the table's `tableState.modal.halt.formData`, not the engine's `actionModalHaltData` default) — it is just no longer invisible.

  **Also removed: four properties `WithTable` redeclared.** `$actionModalFormInstance`, `$haltModalFormInstance`, `$actionModalInfolistInstance` and `$actionModalConfigCache` are already provided by the traits it composes, and were declared a second time here — identically, which is why PHP accepted it (it only rejects an *incompatible* redeclaration) and why nothing noticed.

  Verified in a browser, since this is the modal frame stack: `verify-modal-layering` 13/13 and `verify-nested-modal` 8/8 — a nested modal stacks over an inert parent, one scrim, Escape closes only the top frame, and the parent's form value survives the round trip.

- **Sub-rows split out of `WithTable` into `Concerns\CanExpandSubRows` + `Services\SubRowFilters`.** The largest section of the trait (~480 lines) — expansion state, the eager-load fast path, sorting, "show all", and two different kinds of filtering. `WithTable` is down to **2,839 lines** from 3,479 at the start of this version.

  The filter *rules* became a service because they only ever needed the `Table` and the raw state arrays, never the Livewire host — so they can now be exercised without one. Worth keeping straight, because the two are easy to confuse: a **scoped main filter** (`Filter::subRows()`) constrains children the same way it constrained their parents, while the **interactive bar** is the per-column filter a user types under an expanded row. `SubRowFilters::hasActiveInteractive()` is load-bearing rather than informational — it is what disables the eager-load and in-memory fast paths, which would otherwise hand back unfiltered children.

  The expansion endpoints stay a host concern: the chevrons, the filter bar and "show all" call them. `getSubRows()`'s fast path is left exactly as it was, comments included — children are eager-loaded per page to avoid N+1, but only when no sub-row filter is active *and* the loaded set is complete, since a limited eager load ships a `*_count` so a later "show all" can tell it is looking at a partial set. Unlike `SummaryBatch`, that property was already guarded: `WithTableSubRowsTest` and `WithTablePerformanceTest` count queries.

  **A second copy of the "which filters are scoped to sub-rows" rule remains** in `TableQueryService`, where it is embedded in a larger classification loop that also sorts out custom callbacks and planner-incompatible filters. The two were checked against each other and agree — both require `appliesToSubRows()`, `canView()`, a non-empty raw value and a non-empty extracted value — so this is one rule written twice rather than a drift. Consolidating it means restructuring the query seam, which is its own change.

- **The `withCount`/`withSum` map that backs every rollup column had two copies — now `Services\AggregateSubqueries` — and record selection split out of `WithTable` into `Concerns\CanSelectRecords`.** `WithTable::getSelectedRecords()` rebuilt the five-arm aggregate map inline, its own comment admitting it "applies the same withCount/withSum aggregate subqueries that buildTableQuery() adds". A missing subquery does not error: the rollup attribute is simply absent, so a summary over it plucks nothing and renders **0** — a wrong total rather than a failure, which is exactly the kind of duplication that stays wrong quietly.

  **The copies had already drifted, and the difference is left alone on purpose.** `TableQueryService` passes a sub-row constraint so a filtered sub-row table and its rollup agree; the selection copy never did. With sub-row scoped filters active, a selection-scope rollup therefore counts *all* children while the query-scope one counts only the filtered ones. Whether selection should follow a sub-row filter is a product question — selection is deliberately an explicit set of keys, unaffected by the table's filters — so the shared applier is called without the constraint, preserving today's behaviour exactly. The difference is now written down in the code and pinned by tests rather than living in a copied `match`.

  `CanSelectRecords` is the first slice off `WithTable` (3,479 → 3,322 lines). It stays a host concern rather than a service: these are the endpoints a consumer's component calls. Worth knowing — **four of its nine methods have no internal caller at all** (`toggleRecordSelection`, `deselectAllRecords`, `isRecordSelected`, `areSomeVisibleSelected`): the table's own UI binds `tableState.selection.records` through Alpine's `$wire.entangle`, so those exist purely as consumer API and only tests exercise them.

- **A column's header-filter API moved into `Concerns\CanBeFiltered` + `Services\ColumnFilterFactory`.** Nineteen methods and the imports of all five concrete filter types lived in `Column` — a factory inside a component. `Column` now asks for a filter and gets one; the factory is the only place that knows which `Filter` backs `filterAsSelect()` and how it is configured, which also makes the legacy `filterable(type: 'select')` vocabulary and the fluent helpers share one map instead of two. Public API is untouched: `filterable()`, `filter()`, `filterAs*()`, `filterOperator()`, `filterUsing()`, `applyFilter()` and friends keep their signatures and behaviour — all fourteen entry points were checked to build the same filter as before, and the filters were driven against a real database (text, select, multi-select and number-range each return the right rows; an empty value still filters nothing). Verified in a browser on `/previews/table-column-filters` (8/8).

  **Correcting the audit that prompted this:** it called `Column` "a base class knowing every one of its own subclasses". It is not — `Column extends DataComponent`, `Filter` is its own root, and the two hierarchies are unrelated. The real problem was smaller and worth fixing anyway: factory logic embedded in a 1,577-line component.

- **`updateTableCell()`'s write path moved out of the trait — into `Services\CellValueWriter` and the shared `Foundation\Support\RecordVersion` — but the two edit hosts did *not* collapse into one action.** [The plan](architecture/plans/god-object-decomposition.md) proposed a single core `CommitEditableValue` that both `WithTable::updateTableCell()` and `WithEditablePanel::updatePanelEntry()` would delegate to, on the grounds that they were "structurally near-identical". They share a *skeleton*, not bodies: the table has five save strategies to the panel's two, plus `CellUpdating`/`CellUpdated` events, record-aware validation, two-phase dehydration, a conflict notification and its own translation namespace. One action covering both needs roughly seven injection points — a template disguised as a service, worse than the duplication it removes.

  What was genuinely shared is smaller and sharper: the **optimistic-locking convention**. A record's version is its `updated_at` timestamp as a string; `'0'` is the client's "I never had one" sentinel; an untimestamped record is unguarded. Three rules that had to agree across two hand-written copies, with the stamp itself spelled out eight more times. `RecordVersion` owns them, both hosts use it, and it is now the kind of thing you can test — including the `UPDATED_AT` bug it exposed (see Fixed).

  The table's five save strategies became `CellValueWriter`, where their **order is the contract**: an author's callback outranks the legacy callback, which outranks pivot, then relation, then a plain attribute. That chain lived in a transaction closure inside a 213-line method inside a 3,500-line trait. Authorization, validation, locking and versioning stay with the host — it is the only thing that can answer for them. Verified in a real browser on `/previews/panels-editable` (7/7: optimistic toggle, select and text writes all persist, no 419s, no console errors). **Worth naming: the table's inline editing has no preview at all** — `panels-editable` drives the panel host only — so the table's own commit path has never been browser-checked, only unit-tested.

- **`SummaryBatch` moved to `Services/` and stopped being a bag of statics — and the one thing it exists for finally has a test.** It was a `final class` whose static methods compiled and ran SQL, filed under `Columns/`, and whose `runRollup()` rebuilt by hand the `fromSub` wrapping that `SummaryCalculator::wrap()` already owns. It is now an instance service, injected, reusing `wrap()`; `WithTable`'s two call sites resolve it from the container. It did **not** fold into `SummaryCalculator` as the plan first proposed: the calculator computes one value and holds no column, the batcher compiles many summaries across many columns into one query and needs them — merging the two would have made one class with two responsibilities.

  **The gap worth naming:** `SummaryBatch` exists solely to run one aggregate query instead of one per summary per column, and nothing asserted that. 85 of its 88 statements were covered — but only *indirectly*, through the `WithTable` suites, which check the numbers and not the query count. Breaking the batching would have left every test green and merely made the footer slower on every render. `SummaryBatchTest` now counts queries (six summaries across two columns → one query; plain + rollup → two, not four; unbatchable summaries → zero, deferred to the per-summary path), and was verified to fail when the batching is deliberately disabled.

- **`HasSummary` (616 lines of SQL and statistics in a trait) is now `CanBeSummarized` + `Services\SummaryCalculator` + `Services\SummaryFormatter`.** It was the standard's anti-example made real: derived-table wrapping, nine SQL aggregates, sample variance and `number_format()` all lived in one trait mixed into `Column` — so all 13 column types carried median maths, and none of it could be tested without building a column. The split follows the shape the standard asks for: the concern keeps configuration and the fluent API (`summarize()`, `summarizeSum()`, `summaryDecimals()`, …) and delegates; two `final` services own the work; and what they need to know about a column arrives as immutable value objects — `Support\SummaryFormat` (decimals, separators, prefix/suffix) and `Support\SummaryTarget` (column, rollup flag, format). `SummaryCalculator::median()` and `::variance()` are now callable with a plain collection and no database.

  The formatter is a constructor dependency of the calculator rather than a peer, because exactly one summary type — `Range` — produces a rendered `"min – max"` string instead of a number. That is the single place computing and formatting genuinely meet, and it is why the two are not fully independent.

  Public API is unchanged: `computeSummaries()`, `getSummaries()`, `hasSummary()`, `hasSummaryInScope()` and `normalizeBatchedSummaryValue()` all keep their signatures, so `WithTable`, `TableExport` and `SummaryBatch` were untouched. **One subtlety worth recording:** of the five places the old trait plucked values, four ended in `->values()` and one — the closure branch for a real column at `query` scope — deliberately did not, handing the user's callback a key-preserving collection. Routing that through a shared helper would have silently reindexed it; the calculator keeps the asymmetry and says why.

- **`Foundation\Concerns\HasVisibility` split three ways — `Column` composes it, and stops carrying API that could never work.** It bundled three capabilities behind one name. A table column wants exactly one: `disabled()` is meaningless on a column, and `visibleWhen('field', 'value')` reads a *sibling field's* live state through the `$get` accessor that only exists inside a form — on a column `$get` is never injected, so it would answer "visible" whatever it was handed. The bundle also collided outright: `ButtonColumn::visibleWhen(Closure)` is a documented BC alias for `visibleForRecord()`, a different concept with the same name, and PHP rejects the incompatible signature.

  | Concern | Owns | Composed by |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `HasVisibility` | `visible()`/`hidden()`/`isVisible()`/`isHidden()` + `HasAuthorization` | everything, now incl. `Column` |
  | `CanBeDisabled` | `disabled()`/`isDisabled()` | fields, layouts, widgets |
  | `InteractsWithStateConditions` | `visibleWhen()`/`hiddenWhen()`/`disabledWhen()` | fields, layouts, widgets |

  Non-breaking: every surface that had the full set (`Component` — so every field —, `LayoutComponent`, `Widget`) composes all three, so no public method moved off any class it was on. The shared `stateCondition()` factory moved to `StateMatcher::condition()`, the canonical comparison owner whose docblock already named these three as its callers. `Column` gains authorization-aware visibility (`isVisible()` now answers false for an unauthorized column) and a working `hidden(Closure)` — see Fixed.

- **`Column` now uses the canonical `HasDefault`, `HasTooltip` and `HasPlaceholder` concerns instead of its own copies — and a `TextInputColumn` no longer offers `-` as its input hint.** Three of the capabilities `Column` hand-rolled already had a canonical owner in `Foundation/Concerns/`, which `CLAUDE.md`'s invariant says to extend rather than re-implement. Adopting them is a **widening**: all three canonical setters accept `string|Closure|null` and evaluate, so `->placeholder(fn () => …)`, `->tooltip(fn () => …)` and a Closure `->default()` now work on columns, where the local copies took plain strings and returned them unevaluated.

  The placeholder was not a swap — it was **two concepts wearing one name**. `Column::getPlaceholder()` hard-coded a `?? '-'` fallback, so it could never answer null, which is fine for "this cell is empty, show a dash" and wrong for "this is the hint an input shows while empty": `TextInputColumn` passed it straight to its input, rendering `placeholder="-"`. The two are now separate — `getEmptyCellText()` owns the dash (one place, not the six `?? '-'` / `?? ''` the old fallback had scattered around, several of which were dead code precisely because `getPlaceholder()` never returned null), and `placeholder()` keeps the canonical input-hint meaning. `SelectColumn` is unaffected: it sets its localized placeholder explicitly in its constructor, so its `?? Trans::get(…)` was always redundant rather than broken.

  **Three of the six planned adoptions did not land, and the reasons are worth recording** ([plan](architecture/plans/god-object-decomposition.md)):
  - **`HasExtraAttributes`** is type-incompatible, not hook-shaped: `Column` stores a raw `?string` attribute blob, the concern stores `array|Closure` and returns `array`. Unifying is an API change for anyone writing `extraAttributes('class="x"')`, not a refactor.
  - **`HasPrefixAndSuffix`** would hand every column `prefixIcon()`, `suffixIcon()`, `prefixAction()`, `suffixAction()`, `hintAction()`, `hasAffix()` and a dependency on `Action` — a form-field affix system, where a column wants two strings. It also carries a `getFieldAction()` that collides with `HasActions`'s. The concern needs splitting before anyone adopts it.
  - **`HasLabel` had the wrong target.** `Column` does not re-implement the concern — it overrides its *parent*, `Core/Components/DataComponent`, which owns its own `$label` + `getLabel()`. The duplication is `DataComponent` vs `HasLabel`, both in core, and they disagree: `DataComponent` renders `company_name` as "Company name", `HasLabel` (and Column's override) as "Company Name". Unifying changes the label casing of the five other `DataComponent` subclasses, so it is its own decision rather than a line item here.

- **`TableQueryService` moved from `Concerns/` to `Services/` — it was a `final class` in the directory the coding standard reserves for traits.** The name said service, the address said trait; nothing about it was ever a concern. It now lives at `NyonCode\WireTable\Services\TableQueryService`, and the three call sites that did `new TableQueryService` resolve it from the container instead. **It is deliberately not a singleton:** the service memoises the last query plan (`getLastPlan()`), so a shared instance would leak one table's plan into the next — it has no constructor, so `app()` auto-resolves a fresh one, matching the old `new` semantics exactly. The old FQCN keeps working through a `@deprecated` alias until v2.0, joining the shims already queued there; the alias is pinned by a test, because an alias nobody exercises is an alias that silently stops working. Its four unit tests moved to `tests/Unit/Services/` to match. Nothing in the docs asks a user to construct it (it is named only descriptively, in `table/overview.md`), so this is internal.

  This is the first step of [`architecture/plans/god-object-decomposition.md`](architecture/plans/god-object-decomposition.md), and the point is the directory more than the file: `packages/table/src` had **no `Services/` at all** — nor does `packages/forms/src` — which is precisely why logic accumulated in traits (`WithTable` is 3,520 lines; `HasSummary` is 616 lines of SQL and statistics mixed into every column). There was nowhere else to put it. The seam's path changed in `CLAUDE.md`, `AI_BLUEPRINT.md`, `AI_RECIPES.md`, `AI_COMPONENT_CATALOG.md` (where it was filed under "Main concerns" — wrong twice over) and `architecture/{table,integrations}.md`.

- **Table filters now render as the same select surface as a forms `Select` — `SelectFilter` and `TernaryFilter` default to the shared combobox, with `->native()` as the opt-out.** The forms `Select` has defaulted to the canonical combobox (`$native = false`) for a long time, but `SelectFilter` defaulted to `$native = true` and `TernaryFilter` had no choice at all (its panel view and the `filterAsBoolean()` column-header partial hard-coded a browser-native `<select>`) — so opening a filter gave you an OS-drawn control sitting next to comboboxes, and a table's header row mixed both designs at once. Both filters now default to the combobox and share one owner for the choice: the new `Foundation\Concerns\HasNativeControl` (core — the lowest layer that can own it, so `SelectFilter`, `TernaryFilter` and future select-like surfaces stop re-declaring the same `native()`/`isNative()` pair). `TernaryFilter` gained `getOptions()` (`['true' => …, 'false' => …]`) which models the "All" state as the combobox **placeholder** — picking it clears the filter — letting a ternary reuse the ordinary optional-select surface rather than a bespoke three-`<option>` view. `->native()` still opts any single filter back out to a `<select>` (both native paths were kept and are covered). Verified in a real browser via CDP on `/previews/table-overview` and `/previews/table-column-filters` (a header row with text + select + multi-select + boolean filters now reports **zero** native `<select>` elements), plus new `SelectFilterTest` / `TernaryFilterTest` coverage for both surfaces. Docs track it in [`filters/select.md`](docs/table/filters/select.md) and [`filters/ternary.md`](docs/table/filters/ternary.md) (EN + CS).

- **Every surface with a native/custom choice now shares one owner: `Foundation\Concerns\HasNativeControl`.** The forms `Select`, the table `SelectColumn` and the forms `DateTimePicker` were migrated onto the shared concern, retiring the last copies of the duplicated `$native` property + `native()` / `isNative()` pair. The concern was **renamed from `HasNativeSelect`** once the date picker joined: its `native` is `<input type="date">` vs the Alpine picker, not a `<select>`, so the old name would have lied about half its consumers (it was introduced earlier in this same unreleased version, so nothing external depended on the name). It holds `?bool $native = null` — distinguishing "never asked" from "explicitly off" — and exposes two extension points: `defaultNative()` for a surface whose *default* differs (necessary because PHP rejects a trait property redeclared with a different initial value; the same "default via overridable method" shape the repo already uses for `HasSheetOnMobile::defaultSheetOnMobile()`), and an aliasable `isNative()` for a surface that must *force* native in some mode — which is how `DateTimePicker` keeps month mode (`<input type="month">`, the custom calendar has no month-only grid) outranking an explicit `->native(false)`. Pure consolidation: no rendered output changes (core/forms/table/sortable/integration suites, PHPStan and Pint all green; the forms combobox re-verified over CDP at 14/14 on `/previews/forms-wizard-live`). One **API-visible nit**: `Select::native(bool $condition = true)` and `DateTimePicker::native(bool $condition = true)` are now `native(bool $native = true)` — only affects the (unused) named-argument form `->native(condition: …)`. Covered by `HasNativeControlTest` in core (both extension points) plus new `DateTimePickerTest` cases pinning the month-mode forcing.

- **`Foundation` no longer carries a compile-time dependency on `Modals` — `Foundation\Enums\ModalWidth` dropped its `HasModalProperties` import.** The import existed purely so a `{@see HasModalProperties::getMaxWidthClass()}` docblock link could be written short; the enum never referenced it in code, which left `Foundation` (the bottom layer) and `Modals` (an application surface above it) importing each other — a cycle on paper, in the one direction the `CLAUDE.md` invariants forbid. The reference is now named in prose, so nothing behavioural changed and the cross-reference is still there for a reader. **This was only harmless while both live in the same package:** the moment `Modals` / `Actions` / `Infolists` are split out of `wire-core` into packages *above* it (the direction modularity is heading), `core → wire-modals → core` becomes a composer-level cycle. Five more such references remain — all `Foundation` → `Actions\Action` / `Widgets\Widget`, and all **real typehints** rather than doc links, so they need a `Foundation\Contracts\Action` plus dependency inversion, not a docblock edit; see [`architecture/plans/v1/modularity-audit.md`](architecture/plans/v1/modularity-audit.md) §E. **Trap worth knowing before attempting the same fix elsewhere:** fully qualifying the name inside a `{@see}` tag does *not* work — Pint's `fully_qualified_strict_types` (part of the `laravel` preset; `pint.json` only adds `declare_strict_types`) rewrites it straight back into a real `use` import on the next `composer lint`, silently restoring the dependency. The reference must stay prose, not a tag; the enum's docblock now says so. Recorded there as well: the split of **vocabulary** (`ModalWidth`, in `Foundation`) from **mapping** (`getMaxWidthClass()`, in `Modals`) is *correct* and must not be "fixed" — the vocabulary has two consumers in two namespaces (`Modals\Concerns\HasModalProperties::width()` and `Actions\ActionHalt::width()`) so it belongs at the bottom, while the mapping has exactly one (`Modals\View\*` ×3 plus `modals/suspended.blade.php`), making `Modals` the lowest layer that can own it. (`HasColor` owning both vocabulary *and* mapping is not a contradiction: colours are cross-surface, modal width is not.) No behaviour change; Pint clean across repeat runs, PHPStan `No errors`, core suite green.

### Removed
- **Breaking: `PollColumn::maxPolls()` and `::onComplete()` are gone — neither could be honoured.** `maxPolls()` advertised a safety cap and enforced nothing: a column cannot see how many times it has polled, and unlike a forms field it has no route to the host (`Column` has no `getLivewire()`), so the count would have to live in table state that no host ever kept. `onComplete()` had the mirror problem: without per-record polling memory it cannot tell "just finished" from "was already finished", so wiring it naively would have fired the callback on every render of a completed row. Building that memory is a seam, not a patch — and rather than half-build it or leave two fake knobs, both are removed along with `handlePollComplete()`. `stopWhen()` / `pollWhile()` / `stopOnComplete()` remain: those are conditions the column can actually evaluate. Docs (EN + CS) now say the cap does not exist and why.

- **`FileUpload` crop, resize and avatar are real — and they run before the upload.** `imageCropAspectRatio()`, `imageResizeTargetWidth()`, `imageResizeTargetHeight()` and `avatar()` were dead setters: a field configured to crop uploaded the untouched original. The picked image is now cropped to the ratio (from its centre) and fitted inside the target box **in the browser**, which is the entire point — resizing a 12 MP phone photo *after* it has been uploaded has already cost the user the upload. `avatar()` implies a 1:1 crop (an explicit ratio still wins, either call order) and a round preview. Deliberately **no new dependency**: a canvas crops and downscales in a few lines, and nothing in this API asks the user to drag a frame — `imageCropAspectRatio('16:9')` names a ratio, and that is what it delivers. An interactive cropper is the remaining delta and is the part that would cost a library. Care taken where it bites: a PNG stays lossless, an SVG passes through (no pixels to resample), a failed decode returns the original rather than losing the file, and nothing is ever upscaled. The processing path also avoids racing Livewire — it listens on its own input, so rather than swallowing and re-dispatching `change` (which would come down to listener order), the user picks into an input we own and only the finished file is placed into the `wire:model` one. Fields without processing keep their original single-input markup untouched. Verified in a real browser (`workbench/scripts/verify-image-upload.mjs`, 6/6): a 1000×1000 PNG came out 320×180 at 16:9, 23 478 B → 1 953 B.

- **`FileUpload` limits are now enforced, not just advertised.** `maxSize()` reached nothing but the hint under the dropzone — the field said "up to 5 MB" and then accepted anything, because no rule was ever derived from its config; `minSize()`/`maxFiles()`/`minFiles()` reached nothing at all. `FileUpload` now implements the existing `ProvidesImplicitValidationRules` contract (as `Radio` and `Select` already did), honouring that the same word means different things to Laravel: on a single upload `max:` is kilobytes of file, on a multiple upload the state is an array and `max:` is a count of items — pinned by a test, since mixing them would silently validate the wrong thing. **Known gap:** per-file size on a *multiple* upload needs `field.*` rules, which only the Repeater's resolver path emits today.

- **`Foundation\Contracts\DehydratesState` / `HydratesState` — the form save path finally has a seam (ADR 0021).** A component could shape its value on the way *in* (`getStateType()` → `StateHydrator`) but not on the way *out*: `Dehydrator` resolves the **model's** cast and never consults the field. Two consumers had already worked around the gap, each inventing its own protocol — `SaveHandler` hardcoded `instanceof FileUpload` as save step 2.5, and `WithTable` duck-typed `method_exists($column, 'formatForSave')` in **three** places. Both now speak one named contract in core: `FileUpload` implements `DehydratesState` (its store-on-submit body moved out of `SaveHandler` unchanged), `TextInputColumn` implements both halves (`formatForSave()` / `formatAfterLoad()` are kept as deprecated aliases — unlike `native()` they *work*, so removing them would break real behaviour rather than a fiction), and `FormRuntime` applies `hydrateState()` after the cast. Two interfaces rather than one, because most components need a single direction and a forced no-op counterpart is noise. **`dehydrateState()` is only promised to be a pure function of its arguments, and the table no longer needs more than that.** `updateTableCell()` dehydrates twice by design — once without a record to pre-validate outside its transaction, once with the locked record inside it — but both passes now start from the state the client sent instead of the second consuming the first's output. "Pure" never implied "idempotent under self-composition", yet the old code required it: `TextInputColumn` survived only by accident (its number parsing is guarded by `is_string()`, which a second pass fails), while a user's `->beforeSave(fn ($v) => $v + 1)` was silently applied twice. **Trap the swap itself walked into, worth knowing before expressing any seam this way:** `method_exists()` fails loudly, `instanceof` fails *silently*. `WithTable` never imported `DehydratesState`, so `$column instanceof DehydratesState` resolved against its own namespace to `NyonCode\WireTable\Concerns\DehydratesState` — a class that does not exist, and `instanceof` against one neither autoloads nor throws, it returns `false`. Every editable cell quietly stopped trimming, casing, parsing numbers and running `beforeSave()`, writing the raw client string to the column; PHPStan and Pint were clean on it, and the suite was green because the tests asserted the column's method in isolation rather than the transform happening *through* the host. Both are now covered end-to-end, and the new [`api:instanceof`](scripts/verify-instanceof-imports.php) gate fails the build on any `instanceof` target that resolves to nothing (449 across `packages/*/src` today).

- **`DateTimePicker::format()` and `::timezone()` do something at last.** Both were dead — [`api:dead`](scripts/verify-dead-api.php) flagged them and nothing read them — because there was nowhere for them to act; the whole "Format" section of their docs configured values the field ignored. `format()` now shapes the value written to the model (it cannot drive the state: state *is* the widget's value, and a native `<input type="date">` parses only `Y-m-d`), and `timezone()` converts in **both** directions — inbound only would have written the shifted time straight back, silently moving it. Both are **opt-in**: unset, the value is stored exactly as the widget produced it. That guard is not decoration — the first implementation formatted unconditionally via `getFormat()`, which defaults to `config('wire-forms.date_format')` (`d.m.Y`), and would have made every existing field quietly start writing `09.03.2026` into a column holding `2026-03-09`. A test caught it. `timezone()` applies to `datetime` only: a bare date or time is a wall-clock value that a conversion would corrupt.

- **`DateTimePicker::displayFormat()` is honoured by the custom picker.** Also dead: the input always showed the raw state (`2026-03-09T14:05`). It now formats with PHP `date()` tokens (`9. 3. 2026 14:05`), parsed from the state string rather than through `Date()`, which would drag the browser's timezone into a value that carries none. A native input deliberately does not get it — its display format belongs to the browser and the user's locale.

- **Breaking: `fileAttachmentsDirectory()` is gone from both `TiptapEditor` and `RichEditor` — it configured an upload neither editor can do.** No view, Blade or JS ever read it: the Tiptap toolbar inserts an image by URL (`setImage({ src })`) and uploads nothing, and `RichEditor` has no upload path at all — so the setter named a directory for a feature that does not exist. `RichEditor`'s was the worse of the two: unlike Tiptap's it was **documented**, with its own "File Attachments" section promising an "upload directory for embedded images/files". Both are removed along with their getters, mirroring the `native()` decision — a fake knob is worse than no knob — and the docs section and API row went with them (EN + CS). A test on each class pins the absence so the setter cannot be reflexively re-added.

  Worth noting for whoever audits next: the api-docs gate could not have caught `RichEditor`'s, because the method *was* mentioned on its page. The gate knows "documented but absent" and "present but undocumented"; it cannot know a documented method does nothing. Every dead setter found so far shares one shape — **a public setter whose getter has no production consumer** — which is the signal a future check would need.

- **Breaking: `SelectColumn::native()` / `::isNative()` are gone — they were a no-op dressed as a switch.** The editable cell's view never branched on `isNative()`: it commits through `wireEditableCell` (`x-model` + commit-on-change) and has no binding for the shared combobox, so it has always rendered a browser-native `<select>` and `->native(false)` silently did nothing — while [`docs/table/columns/select.md`](docs/table/columns/select.md) advertised a whole "Native vs Styled" section promising it produced a "custom styled dropdown". Rather than keep a documented-but-fake toggle (or ship a half-real one), `SelectColumn` no longer uses `HasNativeControl` at all: the choice does not exist for this surface, so the API no longer pretends it does. `Column` is not `Macroable` and defines no `__call`, so a leftover `->native(false)` now fails loudly with `Error: Call to undefined method` instead of being quietly ignored — an intentional trade: a call that never did anything cannot break behaviour by being removed, only by being noticed. Docs rewritten in EN + CS to state what actually renders and point at `SelectFilter` (or a forms `Select` in an edit action) for a searchable dropdown, and two tests pin it: the cell renders a native `<select>`, and `SelectColumn` exposes no `native()`/`isNative()` (guarding against someone reflexively re-adding the concern).

- **The literal Tailwind hues are now first-class colors, no longer aliases of the semantic roles — and the palette gains adaptive `white`/`black`.** Since 1.8.1 the full palette resolved on every surface, but five raw hue names were folded into a semantic role: `blue`→primary, `green`/`emerald`→emerald, `yellow`/`amber`→amber, `red`→danger, `cyan`→info — so `->color('blue')` painted the re-themeable brand primary rather than an actual Tailwind blue, and there was no way to ask for a literal blue/green/yellow at all. `blue`, `green` and `yellow` are now **their own literal hues**, distinct from `primary` / `success`(emerald) / `warning`(amber); `red` and `cyan` gain their own `Foundation\Colors\Color` cases too (they render the same hue as `danger`/`info` but stay addressable by name), while only the true same-hue aliases remain (`emerald`→success, `amber`→warning, `secondary`→gray). The palette additionally gains the two **achromatic endpoints** `white` and `black`: Tailwind has no numeric `white`/`black` scale, so both resolve **adaptively** through the canonical resolvers — `black` is a dark ink/fill in light mode and flips to white in dark mode, `white` is the inverse — keeping them readable on both themes (on filled surfaces they are literal `bg-white`/`bg-black`; on text-only surfaces they flip via `dark:` variants; the modal-submit resolver uses `!text-gray-900` for `white` to beat its `text-white` base). All **17 `HasColor` resolvers** (`getSolidColorClasses` / `getOutlinedColorClasses` / `getGhostColorClasses` / `getIconButtonColorClasses` / `getBadgeColorClasses` / `getChoiceColorClasses` / `getTextColorClasses` / `getLinkColorClasses` / `getSolidBgClass` / `getSoftBgClass` / `getRowTintClasses` / `getGradientFillClasses` / `getFillTextClasses` / `getModalSubmitButtonClasses` / `getAlertColorClasses` / `getModalIconBgClass` / `getModalIconTextClass`) and the `Color` enum (new `Blue`/`Green`/`Red`/`Yellow`/`Cyan`/`White`/`Black` cases; `resolve()` maps the literal hues to themselves) were updated together. **Breaking (in theory only):** `Color::resolve('blue')` now returns `Color::Blue` instead of `Color::Primary` (and `red`→`Red`, etc.) — but `resolve()` has **no production consumer** (enum-driven color flows through `->value`, and downstream surfaces read the string), so no rendered output for the semantic roles changes; only callers who explicitly asked for `blue`/`green`/`yellow` now get the literal hue they named. Because a consuming app scans both package `views` **and** `src/**/*.php`, the new literal class strings are picked up automatically; the bar-chart `safelist.blade.php` (views-only builds) was extended with the new `yellow`/`white`/`black` gradient + accent-text classes and its `BarChartSafelistTest` `colorKeys` widened. Covered by rewritten alias assertions and new literal-hue / adaptive-endpoint coverage in `ColorTest`, `Concerns\HasColorTest` and `Actions\HasColorTest` (core/forms/table/integration suites green, PHPStan clean, Pint clean). The color swatch reference in [`docs/core/foundation.md`](docs/core/foundation.md) (new **Achromatic (adaptive)** swatch group + reworked role table and notes), the forms [`radio.md`](docs/forms/fields/radio.md) supported-colors list (EN + CS) and the wire-core boost guideline all track it.

- **Stacked (nested) action modals are now a *live frame stack*, not a suspend-snapshot — every layer stays a reactive form.** [1.10.1] made modals stack, but only the **top** modal was a live wire-forms instance; each parent was frozen into a `suspendedActions` snapshot and rendered as a dead, dimmed *card* (heading + description only), and a nested modal had **no way to write back** into the modal that opened it. The runtime is rearchitected onto a single ordered array of **live frames** — the standalone host's `public array $mountedActions`, the table's `modal.actions` `StateContainer` bag — where **every** open modal (top *and* each parent) is a fully bound, reactive wire-forms `Form` at its own depth-scoped state path (`mountedActions.{depth}.data` / `tableState.modal.actions.{depth}.data`). The crux is threading that per-depth `statePath` through `HasModal::getFormInstance()` / `getStepFormInstance()` so the `FormValidationResolver` prefixes field bindings **and** validation error keys per level automatically; the engine (`InteractsWithActions`) trades its suspend/resume seams for depth-addressed ones (`pushActionFrame` / `popActionFrame` / `getActionFrameState` / `writeActionFrameData` / `resolveActionForFrame` …), with the top-frame convenience (`getMountedActionState`, `resolveCurrentModalAction`, `getMountedActionModals`) resolved concretely from the stack. Rendering draws every parent as a dimmed, **`inert`** — but still *live* — form behind the active modal, so a value written into a parent is reflected behind the child at once; only the top modal is interactive (Filament-style), and a **single** scrim covers everything beneath it so a deep stack never darkens to black. `ModalStack::MAX_DEPTH` remains a runaway-re-entrancy guard, not a UX limit. The four-package suite is green, PHPStan/Pint clean, and the `workbench/scripts/verify-nested-modal.mjs` CDP driver passes 8/8 (live parent form behind, form data preserved across the stack, one scrim, `Escape` pops only the top). Documented in [`docs/core/actions.md`](docs/core/actions.md) → "Stacked (Nested) Modals" (EN + CS) and the wire-boost guidelines, with a live workbench preview at `/previews/actions-modal-stacking` (six configurations on a standalone `WithActions` host: basic stacking, create-and-select, deep `$setFrame`, inline `registerActions`, slide-over, and a stacked wizard — CDP-verified end to end). **This supersedes the suspend-stack from [1.10.1].**

- **`isActionModalVisible()` is the single source of truth for "a modal is open" (`= actionFrameCount() > 0`).** The rewrite briefly carried three overlapping representations (a per-frame `show`, a stable `open` flag, and the frame count); the per-frame `show` is gone, `open` is now only the client-side entangle mirror the active modal binds to, and the table dropped four duplicated resolver overrides (`resolveCurrentModalAction` / `regenerateModalConfig` / the two infolist resolvers) — the header-action nuance (its **form** builds from the live form-data bag, while config/infolist keep the record context) now lives in exactly one place, `InteractsWithActionForms::actionFormContext()`. Resolved `[action, context]` is memoized per request (`resolvedActionFrameCache`, cleared on push/pop/invalidate) so a render re-hydrates each frame's record only once instead of O(depth) times, and the dead `modal.action.*` legacy-property aliases were removed from `TableStateSchema`.

### Fixed
- **Deleting the last row on a page left the user stranded on an empty page instead of stepping back.** Standard length-aware pagination (`paginate()`) reports its total independently of the page it hands back, so once a delete on, say, page 3 removed the only row there, the stored page number still pointed at 3 and Laravel returned an empty result set — a blank table with working "previous" links but nothing on screen. Neither Livewire's `WithPagination` nor the paginator self-heals here; the table only ever reset the page to 1 on a *search/filter/sort/perPage* change, never after a data mutation. `WithTable` now owns a `clampPageToBounds()` that runs from the post-action hook (`afterActionExecuted()`, after `invalidateTable()`), so it fires for both a row `DeleteAction` and a bulk delete — anything through the action pipeline. When the current page is past `lastPage()` it re-anchors to the last populated page (dropping the cached empty result so the next render re-queries the right page), which walks back across *several* pages a bulk delete may have emptied at once. Only the length-aware (`standard`) mode is touched — `simple` and `cursor` have no total to clamp against, guarded both by a mode check and an `instanceof LengthAwarePaginator` guard once records are fetched — and page 1 (which always exists, even empty) and a delete that leaves rows on the page are both left alone. **Why this survived a green suite:** every existing pagination test set the page directly and asserted the query, never the browser round-trip of *delete-while-deep-in-the-pages*; caught by `PaginationClampAfterDeleteTest` (step-back on an emptied tail page, no move when rows remain, a bulk delete clamping past several emptied pages to page 1, page 1 untouched, and `simple` mode deliberately not clamped). Full table suite (1205), PHPStan and Pint green.

- **`CellUpdated` carried a null `oldValue` for the most common edit there is.** Of the five ways `updateTableCell()` could persist a value, four returned `'oldValue'` from the transaction and the fifth — the plain attribute write, the default path — did not. The post-transaction `$result['oldValue'] ?? null` then handed `null` to the event, so any listener reading the previous value got nothing from an ordinary inline edit while working fine for a pivot, a relation or a save callback. `EventDispatchTest` asserted `newValue` and never `oldValue`, which is how it survived; it asserts both now, and was checked to fail against the old behaviour.

- **An inline-edited record whose model renames `UPDATED_AT` was not optimistically locked at all.** Both edit hosts read `$record->updated_at` literally, so a model declaring `const UPDATED_AT = 'modified_at'` had no version — and a record with no version is not guarded, it is waved through. `RecordVersion::stamp()` goes through `getUpdatedAtColumn()`.

- **`Column::hidden(fn () => …)` silently did nothing — the column stayed visible.** `hidden()` accepted a Closure and stashed it in `$hiddenCallback`; `isHidden()` returned the plain `$hidden` bool and **nothing in the package ever read the callback**. So `->hidden(fn () => ! auth()->user()->isAdmin())` rendered the column to everyone. A previous audit (2026-06-23) fixed exactly this shape on `visible(Closure)`; the `hidden()` twin was missed.

  **The test suite had frozen the bug in place.** `ColumnConfigCoverageTest` asserted `->hidden(fn () => true)->isHidden()` was `false`, under a comment reading *"hidden() accepts a Closure (stored, not evaluated by isHidden)"* — someone noticed the Closure was dropped and pinned it as intended while chasing branch coverage. Covered ≠ correct. `Column` now composes the canonical `Foundation\Concerns\HasVisibility`, whose `isHidden()` evaluates, and the test asserts the fix.

- **A `BadgeColumn` or `IconColumn` over an ordinary JSON column took the whole table render down — the icon ladder is now `Foundation\Support\IconResolver` + `Foundation\Concerns\InteractsWithStateIcon`.** `getIconForState()` indexed its map with an unguarded `isset()`, and `isset($map[$array])` does not answer false — it throws `Cannot access offset of type array in isset or empty`. **No `icons()` call was needed to trigger it:** `isset([][$array])` throws just the same, so pointing either column at an `array`/`json`-cast attribute was enough to fatal out of `renderCell()`. `Column::formatValue()` carries a comment saying it was hardened for exactly this state "so a plain Column over them never fatals" — `TextColumn` survives it; the icon twins were missed. Two further drifts went with it: `IconEntry::icons(['x' => Icon::Pen])` leaked an `Icon` enum out of `getResolvedIcon(): ?string` (the same latent shape `PollColumn` had for colour, never reported), and `IconEntry` had no `iconUsing()` while both columns did.

  This is the icon twin of the colour consolidation below, and it mirrors it exactly: a `final`, dependency-free resolver owns the ladder (callback → `Icon` unwrap → scalar key behind an `is_scalar` guard → enum `HasIcon` contract → default), and the concern holds only configuration plus the same three hooks — `resolveStateIconOverride()` (`boolean()` mode), `resolveStateIconMap()` (an `Entry`'s `Closure` map) and `getDefaultStateIcon()`. **`->icon()` now means something on a stateful column:** it was dead on `BadgeColumn`/`IconColumn` (nothing read `getIcon()`; an unmapped state returned `null`) and is now the fallback, matching what `IconEntry` always did. `PollColumn::stateIcons()` is deliberately left alone — it is a record-aware, already-scalarised map, the twin of `stateColors()`, and is not vulnerable. Pinned by `IconResolverTest` (the ladder in isolation, both the array and object shapes) and `StateIconFatalTest`, which drives `renderCell()` rather than the resolver, because the resolver is not where it broke. Full suite (3709), PHPStan and Pint green.

- **`PollColumn` threw a `TypeError` out of `renderCell()` for a colour every other column accepts — the four "state → colour" resolvers are now one owner, `Foundation\Concerns\InteractsWithStateColor` + `Foundation\Support\StateColorResolver`.** `BadgeColumn`, `IconColumn`, `PollColumn` and `IconEntry` each re-encoded the same ladder (callback → `Color` unwrap → scalar key → map → enum `HasColor` contract → fallback), and the copies had drifted: `PollColumn`'s was the degraded one, missing both the `Color` unwrap and `EnumResolver::scalar()`. So `->colorUsing(fn () => Color::Success)` returned the enum out of a `: string` method and **took the whole table render down** (`PollColumn::make('status')->badge()->colorUsing(fn () => Color::Success)->renderCell($record)`), and an enum state indexed the map directly (`Cannot access offset of type X on array`). It had fallen out of the enum-cast consolidation (1.9) — the one column of the four with no test over `colors()`/`colorUsing()` and no workbench preview, so neither gate could see it. The resolver is a `final`, dependency-free class (testable on its own, per `AI_CODING_STANDARD.md`: no business logic in traits); the concern holds only configuration and three extension points — `resolveStateColorOverride()` (`boolean()` mode), `resolveStateColorMap()` (an `Entry`'s `Closure` map, evaluated against the record) and `getDefaultStateColor()`.

  Two behaviour changes fall out, both widening what already worked elsewhere. **`->color()` now means something on a stateful column:** it was dead on `BadgeColumn`/`IconColumn`/`PollColumn` (nothing read `getColor()`; an unmapped state hard-coded `'gray'`), and is now the fallback before `Color::Gray` — matching what `IconEntry` always did. And **`PollColumn` now honours an enum's `HasColor` contract through a render**, which it structurally could not: `getCurrentState()` flattens an enum to its scalar to key the class/icon/content maps, so colour resolution now takes the raw state instead (`getStateForColor()`), the way `BadgeColumn` and `IconColumn` already let an enum answer. `IconEntry` also stops leaking a `Color` enum out of `getResolvedColor(): ?string` when a `colors()` map holds one — the same latent bug as `PollColumn`'s, never reported. Pinned by `StateColorResolverTest` (the ladder in isolation) and `PollColumnStateColorTest`, whose last two cases drive the real Blade path rather than the resolver, because the resolver is not where it broke. Full suite (3670), PHPStan and Pint green; `/previews/table-overview` re-checked in the running app.

- **Every `colors()` / `icons()` example in the docs was keyed the wrong way round — a copy-paste would render a silently grey badge.** The maps are indexed by the **state** (`['active' => 'success']`), but every example wrote colour-to-state (`['success' => 'active']`), so `getColorForState('active')` found no key and fell through to grey; `icons()` had the same inversion, and `icon.md` additionally documented an array *value* (`'danger' => ['inactive', 'error']`) that the code never accepted. 32 entries across `badge.md`, `icon.md`, `poll.md`, `patterns.md`, `advanced.md`, `overview.md` and `getting-started.md`, in EN and CS. No gate could catch it — the method exists and the argument is still an array of strings, so reflection is satisfied while the reader gets the wrong output. A new semantic check in `verify-api-docs.php` closes that: colour and icon names are closed vocabularies, so it derives them from `Color::values()` and `DefaultIconSet::names()` (no hard-coded list) and fails when a map **key** is one of them — a key that is a colour cannot be a state. Verified to catch a re-introduced inversion, and to run over guides as well as class-reference pages (`getting-started.md` carries these examples too).

- **`DateTimePicker::timezone()` is now covered by a round trip through a real column** — `DateTimePickerTimezoneRoundTripTest`, on the MySQL/Postgres matrix. The existing tests only exercised the two halves in isolation; these save through an Eloquent `datetime` cast and read back, covering what the halves cannot: that a loaded value written back **does not drift** (the one-sided-conversion bug, asserted over three save cycles), that a summer-time offset is resolved per date rather than fixed, and that a date-only field keeps its calendar day. Verified with teeth, not just green: injecting the one-sided conversion fails 3 of the 6 cases. Run locally against MySQL 8 and Postgres 16 in containers, not only asserted to be matrix-eligible — the whole matrix scope passes on both (367 tests each).

  Correcting this changelog's own earlier claim: ADR 0021 said SQLite "would happily pass a wrong implementation". **It does not** — the injected bug fails on SQLite too. The field converts in PHP and stores a plain string, so for a `dateTime` column the round trip is driver-independent; the matrix run still guards against a driver that coerces on write (a Postgres `timestamptz` would), but the ADR overstated the case and now says so.

- **Czech docs anchors are readable again — `#rezimy`, not `#re-imy`.** `slugify()` lowercased bytes and then dropped everything outside `[a-z0-9]`, so a diacritic was **discarded rather than transliterated**: every Czech heading got a mangled, collision-prone id (`Režimy` → `re-imy`, `Souhrnná patička (agregáty)` → `souhrnn-pati-ka-agreg-ty`, `Volby času` → `volby-asu`) and the whole CS site linked to them. It now uses Laravel's own `Str::slug` — no `ext-intl` needed, which this repo does not require — and the 89 internal links that pointed at the old ids were remapped by computing both the old and new id for every heading, not by hand.

  The JS mirror in `verify-docs.mjs` was rewritten to follow `Str::slug` step for step, and the two are **asserted equivalent across all 1759 headings in the tree**. That check earned its keep immediately: a naive `[^a-z0-9]+ → '-'` mirror disagreed on 37 of them, because `Str::slug` *removes* punctuation before collapsing separators (`Date/Time Formatting` → `datetime-formatting`, not `date-time-formatting`). Left unnoticed, the gate would have quietly condemned working anchors and passed dead ones.

  **Breaking for deep links:** an external link to an old id (`#re-imy`) no longer resolves. Judged worth it — those ids were unreadable accidents nobody could have linked to deliberately, and nothing in the repo referenced one.

- **Four fluent setters that nothing read are now wired up — `ImageColumn::stacked()` / `stackLimit()` / `visibility()` and `SelectColumn::relationship()`.** All four configured state no caller ever consumed, so they were `native()`-class traps: fluent, documented, and inert. `ImageColumn` only ever rendered a single `<img>` from a plain Storage URL, so an array state is now a gallery — `stacked()` overlaps the images like avatars (`-space-x-2` + a separating ring, matching the infolist `ImageEntry` precedent), `stackLimit()` caps how many are drawn and summarises the rest as a `+N` chip, and a JSON array (as an `array`-cast column delivers it) is understood too. `visibility()` now decides how the URL is built: it **defaults to `'public'`** (previously `'protected'`, which nothing read) so every existing `->disk()` column keeps the exact URL it renders today, while any other value asks the disk to sign a temporary one, with a new `urlExpiry()` (default 5 min) — a driver that cannot sign, such as `local` without the temporary-url route, falls back to the plain URL rather than taking the table down with it. `SelectColumn::relationship()` set a relation name that no renderer read, so `->relationship('category', 'name')` produced an **empty** `<select>` while the docs promised it "loads options from a related model"; options are now loaded on the first cell render and memoized — the related list is identical for every row, so it costs **one query per render, not one per row** (pinned by a test counting queries across five rows), and an explicit `->options()` still wins. Covered by a new `ImageColumnGalleryTest` (gallery, JSON state, stacking, the `+N` cap, signed vs plain URLs, and the unsignable-driver fallback via a throwing mock disk) plus new `SelectColumnRelationshipLoadingTest` cases.

- **`ImageColumn` no longer mangles an inline `data:` URI into a storage path.** `resolveImageUrl()` gates "is this already a URL?" on `FILTER_VALIDATE_URL`, which **rejects** a data: URI — so an inline SVG or base64 image fell through to the storage branch and rendered `src="/storage/data:image/svg+xml,…"`, a guaranteed broken image. A `data:` source is now passed through untouched. Found while building the gallery preview: the browser reported all 28 images undecodable (`naturalWidth === 0`) even though every server-side assertion was green — the markup was right, the URL was not.

- **`PollColumn::stopWhen()` / `pollWhile()` were documented with the wrong argument — every example was a no-op.** The callbacks receive the **record** (`($this->stopWhen)($record, $this)`), but the docs showed `fn ($state) => $state === 'done'`, which compares an Eloquent model to a string: always false, so the column would poll forever. All four examples across EN + CS now pass `$record`, the API lines read `fn($record, $column) => bool`, and the previously undocumented `stopOnComplete()` shorthand is written up with a note about the argument.

- **A filter with no default never reached the server when rendered as a combobox — Livewire's `entangle()` silently no-ops on an undefined path.** `mountWithTable()` only seeded `tableState.filters.<name>` when the filter declared a `default()`, and `columnFilters.<name>` only for multi-select columns. That was invisible while filters rendered as native `<select>`s — `wire:model.live` *creates* the path on write — but a combobox binds via `$wire.entangle('tableState.filters.<name>.value')`, and Livewire bails out of entangle entirely when the property is `undefined` at render (`if (typeof … === 'undefined') { console.error('Livewire Entangle Error…'); return }`), so the picked option updated the Alpine label and **nothing else**: no roundtrip, no query, no console noise the user would see. Every *rendered* filter (and every filterable column) now gets a state slot at mount, holding its wrapped default or `null`; a null slot stays inactive everywhere (`apply()` ignores it, and it is not counted as an active filter or an indicator chip), and hidden filters are still only seeded when a default actually forces a value. This also fixes `SelectFilter::searchable()`, which has been quietly broken the same way since it started rendering the combobox. **Why this survived a green suite:** every server-side test sets filter state directly, so the one broken hop — browser → server — was the only thing never exercised; caught by driving the real preview over CDP (picking an option left all 4 rows on screen, while the native path filtered to 1). Covered by a new `WithTableQueryStringTest` case pinning the "every rendered filter has a slot" invariant.

- **Choosing an option in a filter's dropdown closed the whole Filters panel instead.** Both the table's Filters panel and the combobox inside it are teleported to `<body>` (so an ancestor's `overflow` can't clip them), which makes the inner panel a **sibling** of the outer one rather than a descendant — so Alpine's `@click.outside`, which tests plain DOM `contains()`, read a click on an option as "outside" the Filters panel and ran its `close()`. The panel collapsed mid-selection and left the option list stranded at the top-left of the viewport. The canonical dropdown primitive (`packages/core/resources/js/dropdown.js`) gained a `$clickedInside($event)` magic that walks the ancestor chain **through teleports** — hopping from a teleported subtree back to the `<template x-teleport>` it was cloned from via Alpine's `_x_teleportBack` link — so containment follows the nesting the author wrote rather than the flattened DOM. Applied to every teleported panel that can host another (`@click.outside="$clickedInside($event) || close()"`), it is a pure short-circuit guard: behaviour is unchanged when nothing is nested. Guarded against dist drift by `DropdownAssetTest`.

- **A `BelongsToSelect` relationship now actually receives the form's parent record — so `createOptionForm()` auto-creates the related model *and* relationship options auto-load.** `BelongsToSelect` resolves its related model by introspecting the `belongsTo` method on `$this->record`, which the field's docblock said was *"set by the form runtime"* — but nothing ever called `->record()`. As a result, through the real host flow both features silently no-op'd: `resolveRelationshipOptions()` returned `[]` (a relationship select with no manual `->options()` showed nothing) and `createOption()` returned `null` (so `->relationship('author', 'name')->createOptionForm([...])` without an explicit `createOptionUsing()` created nothing and selected nothing — Filament auto-creates here). `FormRuntime::prepare()` now propagates the form's `->model()` to every field exposing a `record()` setter (a Model instance as-is; a bare class string instantiated for relationship introspection) via a recursive schema walk that descends through layouts **and Repeater templates** — a Repeater is a `LayoutComponent`, so the record rides its template children into each per-item clone, which is what lets a relationship Select inside a repeater item auto-create too. Both the auto-create and the option-loading paths are covered by new `BelongsToRelationshipRecordTest` cases exercising the full Livewire host (relationship auto-create at top level and from inside a Repeater item, options loading, and the record dependency of `createOption()` itself).

- **A Select's `createOptionForm()` / `editOptionForm()` overlay opened from *inside* a stacked action modal no longer renders behind that modal.** The option overlay is a plain `<x-wire::modal>` at the base layer (`z-50`), but an action modal stack lives at `z-50 + depth·10` — so a "+ Create" affordance on a Select embedded in an action modal popped its form up *behind* the modal it was opened from (topmost element at centre-screen was the action modal, not the create form), leaving it unreachable. The overlay now reads the host's live `actionStackDepth()` (a new side-effect-free public accessor on `InteractsWithActions` — unlike `getMountedActionModals()` it rebuilds no forms, so it is safe to call mid-render from a field's own Blade) and layers itself one step above the top action frame via `ModalStack::zIndexForDepth()`; outside any action modal it stays on the base layer. The create/edit flow already resolved the frame-scoped field (`resolveFieldForAction()` searches the active modal form) and wrote back through `StateContainer::writeInto()`, so only the stacking z-index was wrong. Covered by new `SelectCreateOptionInModalTest` cases (frame-scoped resolution, write-back into the open frame, and the elevated z-index) and demonstrated on `/previews/actions-modal-stacking` (the depth-1 "Add note" modal now carries a create-enabled *assignee* Select).

- **A floating *panel* (Select/combobox list, date picker, tag list, action menu) opened from *inside* a stacked action modal no longer renders behind that modal.** The sibling of the option-overlay fix above, and the same root cause one layer down: the overlay that a Select's "+ Create" opens was lifted onto the action-frame layer, but the Select's **own dropdown panel** — the list you must reach *first* — was still stranded at its `z-50` class while the depth-1 modal around it sat at `z-60`, so the options rendered behind the modal that owned them. Teleporting every panel to `<body>` is what lets it escape an ancestor's `overflow` (a horizontally scrolling table, a modal's clipped body), but it also means the panel lands in the **root stacking context** — the wrapper it is teleported into is `position: static` and establishes no context of its own — where its class-level z-index competes directly against `ModalStack::zIndexForDepth()` and loses. The canonical `floatingAnchor()` primitive (`packages/core/resources/js/dropdown.js`, so every floating surface inherits it at once rather than each view re-encoding a z-index) now resolves the layer of the surface its **trigger** lives in and pins the panel one step above it: it walks the trigger's ancestors and takes the **outermost** explicit z-index rather than the nearest — a nearest-match for a trigger inside a modal's sticky header (`z-10`) would pin the panel *under* the modal — and the resolved layer is applied only when it **raises** the panel above the z-index its own view already gives it, so a trigger inside low-z page chrome can never drag its panel down. The offset is `+1`, never `+10` (`ModalStack::Z_INDEX_STEP`), so a panel clears its own modal but a modal opened on top of it still wins; the layer is re-asserted on every reposition because a Livewire morph strips the whole inline `style` attribute (absent from the server HTML) and would otherwise drop the panel back behind its modal on the next re-render. **Why this survived a green suite:** a buried panel still reports `offsetParent !== null` and still passes every server-side and naive DOM assertion — only a hit test at the panel's own coordinates catches it (`elementFromPoint` at the panel's centre returned the modal's sticky footer). Guarded by the new `workbench/scripts/verify-modal-layering.mjs` CDP driver (13/13 — panel outranks and is hit-testable inside a depth-1 modal, the create-option overlay above it, and no regression for page-level dropdowns, which keep their own layer with no inline override) and against dist drift by `DropdownAssetTest`.

- **A modal stacked over a *slide-over* (or any surface-type change up the stack) no longer renders off to the left edge and no longer closes when you click inside it.** The `<x-wire-modals::modal|slide-over|confirmation>` surfaces teleport to `<body>` via Alpine `x-teleport`; without a stable key, Livewire's morph reconciled the previously-active surface into the new one across a **type change** (slide-over → modal), collapsing the modal's flex-centering container so the panel became a direct child of the backdrop — pinned top-left, and clicks bubbling to the backdrop's close-on-click-away. Each teleported surface now carries a per-type `wire:key` (`wire-modal-modal` / `-slideover` / `-confirmation`) and each suspended parent shell a per-depth key, so morph never cross-reconciles differently-typed surfaces. (Browser-only; the stable `actionModalOpen`/`modal.open` visibility flag still prevents the separate Escape-closes-both morph-reset.)

- **A footer/action callback that mutates the stack — `$close()`, opening a nested modal, or `replaceMountedAction()` — is no longer fought by the auto-close step.** The "auto-close unless the callback opened a nested modal" guard compared frame **counts** (`actionFrameCount() <= $depthBefore`), which mis-fired two ways: a callback that *popped* its own frame (count `<` before) tripped a second close that removed the parent, and a callback that *replaced* the modal (pop + push, net-zero count) would have had its replacement immediately torn down. All three hosts (`InteractsWithActions::callModalFooterAction`, `WithActions::callMountedAction`, `WithTable::submitActionModal`) now compare a per-request **stack-mutation counter** (`actionStackVersion`, bumped on every push/pop): the modal auto-closes only when the callback left the stack completely untouched. Covered by regression tests for both the `$close()` and the `$replace(...)`-from-a-callback paths in each host.

- **`Action::registerActions()` now resolves for header and bulk actions too, not just row actions.** `matchRegisteredAction()` was generalized to the shared `BaseAction`, and `WithTable::findHeaderAction()` / `findBulkAction()` now recurse into registered nested actions (they previously did a flat name match), so a nested action declared inline on a header/bulk action and opened via `openHeaderActionModal()` / `openBulkActionModal()` resolves instead of silently no-oping.

- **A searchable Select inside a *table* action modal no longer pins to the top-left corner either.** The `wire-table` action-modal partial now preloads `wire-core::partials.floating-assets` on the table's initial render (same fix as the standalone host below), covering a table with no filters / column toggle / row context menu whose action modal contains a dropdown.

- Hardening from the same review pass: the standalone host no longer persists a raw Model inside a frame's `arguments` bag (`WithActions::mountAction` strips the record, which is already stored as a `{class,key}` descriptor); `getMountedActionModals()` keeps one entry per frame index-aligned with depth (a frame whose action vanished from the registry yields an empty config rather than shifting the array); `getValidationAttributes()` is threaded with the per-frame state path like its siblings; and the dead `ModalHostComponent::$showModel` prop was removed.

- **A searchable Select / floating dropdown inside a standalone `WithActions` action modal no longer pins to the top-left corner.** The floating-dropdown bundle (Alpine's `$float` magic, Floating UI) ships via `@assets`, but that directive sits inside the searchable-select partial — which, on a standalone `WithActions` host, first renders inside the teleported modal that only appears on the action roundtrip. Livewire does not inject a teleported `@assets` block in time, so the dropdown's `$float` was `undefined` (`TypeError: this.$float is not a function`) and the panel had no computed position. The core action modal-host view now `@include`s `wire-core::partials.floating-assets` once on the host's **initial** render (the host view is always present, even with no modal open), so the bundle is registered before any modal opens — no effect on wire-table, whose own dropdowns already load it on first paint.

- **Closing a nested modal no longer briefly hides the resuming parent (Alpine morph-reset).** The active modal element is reused across pushes/pops; binding its `wire:model`/entangle to a per-depth path (`…actions.{depth}.show`) changed the `x-data` text on every pop, forcing Alpine to re-initialise and reset the enter transition — so `Escape` on a child could flash the whole stack closed. The active modal now entangles a **stable** visibility flag (`actionModalOpen` / `tableState.modal.open`, managed by push/pop) instead, mirroring why the old single-slot `modal.action.show` worked. Caught only in the browser; verified fixed by the CDP driver (8/8).

## [1.10.2]

### Changed
- **Per-column header filters are now a placement of the canonical `Filter`, not a second filtering engine.** The header-filter machinery had grown into a parallel mini-API baked into `Column`: a `$filterType` string discriminator with **two** `match` maps (`applyFilterCondition()` for SQL + `renderFilter()` for the view), ~14 ad-hoc `$filter*` properties, and duplicated apply logic (`applyBooleanFilter` / `applyDateRangeFilter` / `applyNumberRangeFilter`) that re-implemented what `TernaryFilter` / `DateFilter` / `NumberRangeFilter` already did — a textbook violation of the repo's "one canonical owner per behavior / no parallel `match` maps" invariant.
