A column filter is now a **placement** of a canonical `Filter` in the header cell: `Column` holds a single `?Filter`, and `filterAsSelect()` / `filterAsMultiSelect()` / `filterAsDate()` / `filterAsDateRange()` / `filterAsNumberRange()` / `filterAsBoolean()` (plus `filterOperator()` / `filterSearchable()` / `filterPlaceholder()` / `filterDebounce()` / `filterUsing()`) become **thin, 100%-BC factories** over `TextFilter` / `SelectFilter` / `DateFilter` / `NumberRangeFilter` / `TernaryFilter` — the column owns *where* it renders and *which attribute* it targets, the `Filter` owns *how* it applies / renders / indicates / persists. The ~360-line duplicated engine (property bag + both `match` maps + the four `apply*` methods) is **deleted** from the hot `Column.php`; relation wrapping now has one owner (`Filter::applyToColumn()`); and the 7 inline header partials stay as a distinct UI surface but are repointed from `$column->getFilterX()` to `$filter->getX()` (view selection is polymorphic via `Filter::inlineView()`). Column filters are now **planned through the same `QueryPlanner`** as panel filters — each `Filter::toPlannerDefinitions()` emits a `FilterDefinition` (text → `LIKE` / comparison, select → `=`, multi-select → `IN`, number-range → `BETWEEN` with one-sided degradation), so join handling + column qualification are shared; the two types the planner can't express as one clause (date `whereDate`, boolean `= false OR IS NULL`) transparently fall back to the shared `Filter::apply()` via the existing `bypassesPlanner()` seam. **No public API is removed** — every `filterAs*` signature and behavior is preserved (header state still lives under `columnFilters.<name>`); a new `Column::filter(Filter)` / `getFilter()` is the canonical entry point for attaching a ready-made filter. Because header filters are now real `Filter` objects, they inherit for free: **authorization** (`->can()` / `->visible()` gate both render and apply), **indicator chips** (`getActiveColumnFilterIndicators()` / `removeColumnFilter()` render removable chips alongside panel-filter chips, sharing the new `filter-chip` partial), and **query-string persistence** (`Table::queryString()` tracks/seeds column filters under a `col_<column>` URL parameter — ranges use `col_<column>_min` / `_max`; dotted relation names are skipped as with panel filters). A new **`TextFilter`** (operator-aware substring/`LIKE` filter) is the engine behind `->filterable()` text filters and is also usable as a standalone panel filter. Covered by new `ColumnCanonicalFilterTest`, `CanonicalFilterPrimitivesTest`, `FilterInternalsCoverageTest`, extended `WithTableFilterIndicatorsTest` / `WithTableQueryStringTest`, and migrated `ColumnFilterCoverageTest` / `ColumnTest` — all four package suites green (table 1008 passing), PHPStan clean, and **every `packages/table/src/Filters/*` file at 100% line coverage**. Documented in [`docs/table/filters/column-level.md`](docs/table/filters/column-level.md), the new [`docs/table/filters/text.md`](docs/table/filters/text.md), [`docs/table/filters/index.md`](docs/table/filters/index.md) and [`docs/table/columns/editing.md`](docs/table/columns/editing.md) (EN + CS), the wire-boost guidelines, and an implementation plan under `architecture/plans/column-filter-canonical-unification.md`.

### Fixed
- **`FileUpload` uploads no longer get collapsed to a dead temp path when they pass through the table state container.** Root cause: `StateSerializer::serializeValue()` (used by `TableStateSynthesizer` to dehydrate the `StateContainer`) stringified any `Stringable` value — and `Illuminate\Http\UploadedFile` / Livewire's `TemporaryUploadedFile` are `Stringable` via `SplFileInfo::__toString()` (which returns the pathname). So a file living in action-modal form state (`modal.action.formData.*`) was cast to its raw `/tmp/phpXXX` path **before** Livewire's own file-upload dehydration ran, and Livewire's file synth never saw it — the upload was lost and a dead path persisted. `StateSerializer` now hands any `SplFileInfo` (file-upload object) straight through untouched so Livewire's `$dehydrateChild` handles it. Covered by a new `StateEngineTest` case (a file object is returned as-is, even nested deep in the state tree).
- **`FileUpload` no longer persists (or renders) a leaked raw upload path as if it were a stored file (defense-in-depth).** Even when a `FileUpload` value still reaches the save step as a bare **absolute** filesystem path — e.g. a raw PHP `$_FILES` tmp_name like `/tmp/phpA1B2C3` — `SaveHandler::storeFieldFiles()` used to treat any non-`UploadedFile` string as an already-stored path and write it straight to the model, so the DB ended up holding a dead temp path (the file is deleted at the end of its upload request). `FileUpload` now owns one canonical classifier, `isStoredReference()` — a legitimate stored value is a disk-relative path or a full URL, **never** an absolute filesystem path — used by `SaveHandler` (a leaked absolute path is now **rescued** onto the configured disk via the new `FileUpload::storeFileFromPath()` when the file still exists, or **dropped** when it is gone, instead of persisting the raw path), and by `FileUpload::getStoredFiles()` / `getFilePreviews()` (a leaked absolute path is never rendered as a stored file). Legitimate relative paths and URLs pass through unchanged. Covered by new `SaveHandlerTest` cases (gone temp path → null; existing path → rescued to a disk-relative path; multiple field filters the dead entry but keeps real paths) and `FieldTypesTest` cases (`isStoredReference` / `storeFileFromPath` / `getStoredFiles` / `getFilePreviews`).
- **Stacked modals: polished layering, a canonical z-index owner, and a depth cap (follow-up to the 1.10.1 stacking).** The z-index scale and safety cap now have one canonical owner — `Modals\ModalStack` (`BASE_Z_INDEX` / `Z_INDEX_STEP` / `MAX_DEPTH`, `zIndexForDepth()`) — instead of `50 + depth*10` hard-coded in Blade. Suspended parent shells now paint **no** backdrop of their own — the active modal draws a **single** full-viewport scrim — so stacking N levels no longer compounds into a near-black overlay; each suspended shell is `inert` + `aria-hidden` so keyboard and assistive tech skip it entirely; deeper parents progressively scale/lift for a "stacked-cards" depth cue; and opening is capped at `ModalStack::MAX_DEPTH` levels to bound pathological re-entrancy. Covered by a new `ModalStackTest` (z-index owner) plus `ModalStackingTest` / `ModalComponentRenderTest` cases (single scrim, inert shell, depth-cue transform, three-levels-deep pop-back, and the max-depth guard), and re-verified in a real browser via the `table-modal-nested` driver (single scrim, elevated z-index, `Escape` pops only the top).

## [1.10.1]

### Fixed
- **Opening an action from within an open modal no longer destroys the parent modal — modals now stack.** The action runtime was built around a **single** open modal at a time (one `mountedAction` bag in the standalone `WithActions` host, one `modal.action.*` slot in the table's `StateContainer`), so opening an action while a modal was already open **clobbered** the parent: its form state was silently discarded and it could never be returned to. Opening an action now **stacks** the new modal on top instead: the parent stays open, dimmed behind the new one, and closing the top modal (`Escape` / close button / backdrop / a footer action that closes) pops just that level and **resumes the parent with its form data intact** — the last close clears the stack. There is **no new public API**: any callback that receives the host `$component` (a `ModalFooterAction`, a field action, an infolist action) can call `$component->mountAction(...)` (standalone) or `$component->openActionModal(...)` / `openBulkActionModal()` / `openHeaderActionModal()` (table) and it just stacks; a footer action that opens a nested modal is **not** auto-closed afterwards (a depth guard skips the close when the callback pushed a level). Implemented as a **suspend-stack** that leaves the existing single active-slot machinery untouched (the active modal is the top of the stack) and adds a stack of *suspended* parent frames: new engine seams on `InteractsWithActions` (`suspendCurrentAction` / `resumeSuspendedAction` / `suspendedActionCount` / `getSuspendedActionModals` + `suspendActiveActionIfOpen()`, with a resume-aware `closeMountedAction()`), so **the form `statePath` / validation / binding layer is unchanged**. The standalone host stores suspended frames in `public array $suspendedActions` with records reduced to serializable `{class, key}` descriptors (re-resolved by key, since Livewire does not reliably serialize Eloquent models nested in arrays); the table host stores them under `modal.suspended` in the `StateContainer` (record re-resolved from `recordKey` via the query) and seeds it in `TableStateSchema::defaults`. Rendering draws each suspended parent as a dimmed, inert shell (`wire-core::modals.suspended`, `pointer-events-none`) behind the active modal, and the new `:z-index` prop on the `Modal` / `SlideOver` / `Confirmation` components layers each level by depth (`50 + depth*10`); only the top modal binds `Escape`/click-away. Covered by new `ModalStackingTest` suites (standalone forms host + table host: stack push, parent resume on close, per-level form-data preservation, suspended-modal exposure, footer-action-keeps-nested-open) and `ModalComponentRenderTest` cases (the `z-index` prop and the suspended-shell partial), with the full four-package suite green; verified in a real browser via a new `table-modal-nested` preview + `workbench/scripts/verify-nested-modal.mjs` CDP driver (nested modal on top, dimmed parent behind at the correct z-index, `Escape` pops only the top, parent form value preserved across the round-trip). Documented in [`docs/core/actions.md`](docs/core/actions.md) → "Stacked (Nested) Modals" (EN + CS) and the wire-boost guidelines.

## [1.10.0]

### Added
- **User-level browser-testing selectors + render-loop optimization for the table.** **Every** active part of the table now carries a stable `data-testid` (with an accessible name/role where the control is icon-only), so [Pest v4 Browser Testing](https://pestphp.com/docs/browser-testing) can target it at the user level instead of via brittle CSS — the search box, table-filters trigger + reset + active-filter chips, column picker, page-size selector, pagination (prev/next/numbered), sortable headers, per-column filter cells, body cells (+ `data-column`), inline-edit cells, rows (+ `data-row-key`, plus the mobile `table-card`), select-all / per-row / per-card checkboxes (`role="checkbox"` + `aria-label`), sub-row expanders (`aria-expanded`), the bulk bar + deselect, and **every** action surface: `action-{name}` (row), `header-action-{name}`, `bulk-action-{name}` and `menu-action-{name}` (context-menu / action-group items), all with `aria-label` (the core `Action` button, the table action view, the header/bulk action views and the dropdown-item view were all tagged, so form/standalone actions get it too). Actions and filter options stay reachable by visible text. Alongside this, the render hot loop was optimized: the column-static getters (`getAlignmentClass`, `getResponsiveClasses`, `shouldWrap`, `isEditable`, `hasResponsiveDisplay`) are now resolved **once per column** into a `$columnMeta` map reused by the header and every body cell, instead of being re-called for every cell (N rows × M columns → M). **The same convention was then rolled out across the whole shared UI** (audited in three parallel passes), so an end-to-end flow is mappable, not just the table: every wire-forms field gets a `form-field-{statePath}` container (+ `data-field`) plus per-type control hooks (`form-toggle-{path}`, `form-repeater-{path}-add|remove-{i}`, `form-file-{path}-remove-{i}`, `form-checklist-{path}-select-all`, `form-tags-{path}-remove-{i}`, `form-keyvalue-{path}-add`, `form-datetime-{path}-trigger`, `form-button-{path}`, field/hint actions); the canonical searchable combobox (forms Select + table filters) gets `select-trigger` / `select-search` / `select-option-{value}` / `select-clear`; modal chrome gets `modal-close` / `slide-over-close` / `modal-cancel` / `modal-submit` / `modal-back` / `modal-next` / `confirmation-confirm` / `confirmation-cancel` / `modal-footer-action-{name}`; layout gets `wizard-step-{i}` / `wizard-back` / `wizard-next` / `tab-{i}` / `section-toggle` / `callout-dismiss`; toasts get `toast-dismiss` / `toast-action-{i}` / `toast-expand`; infolist actions get `infolist-action-{name}`; and the sortable drag handle gets `sortable-handle` (`role="button"` + `aria-label`). The rollout goes all the way down to the **granular micro-controls**, following the convention `form-{type}-{statePath}` with sub-controls appending `-{action|value|index}`: the date-time picker's month nav / calendar days / hour-minute-second steppers / clear / done (`form-datetime-{path}-…`), colour-picker swatches (`form-color-{path}-swatch-{color}`), rating stars (`form-rating-{path}-star-{n}`), OTP digit boxes (`form-otp-{path}-{i}`), individual radio / checkbox / checkbox-list options (`form-radio-{path}-{value}`, `form-checkbox-{path}`, `form-checklist-{path}-{value}`), sliders (`form-slider-{path}`), and the markdown/rich/tiptap editor toolbar buttons + tabs + body (`form-editor-{path}-…`) — each carries a `data-testid` (dynamic ones bound per value/index) so even a single star or calendar day is addressable. The full selector reference (all surfaces + the naming convention) is tabulated in the docs. Covered by a `TableTestHooksTest` and a new `FormTestHooksTest` (asserting field containers, per-type controls AND the micro-controls render), with all four package suites (core/forms/table/sortable) green, plus a real-browser check that every hook is queryable in the live previews; documented in [`docs/table/advanced.md`](docs/table/advanced.md) → "Browser Testing Selectors" (EN + CS) with a ready Pest Browser example.
- **Multi-select column filter — `Column::filterAsMultiSelect()`.** Per-column header filters covered text, single-select, date, date-range, number-range and boolean, but there was no way to filter a column by **several** values at once — even though `applyFilterCondition()` already did a `whereIn` for array values, no API or UI exposed it. `filterAsMultiSelect(array|string $options, ?string $placeholder)` adds it, matching **any** of the chosen values (`whereIn`); an empty selection constrains nothing. Options accept an array or an enum class (shared `EnumResolver`). Because Livewire only treats header checkboxes as an array group when the bound value is already an array, `mountWithTable()` now **seeds each multi-select column filter's `columnFilters.<name>` state to `[]`** — without which the second pick would replace the first instead of adding (a bug caught only in the browser). Covered by new `ColumnFilterCoverageTest` cases (config, `whereIn` apply + empty-selection skip, checkbox render) and a `WithTableMultiSelectFilterTest` (mount seeds `[]`, `whereIn` row filtering, empty = all rows), plus a real-browser CDP run (open the dropdown, tick two roles → table narrows via `whereIn` with the panel staying open across the live roundtrip, count in the trigger, clearing restores all rows); a live demo lives at `/previews/table-column-filters`, documented in [`docs/table/columns/editing.md`](docs/table/columns/editing.md) (EN + CS). Both `filterAsSelect` and `filterAsMultiSelect` now render the **canonical searchable combobox** (`wire-core::partials.searchable-select`, the same owner used by the wire-forms `Select` and the table `SelectFilter`) rather than a native `<select>` / bespoke dropdown, so a column select filter gains an in-panel **search** for free (on by default; `Column::filterSearchable(false)` opts out) and looks identical to a form Select. The rest of the header-filter controls (text, boolean, date, number-range) were unified onto one canonical style owner — **`Support\FilterControl::classes()`** — that mirrors the **wire-forms field look** (same border, shadow, background, text size, hover and focus ring as `TextInput`), so the whole filter row reads like the rest of the form UI instead of a mix of native and custom widgets. (Multi-select column filters seed their `columnFilters.<name>` state to `[]` on mount so the combobox's array entangle binds cleanly.)
- **Right-click row context menu — `Table::rowContextMenu([...actions])`.** Power users can now right-click a row to open a menu of actions pinned at the cursor, a shortcut alongside the actions column. The menu's actions are declared **separately** from `->actions()` — an explicit dedicated list, not an implicit mirror of the row toolbar (pass the same objects to match) — and render through the exact menu-item markup of the action-group dropdown (`Action::renderForDropdown()`), so only **visible/authorized** actions appear, action groups are flattened in, and a row with no visible action renders no menu. A new canonical **`wireContextMenu`** Alpine factory (shipped in the pre-built `wire-core-dropdown.js` bundle, loaded via the existing `@assets` floating-assets `<script src>`) pins a teleported `position: fixed` panel at the pointer and clamps it inside the viewport; a module-level guard keeps **only one menu open at a time** (right-clicking another row closes the previous); it closes on outside-click, `Escape`, scroll, or after choosing an action (which runs normally, e.g. opening its modal). The per-row `<template x-teleport>` is a valid script-supporting child of `<tr>`, so no markup is duplicated. Desktop pointer feature (touch devices have no context menu — the actions column remains). Covered by a new `RowContextMenuTest` (dedicated-actions gating, separation from the toolbar actions, menu-HTML incl. hidden-action omission, action-group flattening, empty-when-unconfigured, and render wiring on/off) plus a `DropdownAssetTest` bundle-registration assertion and a real-browser CDP run (right-click opens the dedicated actions at the cursor, single-open across rows, viewport-clamped, choosing/Escape closes, no JS errors); documented in [`docs/table/advanced.md`](docs/table/advanced.md) (EN + CS).
- **Per-user column layout memory — `Table::rememberColumns()`, driver-based.** Toggling columns previously lived only for the component's lifetime (a full page reload reset the layout), so a user's chosen columns never stuck. `Table::rememberColumns('key')` now loads the current user's saved hidden-column set on mount and persists it on every toggle, scoped to `auth()->user()` — **one key serves every user**, so it scales to any number of tables (distinct keys) and users. Where it is stored is a pluggable **`TablePreferenceDriver`** selected in `config('wire-table.preferences')`: `null` (the default — no persistence), `session` (no migration), or `database` (a `table_preferences` row per user+table; publish `--tag="wire-table::migrations"` and migrate — the migration is publish-only, never auto-run). A `TablePreferenceManager` resolves the driver (per-table `Table::preferenceDriver()` override > global default, with a separate `guest` driver so a DB-backed default still falls back to per-session memory for visitors); a stored column that no longer exists is dropped on load, and a "Reset columns" control in the picker clears the saved layout (`resetColumns()`). Covered by a new `TablePreferencesTest` (18 cases: fluent API, manager resolution incl. guest/override/swap/unknown-alias fallback, all three drivers' load/save/forget + scoping, mount-load over defaults, stale-column pruning, toggle-persist, reset-forget, a fresh-mount round-trip, and the reset control's render gating), plus a real-browser CDP check of the toggle persisting across a page reload and the reset restoring defaults; documented in [`docs/table/advanced.md`](docs/table/advanced.md) (EN + CS).
- **Conditional whole-row coloring — `Table::rowColor()` and a Closure-aware `Table::rowClass()`.** Painting a row by a per-record condition previously meant hand-writing background utilities in a `rowClass()` Closure — except `rowClass()` only accepted a static `string` and `getRowClass()` never invoked a Closure, so the documented dynamic-class example did not actually work. `Table::rowColor(string|Closure|null)` now tints an entire row with a semantic role or raw Tailwind hue (`danger`, `warning`, `success`, `primary`, `gray`, … or any hue), resolved through a new canonical **`HasColor::getRowTintClasses()`** owner — the lightest `-50`/translucent-dark tint with a matching same-hue hover, so a tinted row reads cleanly and drops the neutral hover + zebra striping it would clash with (return `null` from the Closure for no tint). `Table::rowClass(string|Closure|null)` was widened so its Closure actually resolves per record (the record is passed in), for arbitrary utilities (font weight, ring, opacity) layered on top of a tint. Both compose into one `Table::getRowClasses($record, $rowIndex)` consumed by the row view, keeping the Blade free of layered conditionals. Covered by new `TableTest` cases (static + Closure resolution, empty-string = no tint, tint suppresses hover/striping, tint + custom class, unknown-color gray fallback) and `HasColorTest` (row tint across the full palette), plus a real-browser CDP check of the rendered tints; a live demo lives at `/previews/table-row-color`, documented in [`docs/table/overview.md`](docs/table/overview.md) (EN + CS).
- **`BarChartWidget::verticalLabels()` — rotate each bar's label vertically beside it.** Long category labels (e.g. package names) truncated or overflowed under/above the bars. `verticalLabels()` moves each bar's label out of the header/caption to a rotated caption (reading bottom-to-top) beside its bar, so long names fit; the value/percentage stays in place. Opt-in and vertical-charts only (finance + system); the horizontal layout is unchanged. Covered by `BarChartWidgetTest` (default off / toggle + rotated render on both vertical variants + horizontal-by-default); documented in [`docs/core/widgets.md`](docs/core/widgets.md) (EN + CS).
- **Named repeater items — `Repeater::itemLabel()`.** Repeater item blocks were headed only by their number (`#1`, `#2`), which makes a collapsed list of items hard to tell apart. `itemLabel(string|Closure|null)` shows a name next to the number, either static or derived from the item's state via a closure (`fn(array $state, int $index): ?string`) — e.g. `->itemLabel(fn ($state) => $state['name'] ?? null)` renders "#1 Ada". The label re-renders on the item's reactive cycle, so pairing it with a `->live()` field updates it as the user types. Covered by `RepeaterTest` (static/closure/null resolution) and a `RepeaterReactivityTest` render case; documented in [`docs/forms/fields/repeater.md`](docs/forms/fields/repeater.md) (EN + CS).
- **`FileUpload` now actually stores uploads — orphan-free, on submit — and merges them with existing files.** Uploads previously never reached the server: the field only client-previewed the picked `File` via Alpine, and no host composed Livewire's file-upload machinery, so nothing was ever persisted. `InteractsWithFileUploads` now composes `Livewire\WithFileUploads` (so `wire:model` file inputs upload for real) and, via the `updated`/`updating` lifecycle hooks, folds each finished upload into the field state as a **pending** `TemporaryUploadedFile` — a **multiple** field appends to whatever was already there (existing paths + earlier pending uploads, captured in `updating`), a **single** field keeps the newest. Files are moved to permanent storage only on **save**: a new `SaveHandler` step walks the schema, and for each `FileUpload` calls `FileUpload::storeUploadedFile()` (honouring `disk()`/`directory()`/`visibility()`/`preserveFilenames()`), dehydrating a single field to one path and a multiple field to an array while keeping already-stored paths — so an abandoned form leaves nothing behind (the temporary upload expires on its own). The field is resolved from the changed state path via `Form::findComponentByStatePath()`, so it works in standalone form hosts and table/form action modals alike; the Blade view renders both stored paths and pending uploads (image previews via disk URL / `temporaryUrl()`, a "Pending upload" label, and index-based removal). Covered by `FieldTypesTest` (`storeUploadedFile` + `preserveFilenames`), `WithFormsTraitTest` (merge stays pending; index removal), and `SaveHandlerTest` (single store, multiple merge, no-upload → null) with `Storage::fake`, plus a real-browser CDP upload round-trip.
  - **`FileUpload` now displays already-stored files, with a per-file remove control.** The field's Alpine view only tracked freshly-selected `File` objects, so editing a record that already had a stored path (or an array of paths) showed *nothing* — the existing upload was invisible and un-removable. `FileUpload::getStoredFiles($state)` now turns the bound state into displayable descriptors (`path`/`url`/`name`/`isImage`), skipping transient `TemporaryUploadedFile` instances and empties and resolving each path to a URL through the configured `disk()` (a value that is already a full URL passes through) — reusing the `ImageColumn` resolution pattern. The Blade view renders those existing files below the drop zone (thumbnail for images, document icon otherwise, a link, and an `×`), separate from the client-side preview of new uploads. Removal is backed by a new canonical host trait **`InteractsWithFileUploads::removeUploadedFile($statePath, $path)`** — composed into every form host (`WithForms`, form/table action modals via `WithActions`/`WithTable`), mirroring `InteractsWithRepeaters` and writing through `StateContainer::writeInto()` so it works inside table action modals too — which drops the path from form state (the physical file is left untouched); `->deletable(false)` renders stored files read-only. Covered by new `FieldTypesTest` cases (deletable toggle, array/single/skip/full-URL descriptors) and `WithFormsTraitTest` host cases (remove from a multiple field, clear a single string path, ignore an absent path), plus a real-browser CDP check of the render + remove round-trip; documented in [`docs/forms/fields/file-upload.md`](docs/forms/fields/file-upload.md) (EN + CS).
  - **Toast notifications gained a countdown bar, action buttons, persistence, a collapsible stack, and a max-visible cap.** `<x-wire-notifications::toast-container />` now renders a per-toast **countdown bar** that depletes toward auto-dismiss and **pauses (with the timer) on hover** — driven by a single `requestAnimationFrame` loop rather than per-toast `setTimeout`s. New container props: `progress` (bar on/off, default on), `stack` (collapse toasts into a pile that fans out on hover; direction from `topAnchored()`), and `max` (cap visible toasts, overflow into a clickable "+N more" pill). The container is now an `aria-live="polite"` region (error toasts `role="alert"`) and honors `prefers-reduced-motion` (no collapse/fan-out, transitions off). `Notification` gained `->persistent()` (sticky: `duration 0`, no bar) and `->action('Undo', 'event')` / `->action(NotificationAction::make(...))` / `->actions([...])`; the new immutable **`NotificationAction`** value object (`Notifications\NotificationAction`: `make(label, event)`, `->payload()`, `->color()`, `->keepOpen()`) renders a toast button that runs `Livewire.dispatch(event, payload)` on click, which the host listens for with `#[On(event)]`. Covered by new `NotificationTest`/`CurrentComponentDriverTest` cases and a real-browser CDP check; a live demo lives at `/previews/core-toasts`, documented in [`docs/core/notifications.md`](docs/core/notifications.md) (EN + CS).

### Fixed
- **Editable `ToggleColumn`/`SelectColumn` cells now update optimistically, and a per-record disabled cell can no longer be written by a forged request.** `updateTableCell()` calls `skipRender()` so the table is not re-rendered (a DOM morph destroys Alpine cell state), which requires each editable cell to switch its own appearance client-side. `TextInputColumn` did this; `ToggleColumn` was a plain `wire:click` and `SelectColumn` a plain `wire:change`, so a toggle's visual only caught up after a reload/poll and neither participated in optimistic-locking or error rollback. All three inline-editable cells now share one canonical Alpine component — **`wireEditableCell`** (shipped in the pre-built `wire-core-dropdown.js` bundle, loaded via the existing `@assets` floating-assets `<script src>` so it survives Livewire morphs and strict CSP) — which flips the cell optimistically, calls `$wire.updateTableCell(...)` with the row's version, rolls back to the last server-confirmed value on failure, adopts the server's value on an optimistic-lock conflict and surfaces the conflict/error message **inline on the cell itself** (a red state on toggle/select/text — no toast or `NotificationManager` setup required; opt in to *also* raise a toast for conflicts with `Table::notifyEditConflicts()`), and reconciles via a `MutationObserver` on `data-server-value`/`data-record-version` when polling refreshes the row. `TextInputColumn`'s editable cell was migrated onto the same base (its live validation / save-on-blur/enter / escape-to-revert now layer on top). **Security:** `ToggleColumn` and `SelectColumn` gained a server-side `canEdit(Model $record)` guard (consulted by `updateTableCell`) so a per-record `disabled()` cell is rejected on the server, not just hidden client-side — record identity is passed via `data-*` attributes rather than interpolated into JS. Covered by a new `WithTableInteractionsTest` case (forged edit to a disabled toggle/select is rejected and does not write; an enabled record still saves), an updated `ColumnPartialRenderTest`, a `DropdownAssetTest` bundle-registration assertion, and a real-browser CDP run (optimistic flip before the server responds, save confirmation, conflict rollback, no JS errors).

### Changed
- **Notifications resolve the active Livewire component themselves — no more threading `$this`, and the default driver carries the full payload.** Producers used to pass the component into every send (`NotificationManager::send($n, $driver, $this)`) so a driver could `dispatch()` a browser event; forget it and the live toast silently never fired. The built-in default is now **`CurrentComponentDriver`** — a decorator that resolves the currently rendering component via `Livewire::current()` and delegates to a wrapped `SessionDriver` — so `NotificationManager::send($n)`, `InteractsWithNotifications::notify()`, `WithTable::sendNotification()`, and `InteractsWithActions::sendActionNotification()` all drop the `$this` argument. `SessionDriver` was also changed to dispatch the **full** `Notification::toArray()` payload (previously only `type`+`message`), so titles, durations, and the new toast actions survive the server round-trip without switching to `LivewireEventDriver`. Fully backwards compatible for callers (the `$livewireComponent` argument still exists and wins when passed); a custom component-dependent driver used directly can be wrapped in `CurrentComponentDriver` for the same auto-resolution. See [ADR 0004](architecture/decisions/0004-notification-driver-defaults.md); covered by `Livewire::test(...)->assertDispatched(...)` cases across core/table/forms.

## [1.9.1]

### Added
- **Forms self-seed their initial state from the schema — `->default()` now actually applies, and array fields never collapse.** A form field's `->default()` was settable (inherited from `Foundation\Concerns\HasDefault`) but nothing consumed it, so the documented `TextInput::make('status')->default('draft')` silently did nothing; worse, array-backed fields (`CheckboxList`, `Tags`, `KeyValue`, multi-`Select`) whose key was absent from the fill data started as a missing/null value, which makes Livewire collapse grouped array inputs into one shared value (checking one checks all) — the reason action forms had to hand-list every field in `fillFormUsing`. Fields now declare a type-correct **blank** via the new `Field::getBlankState()` (derived from `getStateType()`: `array → []`, `bool → false`, else `null`), and `Form`/`FormRuntime` expose `getInitialState()` — a schema walk seeding a key for every field (and top-level `Repeater`) with its `->default()` when set, otherwise its blank. `Form::fill()` now layers this seed **under** the incoming data with a union merge, so the seed only fills genuinely-absent keys: defaults apply in create mode and to new/virtual fields, and a record's persisted value — **even an intentional `null`** — is never overwritten. Action modals route through the same canonical seed: `HasModal::getFormDefaults()` returns the form's `getInitialState()` with `fillFormUsing` overrides on top (via a new side-effect-free `resolveFormForSeeding()` that never mutates the instance that later renders), so the old "run `fillFormUsing` even without context so `CheckboxList` gets an array" workaround is gone — array fields are seeded structurally whether or not `fillFormUsing` is set. Covered by new `FormRuntimeTest` cases (blank/default/array seeding, layout descent + top-level repeater seeding, create-mode fill, intentional-null preservation, caller-wins) and `HasModalTest` cases (schema seed with/without `fillFormUsing`, closure/array/no-form resolution); documented in [`docs/forms/fields/index.md`](docs/forms/fields/index.md) (EN + CS).

### Fixed
- **Teleported floating panels no longer flash open on page load.** Every floating dropdown is rendered inside `<template x-teleport="body">` and hidden with `x-show` + `x-cloak`. Because the panel is cloned into `<body>` and made visible *before* Alpine evaluates `x-show` (and the global `[x-cloak]{display:none}` rule does not reliably cover freshly teleported nodes), the open panel briefly painted in the top-left corner on load / Livewire re-render before snapping shut. Each panel now also carries an inline `style="display: none;"` — which the browser honors immediately regardless of CSS/JS timing, and which Alpine's `x-show` overrides the moment the panel should open — closing the flash. Applied to the generic `<x-wire::dropdown>` (`wire-core::foundation.dropdown`), the action-group menu, the bar-chart widget menu and the table column-toggle + filter panels; and now **also** to the three panels the first pass missed — the shared searchable-select (`wire-core::partials.searchable-select`, so `Select` / `BelongsToSelect` / table `SelectFilter`), the forms `Tags` suggestion list and the `DateTimePicker` calendar — which previously had no `x-cloak` on the panel at all and so were the most exposed. The same guard is applied to the teleported **modal / slide-over / confirmation** overlays (`wire-core::modals.*`), whose root `x-show`/`x-cloak` wrapper (backdrop + panel) could flash the same way — hiding the root keeps every child inert until Alpine opens it. Purely presentational; no behavioral or API change.

## [1.9.0]

### Added
- **Optimistic locking for forms — `Form::optimisticLock()`.** Concurrent edits could silently overwrite each other: two users open the same record, both save, and the second save clobbers the first with no warning — the worst class of data loss in ERP/MES workflows. `Form::optimisticLock(string $column = 'updated_at')` opts a form into a version check: the lock column's raw value is captured when the form is filled from the record and carried through the Livewire round trip, and on save the **authoritative current database value is re-read**; if it no longer matches the captured baseline (someone else saved — or deleted — the record in the meantime) the save is aborted with a `WireForms\Forms\Runtime\StaleModelException` plus a conflict notification (`wire-forms::messages.stale`) instead of overwriting the newer data. Works with a timestamp or any integer/version column; opt-in and fully backwards compatible (no lock unless called). The guard runs only in update mode and fails open when no baseline was captured (e.g. `->model()` set after `->fill()` — set the model first). Covered by `OptimisticLockTest` (happy path, concurrent-change conflict, opt-in control, custom version column, fail-open, bound/unbound conflict notification).
- **Per-record cell visibility — `Column::visibleForRecord()`.** Column visibility (`->hidden()` / `->visible()` / `->authorize()` / `->permission()`) is *structural* — evaluated once, without a record, deciding whether the column exists in the table at all (it also drives the header, column toggle and export, and is memoized once per parent for sub-rows). To hide or **redact a single cell per row** — e.g. show `salary`/`margin` only on records the user may see — the base `Column` gains `->visibleForRecord(fn ($record) => bool)`, consulted at cell render *alongside* the structural check: a denied cell renders empty while the column keeps its place in every other row. It is deliberately a separate closure rather than folded into `canView()`/`authorizeUsing()`, which would misfire structurally with a null record. Wired across the whole column family (text, badge, boolean, icon, image, poll, select, toggle, text-input, split incl. its child columns, and stacked — which also gained the structural `canView()` guard it was missing). `ButtonColumn::visibleWhen()` becomes a backwards-compatible alias for it (one canonical owner). Covered by `ColumnVisibleForRecordTest`; documented in [`docs/table/columns/index.md`](docs/table/columns/index.md).

### Fixed
- **A remotely-searched combobox selection keeps its label after being picked.** In remote-search mode (`Select::getSearchResultsUsing()` / `BelongsToSelect`), choosing an option went blank: the shared `wire-core::partials.searchable-select` reset `search` to `''` on select, which fired the `search` watcher → `fetchRemote('')` → the server returned nothing for the empty term, so `options` was rebuilt from `initialOptions` alone. Because the just-chosen option existed only in the transient remote results (and Alpine never re-reads the render-time `x-data` seed across a Livewire morph — the same constraint behind `upsertOption`), `selectedLabel`/`isSelected`, which read purely from `options`, lost the choice and the trigger fell back to the placeholder — most visibly for a value picked in a fresh create/action modal (an existing record's label survives because the view seeds it via `getSelectedOptionLabels()`). `select()` now persists the chosen option into `initialOptions` **before** resetting the search (new `persistSelectedOption()` helper, mirroring `upsertOption` and the server-side seed), in both the single branch and when a value is *added* in the multiple branch — so the label survives the post-select `fetchRemote('')` and every subsequent remote search. One canonical owner, so the base `Select`, `BelongsToSelect` and the table `SelectFilter` all inherit the fix; non-remote selects are unaffected (no `search` watcher, `options` is never rebuilt). Covered by `SelectTest` (helper present, writes into `initialOptions`, and runs before the search reset in both branches).

### Changed
- **`authorizeUsing()` callbacks now receive the row's record.** The shared `Foundation\Concerns\HasAuthorization` custom-authorization callback was passed only the authenticated user, so `->authorizeUsing(fn ($user) => …)` could not gate per record — even though row actions already carry one. `isAuthorized($context)` now forwards the record to the callback: `->authorizeUsing(fn ($user, $record) => $user->id === $record?->owner_id)`, enabling per-record **action** authorization out of the box (approve only your own records, guard a transition only for the owner). Fully backwards compatible — existing one-argument closures keep working (PHP ignores the extra argument), and record-less surfaces (structural column/filter visibility, fields, widgets) pass `null`. Covered by `HasAuthorizationTest` (per-record forwarding + one-arg backward compatibility). This intentionally does **not** turn structural `Column::canView()` per-record; for per-cell redaction use `->visibleForRecord()` above.
- **`Form::using()` + relationship repeaters — documented behavior, regression-tested.** A custom `using()` callback receives the full data array **including relationship `Repeater` arrays** (e.g. a `children` key), which the default persistence path strips before writing the parent — so a naive `Model::create($data)` inside `using()` would try to mass-assign them. The [save lifecycle guide](docs/forms/save-lifecycle.md) now spells this out: assign only the parent's own attributes, return the persisted `Model` and the Step-6 relationship cascade still runs for you, or return a non-`Model` (an id/DTO/command result) and the cascade is skipped so your callback owns persistence entirely. No behavior change — the cascade already ran for any `Model` result; locked by two `SaveHandlerTest` regression tests (`using()`→Model cascades children; `using()`→non-Model skips).

## [1.8.1]

### Changed
- **The `Split` schema layout is renamed to `Flex`.** The side-by-side layout added in 1.8.0 is implemented purely as a flexbox row (`flex-row` with `justify`/`align`/`gap`/`wrap`/`grow`), so its name now matches what it does — and stops colliding conceptually with a proportional/resizable "split". `WireCore\Foundation\Schema\Split` → `Flex`, the standalone tag `<x-wire::split>` → `<x-wire::flex>` (auto-registered from `Foundation\View\Flex`), and the views `wire-core::schema.split`/`wire-core::foundation.split` → `.flex`. Fixed-ratio column division stays owned by `Grid` + `columnSpan`; the unrelated table `SplitColumn` (which splits space *within a single cell*) is unchanged. **Breaking:** update `Split::make()` → `Flex::make()` and `<x-wire::split>` → `<x-wire::flex>`. Docs (`docs/core/schema/layout/flex.md`, foundation/infolists/forms/schema overviews), the wire-core boost guideline and `TypeCatalog` (short name `split` → `flex`) track it; covered by the renamed `FlexTest`, `InfolistTest`, `LayoutTagsTest` and boost `TypeCatalogTest`.
- **`->color()` accepts the complete Tailwind palette on every surface.** The canonical `Foundation\Concerns\HasColor` resolvers had drifted to different-width palettes: badges and text tints accepted a handful of raw hues, but the button/link/modal/choice surfaces only knew the semantic set (`primary`/`success`/`danger`/`warning`/`info`/`gray`), so `->color('purple')` produced a purple badge next to a gray-fallback solid button. Every owner-facing resolver — `getSolidColorClasses()`, `getOutlinedColorClasses()`, `getGhostColorClasses()`, `getIconButtonColorClasses()`, `getLinkColorClasses()`, `getSolidBgClass()`/`getSoftBgClass()`, `getBadgeColorClasses()`, `getTextColorClasses()`, `getChoiceColorClasses()`, `getModalSubmitButtonClasses()`, `getModalIconBgClass()`/`getModalIconTextClass()` and the chart `getGradientFillClasses()`/`getFillTextClasses()` — now resolves the full palette: the semantic roles (with aliases `blue`→primary, `green`/`emerald`→emerald, `yellow`/`amber`→amber, `cyan`→cyan, `secondary`→gray) **plus** every raw Tailwind hue family (`slate`, `zinc`, `neutral`, `stone`, `orange`, `lime`, `teal`, `sky`, `indigo`, `violet`, `purple`, `fuchsia`, `pink`, `rose`), so one color value renders identically on a badge, a solid/outlined/link button, a modal, a choice card and a chart bar. Class strings stay literal (never interpolated from the owner-supplied name) so Tailwind's JIT scanner sees every hue and the maps double as a safe allow-list; the chart hues are additionally mirrored in `bar-chart/safelist.blade.php` (guarded by `BarChartSafelistTest`) for consumers that scan only package views. The type-safe `Foundation\Colors\Color` enum gained a case for every raw hue family (`Color::Teal`, `Color::Rose`, …) and its `resolve()` maps the semantic aliases. Docs (`docs/core/foundation.md` **Colors** section rewritten from "7 semantic color names" to the full palette + resolver table) and the wire-core boost guideline track it, and a new **Full Color Palette** preview (`/previews/palette`, wired into the docs-site capture pipeline) shows every hue across surfaces. Covered by parametrized `HasColorTest` cases over the whole palette (a test double exposes the protected button resolvers) and the extended `BarChartSafelistTest`.
- **Type-safe value enums for every fluent token — `Breakpoint`, `Size`, `FontWeight`, `Alignment`, `IconPosition`, `Placement`, `ModalWidth`.** Following the canonical `Foundation\Colors\Color` pattern, seven scattered "magic string" vocabularies are promoted to canonical enums in `Foundation\Enums\`, each the single owner of its tokens (`values()` + `resolve()` with a sensible default). Every owner-facing setter that took a string now accepts `string|Enum` and is **fully backwards compatible** — `->size('lg')` and `->size(Size::Lg)` are interchangeable: `Breakpoint` (`sm`…`2xl`) on column `visibleFrom()`/`hiddenFrom()`/`mobileBreakpoint()`, `Table::stackedOnMobile()`, the mobile-sheet/modal `mobileBreakpoint()` and `ResponsiveGrid`; `Size` (`xs`…`xl`) on every `size()`/`sm()`/`md()` surface (actions, buttons, `ImageColumn`, badge/icon columns); `FontWeight` (`thin`…`black`) on column `weight()` and `TextEntry::weight()`; `Alignment` (`left`/`center`/`right`) on column `alignment()` and `Table::actionsAlignment()`; `IconPosition` (`before`/`after`) on `->icon($icon, $position)` across actions/buttons/fields; `Placement` (`{bottom,top}-{start,end}`) on `ActionGroup::dropdownPosition()`; and `ModalWidth` (`sm`…`7xl`/`full`) on modal/slide-over/action-modal `width()`/`modalWidth()`. This consolidates the duplicated `match` maps into one owner per concern — the column/table/grid breakpoint maps, `HasResponsive`, `ActionGroup`'s three dropdown-placement maps, and the modal-width resolver all now delegate — and the `Breakpoint`/`Alignment`/`Placement` enums own the **literal** Tailwind class each token maps to (`tableCellClass()`, `textClass()`/`justifyClass()`, `panelClasses()`/`originClass()`), so the table Blade views consume a scannable utility instead of interpolating `text-{$align}` / `{$breakpoint}:hidden` (a latent Tailwind-JIT gap, now closed in `index`, `group-subtotal`, `summary-footer` and the responsive-cell partials). Docs (`docs/core/foundation.md` gains a **Type-safe value enums** table) and the wire-core boost guideline track it. Covered by dedicated `Foundation/Enums/*Test` suites (full case + resolver coverage) plus enum-acceptance cases threaded through `FoundationTraitsTest`, `ColumnConfigCoverageTest`, `TableTest`, `ActionGroupTest`, `MobileSheetTest` and `ModalTest`; PHPStan surfaced and fixed the contravariance where a downstream override (`forms\Components\Button::size()`/`icon()`) had to widen to match the widened trait.


## [1.8.0]

### Added
- **Infolist actions — interactive buttons on section headers, entries, and repeatable rows.** A read-only infolist can now carry [`Action`](docs/core/actions.md) buttons built from the same fluent API as table/modal actions, wired through the canonical field-action contract instead of a parallel mini-API: a new shared `WireCore\Foundation\Concerns\HasActions` (the canonical owner of "a display component carries actions") stores the list and implements `Foundation\Contracts\HasFieldActions`, so infolist actions share one resolution vocabulary with form-field affix actions. `Section::headerActions([...])` renders actions in the section header (receiving the bound record); `Entry::actions([...])` renders them below an entry value (receiving the record and the entry's `$state`); and `RepeatableEntry::actions([...])` renders **per row**, invoked with that row's item as `$record`/`$state`. Dispatch lives in the core action runtime next to `callModalFooterAction()` — `InteractsWithActions::callInfolistAction(string $actionName, ?int $rowKey = null)` re-resolves every infolist the host exposes (`infolistsForActions()`, defaulting to the action-modal infolist and overridable), locates the action-bearing component by name via a depth-first `findInfolistAction()`, and invokes its callback through the shared `invokeActionCallback()` payload resolver; for a repeatable row the record is the item at `$rowKey` (rows are re-indexed from zero by the new `RepeatableEntry::getRowItems()`, out-of-range is a safe no-op). Rendered by the shared `wire-core::partials.component-action` button (optional `rowKey`) plus `wire-core::infolists.entry-actions`; because infolists are stateless and re-resolved per request, the button carries only the action name (and row index). Actions dispatch on any host composing the action runtime — an action modal (`WithTable` / `WithActions`) out of the box. Covered by `HasActionsTest`-style cases across `EntriesTest` / `InfolistTest` (storage, hidden filtering, name resolution, header/entry/row rendering wired to `callInfolistAction`) and `WithActionsTest` (entry, header, and per-row dispatch end-to-end through Livewire, including out-of-range and callback-less no-ops).
- **Three new infolist entries — `BadgeEntry`, `BooleanEntry`, `ListEntry`.** `BadgeEntry` and `BooleanEntry` are thin first-class presets over the canonical entries (a `TextEntry` with `badge()` on, and an `IconEntry` with `boolean()` on), so the badge / boolean chrome stays owned in one place while `BadgeEntry::make('status')` / `BooleanEntry::make('is_verified')` read as intent. `ListEntry` renders a collection state as a bulleted list or a row of badge chips — the middle ground between a single `TextEntry` and a full `RepeatableEntry`: `separator()` splits a delimited string into items, `limitList(n)` caps the visible items with a `+N` overflow indicator, `badge()` switches to chips, and items reuse the `TextEntry` formatting (`formatScalar` was promoted to `protected` for the subclass). Rendered by the new `wire-core::infolists.entries.list` view. Covered by `EntriesTest` and `InfolistTest` (preset defaults + view names, item resolution from arrays/separators, empty-state and overflow, chip vs bullet rendering).
- **`Split` schema layout.** A new canonical layout in `WireCore\Foundation\Schema` (alongside `Grid`/`Section`/`Fieldset`, reusable by forms) that arranges its children side by side on one horizontal axis and stacks them vertically on small screens: `Split::make()->from('lg')->schema([...])`, where `from()` sets the breakpoint (`sm`/`md`/`lg`, default `md`) at which the row turns horizontal. Children grow to share the row evenly. Rendered by `wire-core::schema.split`; covered by `InfolistTest` (side-by-side rendering, record propagation, custom breakpoint, invisible-child skipping).
- **`RepeatableEntry::with()` — N+1 hardening for relation rows.** When repeatable rows are Eloquent models whose child entries read a **nested** relation path (e.g. `product.name` per order line), that path lazily loaded the relation once per row. `RepeatableEntry::make('lines')->with(['product', 'tax'])` now eager-loads the declared relations across every model row in a single query per relation before rendering — `getRowItems()` filters the model rows and runs `loadMissing()` on a temporary `Eloquent\Collection`, which populates the very instances the rows render from (a no-op for array rows; repeated `with()` calls merge). This mirrors the table's own `WithTable::eagerLoadSubRows()` collection-load pattern, kept in core with no table dependency. Covered by `RepeatableEagerLoadTest`, which proves via the query log that `with()` collapses three lazy per-row loads into one.
- **Standalone actions — declare and fully run modal/slide-over/wizard/confirmation/form actions in any Livewire component, no table required.** Previously the whole action runtime (opening a modal, confirmation, hosting a reactive form, the before → callback → after → halt → notification → redirect lifecycle) lived only inside `WithTable`, so `<x-wire-actions::button :action="$action" />` outside a table was purely presentational. The new host trait `WireForms\Concerns\WithActions` makes any component a first-class action host: declare named actions in `actions(): array`, render them with `<x-wire-actions::button :action="$this->fooAction()" />` (the button now auto-derives `wire:click="mountAction('foo')"` when no handler is given, and skips rendering a hidden action), and drop `<x-wire-actions::modal-host :component="$this" />` once to render the mounted action's modal/slide-over/wizard/confirmation + form + footer. The trait adds `mountAction($name, ['record' => $model])` (opens the modal, or runs a plain action immediately, optionally record-scoped), `callMountedAction()` (validate + run), `unmountAction()`, `nextActionModalStep()`/`prevActionModalStep()`, and `callModalFooterAction()`; the modal form binds to the public `actionModalFormData` property, so `fillFormUsing`, field actions and `createOptionForm` behave exactly as in a table action modal. It lives in `wire-forms` because a form-capable host has to compose the wire-forms field concerns — the same engine backs `WithTable`. New `Actions\View\ModalHostComponent` (+ `wire-core::actions.modal-host` and `partials/modal-host-*`). Covered by `WithActionsTest` end-to-end through Livewire (mount/submit/validation, confirmation, halt, record scoping, multi-step wizard, footer + field actions, auto-`mountAction` render) and a form-free `InteractsWithActionsTest` in core (the engine works with no wire-forms present).
- **Mobile bottom-sheets — every floating panel and modal becomes a native sheet on small screens, with a configurable breakpoint.** Action-group menus, the generic `<x-wire::dropdown>`, the shared searchable-select, `DateTimePicker`, `Tags`, and the table filter & column-toggle panels now present as a bottom sheet (full-width, pinned to the bottom edge, slide-up transition, dimming backdrop) below a breakpoint instead of a trigger-anchored floating panel; from the breakpoint up the original desktop panel is byte-identical. The class vocabulary is owned once by `WireCore\Foundation\Support\MobileSheet` (breakpoint-parameterised literal Tailwind strings — `panel()`/`motion()`/`grabberShow()`/`backdropHide()`/`px()`) and the opt-in behavior by the `WireCore\Foundation\Concerns\HasSheetOnMobile` trait (`->sheetOnMobile(bool)` + `usesSheetOnMobile()`). Global defaults live in a new `wire-core.mobile` config block — `sheet` (`WIRE_MOBILE_SHEET`, default `true`) and `breakpoint` (`WIRE_MOBILE_BREAKPOINT`, `sm`|`md`|`lg`, default `sm`). Sheets add **safe-area padding** (`env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`), a **drag-to-dismiss grabber** (the `x-sheet-dismiss` Alpine directive: dragging the panel down past a threshold runs its close, mobile-only) and a **focus trap** (the `x-focus-trap` directive: focus moves into the sheet, Tab cycles within it, `aria-modal` is set, focus restores to the trigger on close) — all shipped in the core JS bundle and served through `@assets`. Searchable `Select`/`SelectFilter` default to the classic floating dropdown (the search box stays usable); an explicit `->sheetOnMobile()` still wins. The modal variants align to the same switch: `->slideOverOnMobile()` renders a bottom-sheet and `->fullScreenOnMobile()` fills the viewport below the breakpoint (`ModalComponent`), and the compose `->slideOver()->slideOverOnMobile()` path (`SlideOverComponent`) becomes a full-width sheet too. Covered across `MobileSheetTest`, `DropdownAssetTest`, `ActionGroupTest`, `SelectTest`, `SelectFilterTest`, `ModalComponentRenderTest` and browser-driven verification.
- **Per-component mobile breakpoint — `->mobileBreakpoint('sm'|'md'|'lg')`.** The sheet switch and the modal sheet/full-screen variants gate at the global `wire-core.mobile.breakpoint` by default, and any component can now override it for one instance: `HasSheetOnMobile::mobileBreakpoint()`/`getMobileBreakpoint()` (fields, filters, `ActionGroup`, `Table`), `HasModal::mobileBreakpoint()` for action modals (threaded into `ModalComponent`/`SlideOverComponent` via a `breakpoint` prop, including the width cap so a tablet sheet spans full-width), and `:breakpoint` on `<x-wire::dropdown>`. `MobileSheet` methods take an optional breakpoint override that falls back to config. So a searchable select can stay a sheet up to `lg` (tablet portrait) while the rest of the app switches at `sm`. Covered by `SelectTest`, `ModalComponentRenderTest` and browser verification at 700/900px widths.
- **Per-breakpoint column maps — Filament-style responsive columns.** `columns()` on `CheckboxList` and the schema layouts (`Grid`/`Section`/`Step`/`Fieldset`/`Tab`) now accepts an int (mobile-first reflow, unchanged) **or** a per-breakpoint map keyed by `default`/`sm`/`md`/`lg`/`xl`/`2xl` — `->columns(['default' => 1, 'md' => 2, 'lg' => 3])`. The canonical `WireCore\Foundation\Support\ResponsiveGrid::cols(int|array)` maps it to literal `grid-cols-*` utilities, clamping to 1–12 and ignoring unknown breakpoints; because the runtime interpolates the class name, a `scannableClasses()` allowlist of every emittable utility keeps Tailwind's scanner generating them (Tailwind 3 and 4 compatible). Covered by `ResponsiveGridTest` and `CheckboxListTest`.
- **`Callout` layout — a canonical colored notice box.** A new `WireCore\Foundation\Schema\Callout` (shared by forms and infolists) with `->heading()`/`->title()`, `->color()`/`info()`/`success()`/`warning()`/`danger()`, `->icon()`, `->content()` (or child components) and `->dismissible()`; color hues delegate to the canonical `HasColor::getAlertColorClasses()` palette and the markup lives in the shared `wire-core::partials.callout` view. The forms `Alert` display field is now the field-style alias of the same surface — it renders through that partial, so there is one owner of the notice box. Covered by `CalloutTest` (API, palette, closures, render).
- **`EmptyState` layout — a canonical "nothing here" surface.** A new `WireCore\Foundation\Schema\EmptyState` (`->icon()`/`->heading()`/`->description()`/`->actions([])`) rendered by the shared `wire-core::partials.empty-state` view; the table's own "no records" / filter-empty state now delegates to the same partial (and honors `->emptyState(icon:)`, previously ignored in that markup). Covered by `EmptyStateTest`.
- **`Split` fluent controls — `justify()` / `align()` / `gap()` / `wrap()` / `grow()`.** The side-by-side schema layout gained real flexbox control on top of `from()`: main-axis distribution (`start|end|center|between|around|evenly`), cross-axis alignment (`start|end|center|stretch|baseline`), a Tailwind gap scale (0–12, default 4), `wrap()`, and `grow(false)` to keep children at their natural width instead of the default even growth — all mapped to literal utilities. Covered by `SplitTest`.
- **Standalone `<x-wire::*>` layout tags.** The layout vocabulary is now usable in plain Blade views without a schema array: slot-based `<x-wire::grid>` (int or per-breakpoint `:columns`), `<x-wire::split>` (from/justify/align/gap/wrap/grow, children grow via a `[&>*]:flex-1` variant), `<x-wire::section>`, `<x-wire::fieldset>`, `<x-wire::callout>` and `<x-wire::empty-state>` (reusing the shared partials), plus the Alpine-driven `<x-wire::tabs>`/`<x-wire::wizard>` with self-registering `<x-wire::tab>`/`<x-wire::step>` children (client-side panel/step switching via new `wireTabs`/`wireWizard` data in the core bundle; no per-step validation — use action-modal or form-schema wizards for that). Covered by `LayoutTagsTest` (render) and browser-driven interactivity checks.
- **wire-boost catalogs layout components.** `TypeCatalog` gained a `layouts` category so the MCP tools (`list-component-types`, `describe-component-api`) discover the schema layouts (`Grid`, `Section`, `Split`, `Fieldset`, `Tabs`/`Tab`, `Wizard`/`Step`, `Callout`, `EmptyState`) by kebab short name — a pre-existing gap, since `LayoutComponent` lives in `Foundation/Components` while its concrete types ship in the sibling `Foundation/Schema` directory (the scanner now targets that directory for this category). Covered by `TypeCatalogTest`.
- **`TableWidget` and `CustomWidget`.** Two additions to the widget set alongside the stats/chart widgets. `TableWidget::make()->table(fn (Table $table) => $table->columns([...]))` embeds a full wire-table inside a widget grid (rendered by `wire-core::widgets.table`; the closure is exposed via `getTableCallback()`), and `CustomWidget::make()->view('dashboard.pipeline')->viewData([...])` renders an arbitrary Blade view as a widget tile for one-off content (falling back to `wire-core::widgets.custom`; `getCustomView()`/`getViewData()`). Both extend the base `Widget`, so heading, description, column span and polling apply unchanged. Covered by the widget suite (`WidgetBaseTest`, `WithWidgetsTest`).

### Fixed
- **Action `after()` hooks — and pipeline-driven notifications/redirects — actually run.** The default `ActionPipeline` wired the action **twice**: as a middle `ActionExecutionStage` that ran the callback and returned *without* calling `$next`, and again at the pipeline's terminal. Because the middle stage was terminal, every stage sequenced after it — `AfterCallbacksStage`, `NotificationStage`, `RedirectStage` — was dead code: `->after()` callbacks never fired, and a result's notification/redirect never reached the context (row redirects only worked because a separate `handleActionSuccess()` path re-read the config). Removing the redundant `ActionExecutionStage` from the default stage set (the action already runs once at the terminal — proven by the pipeline's own custom-stage test) restores the intended onion: before-callbacks guard/halt ahead of the action, after-callbacks/notification/redirect post-process the result. That in turn exposed a latent bug in the now-live notification branch — `Notification::make()` was called with no arguments against a `make(string $type, string $message)` signature — now fixed. Finally, the declarative `->successNotification()` / `->failureNotification()` config (whose getters were never called at runtime) is wired: when a run produces no result-notification, the engine falls back to the action's configured success/failure message, resolved with the record and delivered through the component's notification driver (fires only when a message is configured, so silent actions stay silent). Covered by the form-free `InteractsWithActionsTest` (after-run + after-halt, result notification, `successNotification`/`failureNotification` config, result and config redirects) and all four `Core\Actions\Stages` reaching full coverage.
- **Inline radio cards no longer overflow the viewport on a phone.** `Radio::inline()` laid its option cards in a single, always-horizontal track (`grid-flow-col auto-cols-fr`), so three or more cards ran off the edge of a narrow screen with no way to reach them. The inline layout now stacks to one column on mobile and only flows horizontally from `sm` up (`grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-none sm:grid-flow-col sm:auto-cols-fr`); the multi-column `CheckboxList` fallback got the same mobile-first reflow (`grid-cols-2` → `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2`). Covered by `RadioTest` and `CheckboxListTest`.

### Changed
- **One canonical owner for the action runtime.** The action lifecycle engine was extracted out of the ~3.6k-line `WithTable` (~420 lines of payload resolver, `ActionPipeline` bridge, halt-modal handling, form/wizard hosting, footer + infolist actions removed) into a shared, form-agnostic `WireCore\Actions\Concerns\InteractsWithActions` plus a `WireForms\Concerns\InteractsWithActionForms` bridge for the wire-forms parts (Form build/validate, wizard steps, halt form). `WithTable` now `use`s both behind thin record-scoping wrappers and six state seams that back the engine with its `tableState` StateContainer bag (`modal.action.*` / `modal.halt.*`), so table behavior is preserved 1:1 (full table + sortable + integration suites unchanged) while the standalone `WithActions` host backs the same engine with plain public properties — following the repo's "shared behaviour lives once in wire-core" rule.
- **Docs, catalog and boost guidelines track standalone actions and the pipeline change.** The [actions guide](docs/core/actions.md) gains a "Standalone Actions (without a table)" section (`WithActions`, the `mountAction`/`callMountedAction`/`unmountAction`/wizard/footer method table, the `actionModalFormData` binding, and `<x-wire-actions::modal-host />`); the wire-forms boost guideline + `wire-forms-development` skill mirror it, and the wire-core guideline notes actions are no longer table-only. The engine docs are corrected for the pipeline change — `architecture/core.md` and `core/unified-engine.md` no longer describe an `ActionExecutionStage` and explain the terminal-action onion. `AI_COMPONENT_CATALOG.md` and `architecture/forms.md` list the new `InteractsWithActions` / `InteractsWithActionForms` / `WithActions` / `ModalHostComponent`.
- **Docs track the 1.8.0 infolist additions.** The [infolists guide](docs/core/infolists.md) documents the three new entries (`BadgeEntry`/`BooleanEntry`/`ListEntry`) with an API table for `ListEntry`, the `Split` layout, the new **Actions** section (header / entry / per-row actions, name-uniqueness, and the host-composition requirement for dispatch), and the `RepeatableEntry` per-row actions plus the `with()` eager-load note on avoiding N+1 over relation rows; the entry-types table and table of contents were extended to match.
- **Docs and boost guidelines track the mobile and layout additions.** The [foundation guide](docs/core/foundation.md) gains a **Layout Components** section (the shared `Grid`/`Section`/`Fieldset`/`Split`/`Tabs`/`Wizard`/`Callout`/`EmptyState` vocabulary with per-breakpoint columns) and a **Standalone Blade tags** subsection; [modals](docs/core/modals.md) documents the mobile bottom-sheet variants, `->mobileBreakpoint()` and the safe-area/grabber/focus-trap behavior; [configuration](docs/configuration.md) documents the `wire-core.mobile` block (`sheet` + `breakpoint`), the per-component overrides and their priority, plus the `WIRE_MOBILE_*` env vars. [Getting started](docs/getting-started.md) is corrected to scan the package **`src`** as well as `resources/views` (both Tailwind 3 `content` and Tailwind 4 `@source`) — utility classes emitted from PHP (mobile-sheet classes, color/size resolvers, responsive grid columns) are otherwise never generated in a consumer build. The wire-core boost guideline mirrors it: a **Mobile sheets** section (config + per-component overrides) and a **Layouts** section (all layout components, per-breakpoint columns, and the standalone tags).
- **Larger touch targets for icon-only buttons on mobile.** `HasSize::getButtonSizeClasses()` — the single owner of the button-size scale for action buttons, action-group triggers and `ButtonColumn` — now enlarges an icon-only button one step below `sm` (the default `p-1.5` → `p-2.5`, `xs` `p-1` → `p-2`, `lg` `p-2.5` → `p-3`) so a tap clears roughly 40px on a touch screen, snapping back to the compact desktop padding from `sm` up. Text buttons are unchanged.

## [1.7.2]

### Added
- **Standalone `Wizard` — server-driven per-step validation and morph-safe step navigation.** The standalone wizard layout navigated purely client-side and validated everything only on submit, so users clicked through steps with invalid data and a failed submit rendered its errors into hidden panels (an error on step 1 was invisible from step 3). "Next" now validates the current step on the server before advancing: the wizard view calls the new `validateWizardStep(int $stepIndex, ?string $wizard)` Livewire endpoint (`WireForms\Concerns\InteractsWithWizards`, composed by `WithForms` **and** `WithTable`, so a wizard inside a table action-modal form gates too), which validates only that step's fields via `Form::validateWizardStep()` / `FormRuntime::validateWizardStep()` — a step-scoped `FormValidationResolver` over the step's flattened schema and repeaters — clears the step's stale error-bag entries first (`LayoutComponent::getDescendantFieldStatePaths()`, with a `{path}.*` wildcard override on `Repeater`), surfaces failures on the host error bag without aborting the call, and returns pass/fail to the client. Two client behaviors ride on the same render-time sync — a dedicated carrier element whose `x-init="sync(total, errorStep)"` re-runs whenever a Livewire morph changes its expression, kept separate from the root `x-data` (whose attribute text must stay byte-identical across morphs, or Alpine re-initializes the component and resets the active step; caught by driving the workbench preview in headless Chrome): a failed full-form submit **jumps to the first step containing an errored field**, and when a `visible()` condition adds/removes a step mid-form the indicator re-aligns and the active step is clamped to the rendered range — previously the Alpine `step`/`total` seed went stale on morph and navigation desynced from the server-rendered steps. Step indices always follow the *visible* steps (matching `Wizard::getSteps()` re-indexing); multiple wizards are addressed by name (`Wizard::make('signup')`), an unnamed lookup takes the first in the schema; without a Livewire host "Next" falls back to plain client-side navigation. Skippable indicator jumps still bypass validation, like Filament. Covered by `WizardStepValidationTest` (step-scoped errors incl. repeater items, error clearing, visible-step re-indexing, name/out-of-range no-ops, error-step + sync render wiring).

### Fixed
- **The combobox honors `live()` — a selection syncs to the server immediately.** The shared searchable-select always entangled deferred (`$wire.entangle(statePath)` with no modifier), silently ignoring `Select::live()` / `Form::live()`: picking an option only reached the server on the next unrelated roundtrip, so `afterStateUpdated()`, sibling `visibleWhen()` and any dependent reactive behavior lagged one interaction behind (most visibly inside create/action modals). `wire-core::partials.searchable-select` now accepts a `live` flag and entangles with `.live` when set; the forms `Select` and `BelongsToSelect` views pass `isLive()`/`isLiveOnBlur()`, and the table `SelectFilter` combobox passes `live: true` — matching its native `<select>` path, which was already `wire:model.live` (a combobox filter previously didn't apply until another roundtrip). `Toggle` and `DateTimePicker` had the same defect (bare `@entangle` with no modifier) and now append the field's wire-model modifier like every other entangle-based field (slider, tags, rating, …). Covered by `SelectTest` (`.live` modifier renders with `live()`, deferred otherwise).
- **A created/edited select option lands in the combobox without a page refresh.** The combobox seeds its Alpine option map from render-time `@js()` state and never re-reads it on a Livewire morph, so an option created through `createOptionForm()` — although selected server-side — rendered an unreadable trigger (no label) and was missing from the dropdown until a full page reload. `InteractsWithSelectCreation::createSelectOption()` / `updateSelectOption()` now dispatch `select-option-created` / `select-option-updated` browser events carrying `statePath` + `value` + `label`, and every combobox instance listens window-wide, merging the option into its map when the state path matches (`upsertOption`). Selection itself syncs through the existing entangle. Covered by `SelectCreateOptionTest` / `SelectEditOptionTest` (events dispatched with the resolved label).
- **`BelongsToSelect::createOptionForm()` actually works — consolidated onto the canonical Select create-option flow.** The relationship select's create-option path was broken end to end: its "+ Create new" footer called `mountAction('{name}_create_option')`, an action nothing ever registered; the schema was stored on a `BelongsToSelect`-local property the parent's `getCreateOptionForm()` never read; and its view rendered no modal at all. The duplicate property and overrides are gone — the schema lives on the parent `Select` (`createOptionSchema`), so the shared `InteractsWithSelectCreation` modal flow (mount → validate → `createOption()` → select + `select-option-created` event) applies unchanged, with `BelongsToSelect::createOption()` still creating the related model (or delegating to `createOptionUsing()`). The view now renders the canonical `select-option-actions` panel footer (create **and** edit) plus the shared `select-option-modals` partial — extracted from the base select view so both variants have one owner. Covered by `BelongsToSelectTest` (canonical footer wiring, form builds from the schema, footer omitted without a host).
- **`visibleWhen()` / `hidden()` evaluate per repeater item.** The repeater view rendered every item component unconditionally — unlike every other layout view, it never consulted `isVisible()` — so a per-item conditional field (`TextInput::make('other')->visibleWhen('type', 'other')`) showed in all items regardless of each item's state. The item loop now skips invisible components, and `Repeater::getItemSchema()` re-binds the owning Livewire instance to item clones (fields directly, layout clones via `prepareChildren(..., livewire:)`), so per-item reactive closures resolve live state even when the repeater renders outside a full form-level prepare. Combined with the live combobox fix, a `live()` select inside a repeater item toggles its siblings on the spot. Covered by `RepeaterReactivityTest` (field visible only in the matching item, flips on the controlling field's roundtrip).

### Changed
- **`wire-forms::partials.select-option-modals` is the shared owner of the create/edit option modals.** The modal markup was extracted from the base select view into a partial consumed by both `select.blade.php` and `belongs-to-select.blade.php`; the orphaned `wire-forms::partials.belongs-to-create-option` partial (which wired the never-registered `mountAction('{name}_create_option')`) was deleted. If you published/overrode the forms views, re-publish or mirror the split.
- **Docs and boost guidelines track the 1.7.2 changes.** The select guide gains a "Reactivity" section (the combobox binds deferred by default; add `live()` when siblings react via `afterStateUpdated()` / `visibleWhen()` / `Form::live()`) and notes that a created/edited option merges into the open combobox immediately via the `select-option-created` / `select-option-updated` browser events; the belongs-to-select guide documents that inline create shares the canonical Select modal flow (create + `editOptionForm()`, selected and merged without a page refresh); the wizard guide gains a "Per-Step Validation" section (server-gated Next, error-step jump, dynamic step re-sync, named wizards, hostless fallback); the repeater guide gains a "Per-Item Reactivity" section and the reactive-fields guide extends its repeater paragraph with per-item conditional visibility plus a pointer to wizard step validation. The wire-forms boost guideline mirrors all of it: per-step wizard validation, per-item `visibleWhen`, and the no-refresh create/edit-option + live-combobox notes.

## [1.7.1]

### Added
- **`wire-core:audit-prune` artisan command.** Audit retention could only be enforced by hand-rolling a scheduled job around `AuditLogger::prune()`. The bundled command prunes entries older than `wire-core.audit.retention_days` — schedulable with `Schedule::command('wire-core:audit-prune')->daily()` — and takes a `--days=` override for ad-hoc runs; without a configured period (and no `--days`) it warns and exits as invalid instead of silently pruning nothing. `AuditLogger::prune()` accepts an optional `?int $days` parameter that wins over the config. Covered by `AuditPipelineTest`.
- **`ModalFooterAction` confirmation — `requiresConfirmation()` / `confirm()`.** A custom modal footer action can now ask the user to confirm before its callback runs: `ModalFooterAction::make('reset')->requiresConfirmation()` uses the shared translated confirm message (`wire-core::actions.confirm_description`), `->confirm('Really reset?')` sets a custom one. The message is carried through `toArray()` as `confirmMessage` and rendered as a native `wire:confirm` dialog on the footer button in `modal-footer-actions.blade.php` (wizard and non-wizard footers alike). Covered by `ModalFooterActionTest` and `ModalFooterActionsConfirmRenderTest`.

### Fixed
- **`AuditEntry` JSON casts apply on Laravel 10.** The model declared its casts via the `casts()` method, which Laravel only consults from 11 up — on 10.x the `old_values`/`new_values`/`metadata` arrays were never JSON-encoded and every `AuditEntry::save()` failed with an "Array to string conversion" QueryException (latent until the new end-to-end audit test first persisted an entry). The casts are now declared as the `$casts` property, supported across 10–13.
- **Audit events persist out of the box — the package now registers its own subscriber.** `HasAuditable` models fired `RecordCreated`/`RecordUpdated`/`RecordDeleted` events, but nothing listened: the package never registered `AuditEventSubscriber`, so no audit entries were written unless every application added `Event::subscribe(AuditEventSubscriber::class)` to a service provider itself (the docs even prescribed it as a setup step). `WireCoreServiceProvider` now subscribes automatically; `AuditLogger` still gates on `wire-core.audit.enabled`, so nothing persists with auditing off. The subscription is idempotent (it skips when an `AuditableEvent` listener already exists), so applications that keep their manual `Event::subscribe` from the old setup do not double-log — the manual line can simply be removed. Covered by `AuditPipelineTest` (persists with zero app wiring, no duplicates with a manual subscribe, respects the enabled switch) and `AuditEventSubscriberTest`.
- **`Select::createOptionForm()` / `editOptionForm()` work inside table action modals.** The "+ Create" / "Edit" panel-footer buttons rendered in a table action modal form (the guard was only `$livewire !== null`), but `WithTable` did not compose `InteractsWithSelectCreation`, so clicking them failed with a missing `mountCreateOption()` wire method. `WithTable` now composes the trait (like `WithForms`), and `selectCreatedOption()` writes the new value through `StateContainer::writeInto()` instead of a plain `data_set()` — the table modal form binds into the `tableState.modal.action.formData` StateContainer bag, where `data_set()` silently drops the write. Covered by `SelectCreateOptionTableTest` (create + select into the StateContainer bag, multi-select append, validation keeps the modal open).
